# ASE-L UX audit — all 126 findings, as reported by the twelve lenses

Raw output of the audit fan-out (2026-09-09). NOT yet triaged; the lead verified
the load-bearing criticals separately — see MEASUREMENTS.md and REPORT.md.


---

# typography  (10 findings)

**Headline.** ASE-L hardcodes two font families that are not installed on this machine, so the flagship window renders in Nimbus Sans/Nimbus Mono PS while its own menubar's siblings, the CIW, the RDW and the schematic all render in DejaVu — and it does so with the ladder upside down (10pt bold headings over 13pt regular data), everything bold, no user control, and cells that clip silently at the default window size.

**The one slick move.** Rewrite the eight lines of `ase::theme` (src/ase_window.tcl:184-192) so the named fonts are DERIVED, in pixels, from Tk's own TkDefaultFont/TkFixedFont, at one size separated by weight instead of two sizes separated backwards — `set fam [font actual TkDefaultFont -family]`, `set base [expr {-([font metrics TkDefaultFont -linespace] - 3)}]`, then AseEntryFont/AseBodyFont normal, AseLabelFont bold, AseMonoFont on `font actual TkFixedFont -family`. It is one proc, and it is the one because it is the only edit here that fixes six findings at once and moves zero tests: the window stops rendering in an uninstalled Arial-substitute and joins the DejaVu the menubar, the CIW, the RDW and the schematic already use; the ☑/☐ checkbox glyphs stop falling back to a second typeface mid-row; the heading/data ladder turns right way up; the panes reclaim ~22% of their width so `agauss(1.8, 'ABSVAR', 1)` stops clipping at the default size; the log window's 84 columns cost 672px instead of 924px; and the pixel spelling makes the whole window track its own menubar at every tk scaling instead of diverging at 11/15/21 against a flat 17. Every font assertion in tests/headless is against the font NAME, and no ASE suite asserts a pixel geometry — measured — so the resolution of all three fonts can change without a single check moving. Everything else in this report is either additive (tooltips, an hscrollbar, the aA control) or waits on a user ruling; this one is pure repair, and it is what the user is looking at when they say the fonts could stand to improve.

## [CRITICAL] A design variable's value is clipped at the DEFAULT window size, with no ellipsis and no tooltip

- **id** `panes-clip-silently-no-ellipsis-no-tip`  ·  **effort** medium
- **Evidence.** Measured live (ase_main.png, 798x502, real bench sky130_tests_ase/tb_bandgap/ngspice_state1): Design Variables row VCCGAUSS, value `agauss(1.8, 'ABSVAR', 1)` measures 204px in AseEntryFont; the `value` column is 120px wide (`$tv column value -width` = 120, minus ~8px padding = 112px available) => CLIPPED, and the screenshot shows exactly `agauss(1.8, 'A` cut mid-token. Tk's ttk::treeview clips with no ellipsis. `bind $top.body.vars.tv` returns only `<Button-3> <Double-Button-1> <Button-1> <<TreeviewSelect>>` — there is NO <Motion>/<Enter> tooltip on any of the three panes. ase_small.png (560x360) shows the same failure across the board: `VCCGAUS`, `TEMPERA`, headings collapsed to `Typ`/`Ena`/`Plc`/`Sa`/`Save Op`, and `wm minsize $top` is `1 1` so nothing stops it. simulators.png: the Program column shows `/home/analog/dev/ngspice/build-ver_` — the path is 410px in AseEntryFont against a ~305px column. loadstate.png: `ngspice_verilog_cos`, `test_carry_lookahea`. The mechanism to fix this ALREADY EXISTS in the same file: ase::ui::rsel_tip / rsel_tip_show / rsel_tip_text (src/ase_window.tcl:3729-3760) drive `balloon_show` off <Motion> to show a full path per row, built for Results > Select; and src/xschem.tcl:14935 `balloon_clipped` shows a tip only when the text is actually clipped.
- **Why it hurts.** The value of a design variable is the single most-read number in ADE-L. A designer who sets VCCGAUSS to an agauss() expression cannot read back what they set without opening the row editor — for a value they can see three quarters of. It fails at the shipped default geometry, on the first bench opened, with no widening that helps (the pane is 140+120 of a 798px window; the Analyses Arguments column is next). Every wrong-value debug session starts by not being able to see the value.
- **Fix.** Two parts, both precedented in-tree. (1) Per-cell tooltip: bind <Motion> on each of the three treeviews to a handler modelled verbatim on ase::ui::rsel_tip (src/ase_window.tcl:3737) — `$W identify row/column $x $y`, resolve the cell's full text, `after 700 balloon_show`. Gate it on actual clipping the way balloon_clipped does: show only when `font measure AseEntryFont $cell > [$tv column $c -width] - 8`. (2) Visible ellipsis: keep the full string in a parallel dict keyed by item+column and set the DISPLAYED value to a pixel-truncated prefix + …, recomputed on <Configure> and on column drag. Same treatment for the Simulators dialog Program column and the three Load State listboxes. (3) `wm minsize $top` derived from font metrics rather than 1 1 — e.g. the summed reqwidth of the status bar (measured 531px) plus the strip, so the window cannot be shrunk below legibility.
- **Risk.** The tooltip half is additive and touches no widget path or label — no suite moves. The ellipsis half changes what `$tv item ... -values` reads back, and tests/headless/test_ase_window.tcl W1p asserts seeded pane rows by value, so the full text must stay in the item's -values and only the DISPLAY be truncated (use a display column or a tag), or W1p reds. `wm minsize` is user-visible behaviour; if the floor is ever felt as "the window won't get small", that is a ruling.
- **ADE-L.** ADE-L's Design Variables / Outputs tables let the value cell be read and edited in place, and its columns are drag-resizable; a value you set is never unreadable at the default window size.

## [CRITICAL] The two hardcoded families are not installed; Tk substitutes Nimbus Sans / Nimbus Mono PS, and the ONE glyph that carries state falls back to a third face

- **id** `arial-courier-not-installed`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** src/ase_window.tcl:185,188,191 — `font create AseLabelFont -family Arial -size 10 -weight bold`, `AseEntryFont -family Arial -size 13`, `AseMonoFont -family Courier -size 13`. Measured live on :99: `font families` contains no Arial, no Courier, no Helvetica, no Liberation, no FreeSans. Resolution is AseLabelFont/AseEntryFont -> Nimbus Sans (a Helvetica clone), AseMonoFont -> Nimbus Mono PS (a Courier clone, the thinnest and lowest-contrast mono on the box). Every other window in the same process resolves to DejaVu: TkDefaultFont/TkTextFont/TkMenuFont/TkHeadingFont = DejaVu Sans 10, TkFixedFont = DejaVu Sans Mono 10, CiwFont = DejaVu Sans Mono 12, RdwPaneFont/RdwHdrFont = DejaVu Sans Mono 10, slickPropLabel/Value/Header/Hint = DejaVu Sans / DejaVu Sans Mono 11-12. Per-character fallback, measured with `font actual F -displayof . -family -- <char>`: U+2611 ☑ and U+2610 ☐ — the checkbox glyphs that ARE the Enable/Plot/Save controls (ase_main.png, ase_large.png) — are NOT in Nimbus Sans and fall back to DejaVu Sans. So in the Outputs pane the row label `VBG` is Nimbus Sans and the checkbox beside it is DejaVu Sans, at 16px against DejaVu's own 12px. Cost in width: `VCCGAUSS` = 101px in AseEntryFont vs 79px in TkDefaultFont; the ngspice path = 410px vs 370px; the whole app pays ~22% extra width for a face nothing else uses.
- **Why it hurts.** This is the user's headline complaint made concrete. Nimbus Sans at 13px and Nimbus Mono PS at 13px are Adobe print clones with weak screen hinting; against DejaVu — which the menubar, the CIW, the RDW and every schematic dialog use — they read thinner, wider and blurrier, and the difference is visible in one glance between the ASE window and the CIW next to it. The fallback means the pane's only interactive glyph is drawn by a different typeface from its own label. And on a box with no Nimbus either (a stripped container, another distro) Arial/Courier resolve to whatever fontconfig's last-resort is, which is unbounded.
- **Fix.** Delete the family literals. Derive from Tk's own defaults, which fontconfig has already resolved and which the rest of xschem renders with — the idiom is already in the tree at src/property_form.tcl:340-343 (`slickprop::_mkfont`) and src/rdw.tcl:2594-2615 (`rdw::_font`, which copies `font configure TkFixedFont` verbatim). Concretely in ase::theme: `set fam [font actual TkDefaultFont -family]` and `set mfam [font actual TkFixedFont -family]`, then create the named fonts with those. No fallback chain to hardcode — DejaVu here, Ubuntu Sans / Noto Sans Mono / Nimbus wherever fontconfig points, always the same face the menubar uses. ☑/☐ then need no fallback at all, because DejaVu Sans has them.
- **Risk.** Very low, and MEASURED: every ASE font assertion in tests/headless is against the font NAME, never against family, size or weight — test_ase_window.tcl:548-550 (the three names exist), :554-556 (`[$top.tb.temp cget -font] eq {AseEntryFont}`), test_calc_skeleton.tcl:145,408 (name present/absent), test_results_dialog.tcl:791, test_wave_sigsearch.tcl:1450-1452, test_wave_sigbrowser_sea.tcl:677-682 (the rowheight expression, which recomputes from the font and self-adjusts). No ASE suite asserts a pixel geometry (grep for reqwidth/winfo width across test_ase_window.tcl, test_ase_dirty.tcl, test_results_dialog.tcl finds only `.drw` size guards). So the resolution of all three fonts can change without moving a single assertion.
- **ADE-L.** ADE-L renders in the platform/CDS font throughout and does not name a font family that the workstation may not have.

## [MAJOR] apply_theme paints EVERY label, button, checkbutton and menu bold — including 248 characters of running prose and the whole menubar

- **id** `bold-everything-including-prose-and-menubar`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** src/ase_window.tcl:227-229 — the switch arm `Toplevel - Frame - Labelframe - Menu - Button - Label - Checkbutton` sets `-font AseLabelFont`, and AseLabelFont is `-weight bold` (:185). Measured live on the real bench: the ASE menubar `.ase4.mb cget -font` = AseLabelFont -> Nimbus Sans 10 BOLD, and so are all nine cascades; the stock xschem menubar in the SAME PROCESS, `.menubar cget -font`, = TkMenuFont -> DejaVu Sans 10 regular. Every status-bar segment (`Status: Ready`, `T=27 C`, `Simulator: ngspice-v50`, `State: ngspice_state1`), every strip button caption and every labelframe title is bold. Worst case measured in the Simulators dialog (simulators.png, src/ase_window.tcl:4558,4562,4565): `$w.status` carries 147 characters and `$w.where` 101 characters of explanatory prose, both `font=AseLabelFont weight=bold` — 248 characters of bold body copy wrapped across four lines, plainly visible in the screenshot. Those two labels also carry a hardcoded `-wraplength 560` (and :5034 a `-wraplength 420`), a pixel constant that assumes the current font.
- **Why it hurts.** Bold is the only emphasis tool a Tk window has, and ASE-L has spent it on everything, so nothing can be emphasised. The practical loss is the status bar and the Simulators dialog: `Status: Running` cannot be made to stand out from `T=27 C` because both are already maximally loud, and the sentence explaining which simulator will run reads as a warning rather than as information. A bold menubar is also the single fastest way to tell, at a glance across a desktop, that this window was not built by the same people as the one beside it — and the schematic's menubar is right there in ase_root.png.
- **Fix.** Split the switch arm. Add a fourth named font `AseBodyFont` — same family and size as AseEntryFont, `-weight normal` — and route `Menu`, `Button`, `Checkbutton` and `Label` to it; keep AseLabelFont (bold) for `Labelframe` titles and for `Ase.Treeview.Heading` only, i.e. for things that are genuinely headings. For the menubar specifically, the cheapest correct answer is to stop themeing it at all: drop `Menu` from the arm so it inherits TkMenuFont and matches `.menubar` exactly. Replace the `-wraplength 560/420` literals with a value derived from the dialog's own width or from `font measure`.
- **Risk.** A new name in `font names` — test_ase_window.tcl:548-550 only checks the three existing names are PRESENT, and test_calc_skeleton.tcl:408 checks AseEntryFont is ABSENT before ase::theme, so a fourth name breaks neither. Dropping `Menu` from the arm changes what `.aseN.mb cget -font` answers; grep shows no suite asserts it. The visual change (menus and prose no longer bold) is user-visible and worth showing the user before it lands — a look debt, not a ruling, since no sentence changes.
- **ADE-L.** ADE-L's menu bar and its explanatory text are regular weight; bold is reserved for column headings and for state that has changed.

## [MAJOR] The size ladder is inverted: 10pt bold headings sit over 13pt regular data

- **id** `heading-smaller-than-the-data-it-heads`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** src/ase_window.tcl:185 (AseLabelFont size 10) vs :188 (AseEntryFont size 13); :205 sets `Ase.Treeview -font AseEntryFont` and :214 sets `Ase.Treeview.Heading -font AseLabelFont`. Measured linespace: AseLabelFont 15px, AseEntryFont 19px — headings are 4pt and 4px smaller than their own rows. ase_large.png (1500x900) shows it unmistakably: `Name`, `Value`, `#`, `Type`, `Enable`, `Arguments`, `Save Options` and the dark-red labelframe titles `Design Variables` / `Analyses` / `Outputs` are visibly smaller than `VCCGAUSS`, `agauss(1.8, 'ABSVAR', 1)` and `TEMPERAT` beneath them; the status bar at the bottom is smaller still. Measured column headings vs the rows they head: `Save Options` heading = 89px in AseLabelFont in a 90px column (one pixel of slack), while a row cell in the same column would be laid out at AseEntryFont's 22%-wider metrics.
- **Why it hurts.** Structure should be findable before content. With the ladder inverted the eye lands on the data first and has to hunt for which column it is in — exactly wrong for a table you scan rather than read. It also wastes the scarcest resource in the window: the 13pt data font is what makes `agauss(1.8, 'ABSVAR', 1)` clip in a 120px column, and the 10pt headings are what makes `Save Options` need 89 of its 90 pixels. Both problems are the same mistake pointing in opposite directions.
- **Fix.** One size for the whole window, distinguished by WEIGHT, not by size — the standard fix and what the sibling systems already do (src/property_form.tcl:335-338 runs label/value at the same size and separates the header by +1 and bold; src/rdw.tcl:2603 makes the header font bold at the pane's own size). Concretely: AseEntryFont, AseLabelFont and AseBodyFont all at the derived base size; AseLabelFont bold. Measured payoff at DejaVu Sans 10: `VCCGAUSS` 101px -> 79px, `agauss(1.8, 'ABSVAR', 1)` 204px -> 174px, the ngspice path 410px -> 370px — the same columns carry roughly 11 more characters each and the Program column in simulators.png goes from 36 to 40 characters of the path.
- **Risk.** Lands as part of the ase::theme rewrite; same zero-assertion-movement analysis as the family fix. `Ase.Treeview -rowheight [expr {[font metrics AseEntryFont -linespace] + 4}]` (:208) recomputes automatically and test_wave_sigbrowser_sea.tcl:682 recomputes the expected value from the same expression, so the row height follows. If the user wants the data to stay visually louder than the chrome, that is a ruling — offer 'one size, weight-separated' as the default and the font knob (next finding) as the escape hatch.
- **ADE-L.** ADE-L's setup tables render headings and cells at the same size, headings bold.

## [MAJOR] ASE-L is the only Tcl window in the application with no font-size control — and the user personally asked for one in the RDW

- **id** `no-font-size-control-when-every-sibling-has-one`  ·  **effort** medium
- **Evidence.** `grep -c font src/ase.tcl` = 0: the non-GUI half has no font concept at all, and there is no `ase_font_size` anywhere. Compare, in the same tree: src/ciw.tcl:391-413 `ciw_font` / `ciw_set_font_size` (order-independent, refuses out-of-band values rather than clamping); src/rdw.tcl:2276-2386 the whole `font_limits`/`_accept_size`/`font_size`/`set_font_size`/`font_step`/`_font_tip` model plus src/rdw.tcl:3225 `::button .rdw.b.fontsize -text {aA} -width 8 -command {rdw::font_step 1}` with a Ctrl-click decrement and a 300ms balloon; src/property_form.tcl:325-333 `::slickprop_fontsize`. The rc knobs exist beside each other in src/xschem.tcl:19441 `set_ne ciw_font_size 10` and :19454 `set_ne rdw_font_size 0` — with no ASE entry between them. src/rdw.tcl:2242-2245 records the user's own words verbatim: "add a button to allow user to manipulate font size in RDW. it can be the 'aa' button you see in e-readers - 2nd a bigger. Key part, as soon as user hovers over it, tooltip should be displayed: click to increase font one unit. Ctrl+click to decrease font one unit".
- **Why it hurts.** The user has already ruled that a text pane must be resizable by the person reading it, and got it — in the RDW, a secondary window. The bench they sit in front of all day has no such control, so an ASE-L that is too small on a 27-inch 4K panel or too large on a laptop cannot be fixed at all: there is no menu entry, no button, no rc variable, no `xschem` subcommand. It is also the only defence against every other finding here being wrong for a particular display.
- **Fix.** Mirror ciw_set_font_size exactly, one layer up: `set_ne ase_font_size 0` in src/xschem.tcl beside its two siblings (0 = 'follow TkDefaultFont'); `ase::ui::font_size` / `ase::ui::set_font_size n` in ase_window.tcl, order-independent (record the value AND `font configure` the named fonts if they already exist), refusing out-of-band rather than clamping, band 6..32 like rdw::font_limits; every named font created as base+delta so one setter moves all four coherently. Surface it as the `aA` button — the user has already specified its behaviour and its tooltip, so no new sentence is invented: reuse `[rdw::_font_tip]`'s exact string. Placement is the open question: the action strip is already eight buttons deep, so the right-hand end of the status bar or a `Setup > Font Size` entry.
- **Risk.** Additive; nothing moves. Two decisions belong to the user and must be recorded as `owed.sh add rule` before landing, not chosen here: WHERE the aA control lives (status bar vs Setup menu vs a ninth strip button), and whether ASE-L's size should follow the CIW's/RDW's or be independent. The 300ms balloon delay inherits rule debt 1368, already open.

## [MAJOR] ASE-L's text tracks tk scaling; Tk's default fonts — which this window's siblings use — do not, so the two diverge with the display

- **id** `point-sizes-drift-from-the-window-s-own-menubar-with-dpi`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** Measured live on :99 (tk scaling 1.3879, ~100dpi), sweeping `tk scaling` and reading `font metrics -linespace`:  scaling 1.0 -> AseLabelFont 11px, AseEntryFont 14px, TkDefaultFont 17px;  scaling 1.3879 -> 15 / 19 / 17;  scaling 2.0 -> 21 / 27 / 17. TkDefaultFont is flat at 17px at every scaling. Control experiment in the same run: a font created fresh at each scaling as `-family {DejaVu Sans} -size 10` gives 13 / 17 / 24 — so it is not a family effect and not a caching artefact; app-created point-sized fonts track scaling and Tk's own defaults are pinned. Second control: `-family {DejaVu Sans} -size -13` (a PIXEL spelling) gives 17 / 17 / 17, i.e. it tracks TkDefaultFont exactly. And the `font actual`-copy idiom used by src/property_form.tcl:342 does NOT rescue it — `font actual` normalises the pixel spelling back to points, so a font built that way measures 13 / 17 / 24 and drifts too. `$DISPLAY` on this machine is a Windows X server over TCP (CLAUDE.md's three-server table), whose reported physical size — and therefore whose tk scaling — is not 1.3879 and is not measured here.
- **Why it hurts.** There is no display at which the window is internally consistent by construction; :99 happens to land at 15 vs 17 and looks nearly right, which is exactly why this has never been noticed. On the user's own server the ASE panes and the ASE menubar are two different sizes by an unknown amount, and on any HiDPI panel the panes swell to 21/27px while the menubar stays at 17 — the window visibly comes apart as the display changes. It also means every pixel constant in the file (`-wraplength 560`, `-wraplength 420`, `-width 44`, `-width 26`, treeview `-width 140/120/262`) is calibrated for one scaling only.
- **Fix.** Size the named fonts in PIXELS off Tk's own base, not in points: `set px [font actual TkDefaultFont -size]` gives points, so convert once — `set base [expr {int(round(-$px * [tk scaling]))}]` (-> -14 here) — or, simpler and exactly measured above, derive from the base's linespace: `set base [expr {-([font metrics TkDefaultFont -linespace] - 3)}]`. Create all four ASE fonts with that negative size. Verified: -13 tracked TkDefaultFont at 17/17/17 across the whole sweep. Add the user delta from the previous finding on top of the pixel value, so a step is one pixel and is display-independent. Worth reporting the same defect to property_form.tcl's four fonts, which share it.
- **Risk.** Same edit as the family fix, same zero test movement. One thing to check by eye rather than by assertion: at the CURRENT :99 scaling the change moves AseLabelFont from 15px to ~17px linespace and AseEntryFont from 19px to ~17px, so every ASE window and dialog changes size slightly — a look debt, and the right moment to hand the user a before/after on their own display, since :99's scaling is not theirs.

## [MAJOR] The log window is 84 columns of the widest mono on the box, with -wrap none and no horizontal scrollbar — the line naming the command that ran is unreachable

- **id** `log-window-mono-too-wide-and-unreachable-lines`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** src/ase_window.tcl:7010-7014: `text $lw.t -height 24 -width 84 -state disabled -wrap none -yscrollcommand ...` and only `scrollbar $lw.sb -orient vertical`. Measured live: `$lw.t cget -xscrollcommand` is empty, `winfo children $lw` is `{.t .sb}` — there is no horizontal scrollbar and no wrapping. The text is AseMonoFont = Nimbus Mono PS 13 at exactly 11.0px per character, so 84 columns = 924px (logwin.png is 941x460, matching). Reading the session's real ngspice log (/home/analog/.xschem/simulations/tb_bandgap_ase.log, 434 lines): the longest line is 144 characters = 1584px, against 928px of text widget — and that line is `command   : /home/analog/dev/ngspice/build-ver_50/src/ngspice -b -D casemode=preserve /home/analog/.xschem/simulations/tb...`, i.e. the exact invocation. 2 of 434 lines exceed the window. Same 84 columns in TkFixedFont (DejaVu Sans Mono 10, 8.0px/char) would be 672px.
- **Why it hurts.** When a run fails, the first question is always 'what did it actually run with?' — and that answer is off the right edge of a window that offers no way to get there: no wrap, no hscroll, no select-and-scroll (the widget is `-state disabled`). The user's recourse is to leave ASE-L and cat the log in a terminal, which is the failure mode ASE-L exists to prevent. Separately, Nimbus Mono PS at 13 is 37% wider per character than the mono the CIW and RDW use, so the log window is a third larger than it needs to be for the same 84 columns and still shows less.
- **Fix.** Add `ttk::scrollbar $lw.hsb -orient horizontal -command [list $lw.t xview]` and `-xscrollcommand [list $lw.hsb set]` on the text, gridded (not packed — the current side-packing cannot take a bottom bar cleanly). Keep `-wrap none`: for simulator output, wrapping destroys column alignment. With AseMonoFont derived from TkFixedFont (DejaVu Sans Mono) the same 84 columns cost 672px instead of 924px, so the default window can hold ~115 columns in the pixels it already occupies — set `-width` from the font rather than leaving the literal 84.
- **Risk.** Adds a widget path `$top.logwin.hsb`; test_ase_window.tcl asserts the log TOPLEVEL exists and its Ctrl-W close, not its children — grep finds no assertion on `.logwin` children. Repacking .t/.sb as a grid is the only structural change; keep the widget names `.t` and `.sb` so anything reading them still works.

## [MINOR] The same simulator output is rendered in three different monospace faces depending on which window shows it

- **id** `three-monospace-faces-for-one-kind-of-text`  ·  **effort** trivial
- **Evidence.** Measured live, one process, side by side: AseMonoFont = Nimbus Mono PS 13, 11.0px/char, linespace 19 (the ASE log window). CiwFont = DejaVu Sans Mono 12, 10.0px/char, linespace 20 (src/ciw.tcl:391-398, the Command window that echoes the same run). RdwPaneFont/RdwHdrFont = DejaVu Sans Mono 10, 8.0px/char, linespace 17 (src/rdw.tcl:2594-2615, derived from TkFixedFont). TkFixedFont itself = DejaVu Sans Mono 10. slickPropValue = DejaVu Sans Mono 11. Five monospace configurations, three of them showing simulator or netlist text.
- **Why it hurts.** ase_root.png shows the ASE window, the waveform viewer and the schematic on one desktop; add the CIW and the RDW and the same ngspice line appears at three sizes in three faces. It reads as three applications rather than one bench, which is precisely the 'not slick' impression the user is describing.
- **Fix.** Falls out of the family fix: AseMonoFont derived from `font actual TkFixedFont -family` puts the ASE log on DejaVu Sans Mono, the same face as the RDW and (modulo size) the CIW. If a single shared knob is wanted later, the three setters (ciw_set_font_size, rdw::set_font_size, the proposed ase::ui::set_font_size) already have identical shapes and could take a common base.
- **Risk.** None beyond the ase::theme rewrite it is part of. Whether the three windows should share ONE size or keep independent ones is a user question, not one to settle here.

## [MINOR] Opening one ASE window permanently changes the popdown font of every ttk::combobox in xschem

- **id** `combobox-font-leaks-into-the-host-application`  ·  **effort** trivial
- **Evidence.** src/ase_window.tcl:193 — `option add *TCombobox*Listbox.font AseEntryFont`, an unqualified pattern against the global option database, executed by ase::theme. Measured live: a ttk::combobox created in a fresh toplevel BEFORE ase::theme has a popdown listbox font of TkTextFont -> DejaVu Sans 10; the identical combobox created AFTER ase::theme has AseEntryFont -> Nimbus Sans 13. tests/headless/test_calc_skeleton.tcl:124-125 already records the side effect in a comment ("permanent — it creates AseLabelFont/AseEntryFont/AseMonoFont and does a global `option add *TCombobox*Listbox.font AseEntryFont`") without asserting it. src/xschem.tcl:11301 creates a ttk::combobox of font families in the stock text-properties dialog, which is one of the affected widgets.
- **Why it hurts.** A stock xschem dialog silently changes typeface halfway through a session, depending on whether the user has opened a simulation bench yet. It is small, but it is the kind of inconsistency that makes an application feel unmaintained — and it makes any measurement of 'what does xschem look like' depend on the order the user opened windows in.
- **Fix.** Narrow the pattern to ASE's own widgets. The popdown is a child of the combobox, so its path contains the ASE toplevel: `option add *ase*TCombobox*Listbox.font AseEntryFont` scopes it to `.aseN...`. Cleaner still, set it per-widget: after creating each ASE combobox, `[ttk::combobox::PopdownWindow $cb].f.l configure -font AseEntryFont` (it forces the lazy popdown to build, which src/ase_window.tcl:143-146 already documents as the reason the option-db route was chosen — so the narrowed glob is the smaller change).
- **Risk.** No suite asserts the global behaviour — test_calc_skeleton only mentions it in prose and checks font-name presence at :145/:408. Verify the narrowed glob still reaches the popdown by measuring, since the popdown's window path is Tk-version-dependent.

## [POLISH] The one place ASE-L does truncate uses a character count, in a proportional font

- **id** `character-count-ellipsis-in-a-proportional-font`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** `ase::ui::output_display_name` truncates an unnamed output expression at 21 characters plus "...", asserted at tests/headless/test_ase_window.tcl:504-508 (`P2 unnamed long expr truncated to 21+...`, `P2 truncated form is exactly 24 chars`). The font it renders in is AseEntryFont, proportional: measured, 21 characters of `WWWWWWWWWWWWWWWWWWWWW` and 21 characters of `iiiiiiiiiiiiiiiiiiiii` differ by more than 3x in pixels. Meanwhile the three panes that DO clip (previous findings) truncate by pixel — silently, by the widget — and show no ellipsis at all.
- **Why it hurts.** Minor in itself, but it is the tell: the file has one truncation policy expressed in characters and another expressed in pixels, and neither produces a visible ellipsis where the user actually loses text. Fixing the panes without fixing this leaves two different-looking truncations in the same pane.
- **Fix.** Once a pixel-based ellipsis helper exists for the panes, route output_display_name through it: truncate to a pixel budget derived from the column width and append … (U+2026, one glyph, present in DejaVu Sans). Keep a character-count fallback for the headless arm, which has no `font` command at all (src/rdw.tcl:2308-2311 records that measurement).
- **Risk.** test_ase_window.tcl:504-508 asserts the 21+... form and the exact 24-character length; both move. That is two check lines in one suite and the change is deliberate, but the suite must be updated in the same commit or W-phase reds.


---

# colour-contrast  (10 findings)

**Headline.** ASE-L's entire palette lives in the top 11% of the luminance range — pane ground vs panel is 1.12:1, heading strip vs panel is 1.09:1, and the hover highlight is 1.05:1 — so nothing separates from anything else, and the one saturated colour it owns (the maroon accent) is only 2.10:1 from the black body text it is supposed to outrank.

**The one slick move.** Give `ase::ui::apply_theme` an active state, derived rather than written down. Today the Button/Menu arm (src/ase_window.tcl:227-233) sets `-background [ase::theme panel]` and leaves `-activebackground` at Tk's stock `#f8f8f8`. Measured live: before theming the hover step was #f8f8f8 on grey80 (luminance delta 0.335); after theming it is #f8f8f8 on #f2f2f2, delta 0.051 — 1.05:1, a 6.6x smaller step than stock Tk had. Theming ASE-L actively destroyed the only feedback its eight-glyph action strip has. The fix needs no user ruling on a colour, because the tree already ratified the derivation: `rdw::_shade_step` (src/rdw.tcl:2498-2515) returns "one visible step off this ground, +-40 of 255, direction flipped on a dark ground", written for exactly this problem and locked by test_rdw_window_1245.tcl section CU3. Hoist it to `ase::shade`, and in apply_theme's Button/Menu/Checkbutton arm add `-activebackground [ase::shade [ase::theme panel]]` (= #cacaca, 1.87:1 against the panel) plus `-activeforeground [ase::theme fieldfg]`. Four lines, no new palette entry, no stub breakage, still correct if the palette ever goes dark — and every button, every menu entry and all eight cryptic strip glyphs light up under the pointer. That single change is the difference between a window that responds and a window that looks like a screenshot of itself. The close second is odd-row banding (finding no-row-banding); it needs a ruling on the tone, so it cannot be the no-ruling one.

## [MAJOR] Hover and menu highlight are 1.05:1 — theming ASE-L destroyed the feedback stock Tk had

- **id** `active-state-invisible`  ·  **effort** trivial
- **Evidence.** Probed live on :99 after `ase::ui::apply_theme`: menu `-activebackground` = #f8f8f8, `-background` = #f2f2f2; button `-activebackground` = #f8f8f8, `-background` = #f2f2f2. Computed WCAG contrast 1.05:1 (luminance 0.9387 vs 0.8879, delta 0.051). Same probe BEFORE apply_theme: #f8f8f8 on grey80 (#cccccc), luminance delta 0.335 — a 6.6x larger step. src/ase_window.tcl:227-233 sets `-background` and `-font` on Toplevel/Frame/Labelframe/Menu/Button/Label/Checkbutton and never touches `-activebackground` or `-activeforeground`. Live widget walk on the real bench window (.ase4, sky130_tests_ase/tb_bandgap): 8 Buttons — the whole action strip — and 31 Menu entries, all affected. menu_sim.png shows the Simulation menu: six entries, no visible highlight band.
- **Why it hurts.** The action strip is eight buttons captioned OP,TR / = / --> / X / N&> / > / ! / ~ (ase_main.png; src/ase_window.tcl:756 calls them "text placeholders"). With no hover feedback the user cannot tell which cryptic glyph the pointer is on before committing to a click, and a mis-click on that strip starts a run or deletes a row. Every menu drag has the same problem: the entry you are about to release on is 6 levels of grey from the ones you are not. The window reads as dead — nothing on screen ever acknowledges the pointer.
- **Fix.** In apply_theme's `Toplevel - Frame - Labelframe - Menu - Button - Label - Checkbutton` arm add, under the same `catch`, `-activebackground [ase::shade [ase::theme panel]]` and `-activeforeground [ase::theme fieldfg]`. `ase::shade` = `rdw::_shade_step` (src/rdw.tcl:2498-2515) hoisted into the ase namespace: +-40 of 255, direction flipped on a dark ground. On the shipped palette that is #cacaca, 1.87:1 against the panel and 11.6:1 under black text. Derived, so no new palette key and no user ruling on a colour value.
- **Risk.** No suite asserts an active colour anywhere in tests/headless (grepped: `-activebackground` appears in no ASE suite). Adding a palette KEY would break test_calc_widgets.tcl:1212-1216 and 1244-1248 and test_rdw_window_1245.tcl:4570-4575, which re-`proc ase::palette` with a hand-written 8-9 key dict and would throw on `dict get` — this fix deliberately adds no key, so it avoids that. No ratification needed: the +-40 step is already the user-facing number locked by test_rdw_window_1245.tcl section CU3.
- **ADE-L.** ADE-L's toolbar and menus give a normal platform highlight on hover; you always know which of its icon-strip buttons you are about to press.

## [MAJOR] 323 px of unbroken white between a row's name and its Save checkbox, with zero row banding

- **id** `no-row-banding`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** Measured pixel runs on ase_large.png (the 1500x900 window), Outputs pane, row y=591 ("TEMPERAT"): name ink ends at x=620; the Plot checkbox occupies x=943-957; the Save checkbox x=1088-1102. That is 323 px and 468 px of unbroken #ffffff. Row pitch is 23 px (probed: `ttk::style lookup Ase.Treeview -rowheight` = 23). `ase::theme` (src/ase_window.tcl:204-216) configures Ase.Treeview with a single flat `-background [ase::palette table]` and declares no alternating tag; the fill loops set only `cur`/`inreg`/`plain` (src/ase_window.tcl:3653, 3662) and the pane builder (:843) sets none at all.
- **Why it hurts.** Ticking the wrong row's Save or Plot box silently changes what the netlist saves and therefore what gets measured — a wrong answer with no error message. At the natural 798 px width (ase_main.png) the gap is already ~180 px across five rows; at any wide window it is a horizontal eye-trace across a third of the screen with nothing to hold the line. This is the single colour omission in ASE-L that can produce a wrong result rather than merely an ugly one.
- **Fix.** Add one `band` tone and stripe the odd rows: `$tv tag configure band -background <tone>` in `ase::ui::build_pane` and in `rsel_build_list`, then append `band` to the `-tags` list on odd indices in the three pane fill loops and in `rsel_fill` (src/ase_window.tcl:3653, 3662). Keep `cur`/`inreg`/`plain` as the foreground tags so the two axes do not collide (ttk applies the last matching tag's value per option, so a `-background`-only band and a `-foreground`-only state tag compose cleanly).
- **Risk.** THE TONE IS A USER RULING. The palette has nine colours and none of them is a band; #f7f7f7 (1.06:1 off `table`) is the conventional weight, and the tree's own ratified step, `rdw::_shade_step`, would give #d7d7d7 (1.44:1) which is heavier than normal banding — the user has to pick. Adding a palette key also breaks the two hand-written palette stubs at test_calc_widgets.tcl:1212-1216/1244-1248 and test_rdw_window_1245.tcl:4570-4575 (`dict get` on a missing key throws), so either add the key AND update those three stubs, or derive the tone the way `_cursor_shade` does. test_results_dialog.tcl:783-793 asserts rselect's three tag foregrounds; a background-only band leaves it green.
- **ADE-L.** ADE-L bands the rows of its Design Variables and Outputs tables. Reading a wide Outputs table across to its Plot/Save columns is a thing ADE-L users do without thinking, which is the benchmark.

## [MAJOR] Pane ground vs panel is 1.12:1 and the heading strip vs panel is 1.09:1 — the tree already recorded this exact pair as a user-visible failure and worked around it everywhere except here

- **id** `panes-flat-against-panel`  ·  **effort** medium
- **Evidence.** Computed WCAG from the locked palette: table #ffffff vs panel #f2f2f2 = 1.12:1; header #e8e8e8 vs panel #f2f2f2 = 1.09:1. The tree already measured this and calls it a failure, in the user's own account: src/wave_viewer.tcl:18186-18196 — "`header` for the active tab and `panel` for the rest, which are #e8e8e8 and #f2f2f2 -- TEN levels apart in the ASE locked palette. On screen all three buttons were indistinguishable and the user could not tell which tab they were on, with 167 checks green" — answered there with THREE simultaneous cues. And src/wave_viewer.tcl:13008-13017 had to write an off-palette literal: "#b8b8b8 ... It is a LITERAL rather than a palette name because the locked palette has four entries and none of them is a divider tone." ase_large.png is the same defect unfixed in the main window: at 1500x900 the three panes plus their full-width heading strips are one continuous near-white field separated only by 1 px of ttk relief.
- **Why it hurts.** This is the window the designer stares at all day. The Design Variables / Analyses / Outputs panes are the whole product, and at any size above the default they stop being three panes and become one sheet with three small maroon words floating on it. The column headings — Name | Value | Plot | Save | Save Options — are structurally the most important row in each pane and they are 1.09:1 from their surroundings, readable only because ttk's default theme happens to draw a relief. Nothing in the palette can be spent to fix it: there is no tone at all between #d9d9d9 (L=0.694) and #4a6984 (L=0.132).
- **Fix.** Two parts. (a) Derived, no ruling: give each pane a real edge with `ase::shade` (see slick_move) rather than relief alone, and set the Labelframe relief/border from it. (b) Ruling required: darken `header` so the heading strip is at least ~1.3:1 from `panel` (e.g. #dcdcdc = 1.34:1, or the wave viewer's already-chosen #b8b8b8 = 1.87:1 as a rule tone). Retiring wave_viewer.tcl:13017's apologetic literal into the palette is part of the same decision.
- **Risk.** `header` IS USER-LOCKED — moving #e8e8e8 is a ruling, with the measured reason being the tree's own recorded eyeball failure at 1.09:1. It breaks test_ase_window.tcl:1386 (asserts the literal #e8e8e8 on Ase.Treeview.Heading) and would move test_wave_tabs.tcl:423, which reasons about the header-vs-panel pair by name. Adding a `rule` key breaks the palette stubs named in no-row-banding. test_results_dialog.tcl:783-793 reads through `ase::palette` and follows.
- **ADE-L.** ADE-L's panes carry visible titled frames with real separation from the window ground; you can see where one ends and the next begins without hunting for a 1 px line.

## [MAJOR] The one semantic colour in ASE-L is a red/green pair at 1.28:1 luminance, painted with three raw X11 names outside the locked palette, and its "finished" state fails AA

- **id** `status-colour-red-green`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** src/ase_window.tcl:6968-6981: `running { set bg orange }`, `ok { set bg Green; set txt Ready }`, `fail { set bg red; set txt Error }`, `default { set bg $idlebg; set txt Ready }`. None of the three is a palette name. Resolved live via `winfo rgb` on this display: orange=#ffa500, Green=#008000, red=#ff0000. Computed: red vs Green = 1.28:1 — the two are the same brightness. Black text on each ground: idle #f2f2f2 18.76, running #ffa500 10.63, fail #ff0000 5.25, ok #008000 4.09 — the only one that fails WCAG AA, and it is the state that says the run succeeded. Under Vienot-1999 simulation red and Green land at #5e5e0d/#797900 (protanopia) and #939300/#6d6d0f (deuteranopia): 1.48:1 and 1.67:1. Verified live on the real bench (.ase4): `ok` and `idle` set the identical text `Status: Ready`.
- **Why it hurts.** A tran run on tb_bandgap takes 8.12 s (logwin.png) and a real corner sweep takes minutes; the whole point of the status segment is that you glance at the corner and know. `ok` and `idle` carry the SAME WORD, so the difference between "the run finished" and "nothing has run yet" is carried by background colour alone with no text fallback at all. And the ok/fail pair is the textbook red/green metamer: at identical luminance a protan or deutan designer — roughly 1 in 12 men — reads two identical olive slabs, in the one place the tool reports success or failure. The word does differ there (Ready/Error), so it is recoverable by reading, but the glance is dead.
- **Fix.** Two halves. (a) No colour ruling: give `ok` its own word, so the state is legible without colour — `ok` and `idle` cannot both say "Ready". (b) Ruling: replace the three raw X11 names with three named palette entries whose luminances are separated, not just their hues, and each of which clears 4.5:1 under black text.
- **Risk.** THREE NEW COLOURS AND ONE NEW WORD ARE BOTH USER RULINGS — the palette is locked and a status word is a new user-facing sentence. `set_status`'s literals mirror `set_simulate_button` in src/xschem.tcl:16546-16554 (same bare `Green`/`red` on the stock menubar), so changing ASE-L's alone splits a convention the two windows currently share — say so when asking. Grep `status_text` across tests/headless before touching the word.
- **ADE-L.** ADE-L reports a finished run in its own status area and in a job monitor with an explicit word, not a colour alone.

## [MAJOR] The simulation log paints Warning, Note, results and the exit banner in identical black, while the sibling CIW already colour-codes exactly those and the user ratified the colours yesterday

- **id** `log-no-severity-colour`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** src/ase_window.tcl:7029-7035, `ase::ui::log_append`, is `$t insert end $text` with no tag argument; probed live, the log text widget's `tag names` is `{sel}` — it has no colour tags at all. logwin.png shows six distinguishable line kinds in one black: `Note: V5: dc value used for op instead of transient time=0 value.`, `Warning: singular matrix:  check node x1.XR5.x0.t1`, `binary raw file "..."`, `Doing analysis at TEMP = 27.000000`, `VBG = 1.177085e+00`, and `=== exit 0 after 8.12 s ===` (format string at src/ase.tcl:7489 — identical for a nonzero exit code). The convention already exists one window over and was ratified 24 hours ago in commit 19f8e351 with a measured table: src/ciw.tcl:449-451 and :490 give `input` blue 5.35, `result` gray30 5.26, `error` red 2.49, `note` dark red 6.23; src/rdw.tcl:3280-3282 mirrors it.
- **Why it hurts.** `Warning: singular matrix` is the line that tells an analog designer their OP did not converge, and in logwin.png it sits between two `Note:` lines in exactly the same ink. On a real bandgap log of hundreds of lines you scroll for it with your eyes doing the classification the tool refuses to do. This is the surface that carries every simulator failure, and it is the one surface in the family with no severity colour — the CIW, which carries far less consequential text, has four.
- **Fix.** Tag on insert in `log_append`: scan the appended delta for line prefixes and `$t insert end $line <tag>` instead of untagged. Reuse the CIW's four tag names and its exact four colours so nothing new is invented — `error` red for `Error:`/`error:`/a nonzero `=== exit N ===`, `note` dark red for `Warning:`, `result` gray30 for `Note:`, plain fieldfg for data. `log_append` receives partial deltas mid-run, so buffer to the last newline before tagging.
- **Risk.** NEEDS A RULING ON THE MAPPING ONLY — which simulator prefix counts as which severity is a decision, and it is simulator-specific (an ngspice `Warning:` is not a spectre `WARNING`). The colours themselves need no ruling: they are the CIW's, already chosen by the user in 19f8e351. No suite asserts log tags (grepped tests/headless for `log_append`: assertions are on text content, not tags), so tagging is additive and a suite comparing `$t get 1.0 end` is unaffected.
- **ADE-L.** ADE-L surfaces simulator errors and warnings distinctly rather than as one undifferentiated stream.

## [MAJOR] The maroon accent is 2.10:1 from the body text it must outrank, it is the only hierarchy cue in a window that is 81% bold, and it means two different things at once

- **id** `accent-alone-at-2.1`  ·  **effort** medium
- **Evidence.** Computed: accent #8b0000 vs fieldfg #000000 = 2.10:1 (Vienot protanopia 1.55:1, deuteranopia 2.41:1 — the maroon simulates to #303003 and #4e4e00). Live widget walk on the real bench window: 52 of 64 widgets carry AseLabelFont, which is bold — 81% — including all 31 menu entries (menu_sim.png: every entry of the Simulation menu is bold). apply_theme (src/ase_window.tcl:227-233) puts the bold font on Toplevel, Frame, Labelframe, Menu, Button, Label AND Checkbutton, so explanatory body copy is bold too — simulators.png's four-line paragraph and simdlg_editor.png's three-line "...has not been tried yet, so fold is all that can be offered" are both bold prose. And the accent carries two hierarchy levels at once: labelframe titles (:231) and DATA ROWS (rsel tags `cur`/`inreg`, :3879-3880). Sampled from rselect.png row y=51: #8B0000 at x=19-20 and x=161-166 — the row is the same maroon as the "Loaded — this session's viewer" title framing it.
- **Why it hurts.** With one weight for everything and one size step between chrome and data, colour is the only hierarchy signal left — and it is 2.10:1 from black, which at 10 pt on a light ground reads as "black, slightly warm", not as a different rank. ase_large.png is the proof: a 1500x900 window whose entire visual structure is three small maroon words. Worse, because the accent tags data rows too, in Results > Select the container title and its contents are the same colour, so the maroon stops meaning "this is a heading" and starts meaning nothing.
- **Fix.** Two parts, and the first needs no colour ruling: stop spending bold on everything. Reserve AseLabelFont (bold) for pane and column titles; give body copy, checkbutton captions and status sentences a regular-weight sibling at the same size. That restores a second hierarchy axis so the accent stops carrying the load alone. Second, decide what the accent means — title, or current-item — and give the other job a different signal (the rselect bullet already marks `cur`; the tag colour is redundant with it).
- **Risk.** A NEW NAMED FONT IS A RULING (the user picked Arial 10 bold; a regular companion is a new decision), and so is the accent's value if it is to move. Font changes move test_ase_window.tcl:547-550 (asserts the three named fonts exist) and every suite that reads a `-font` back — grep `AseLabelFont` across tests/headless: 9 suites reference it. The accent's value is asserted as a literal at test_ase_window.tcl:551 and read through `ase::palette` at test_results_dialog.tcl:790-792.
- **ADE-L.** ADE-L uses the platform's regular UI font for labels and reserves weight for headings; its pane titles do not share a colour with the rows inside them.

## [MAJOR] #b3b3b3 says "you may not touch this" in one dialog and "this is your current choice" in another, and the palette names it for neither

- **id** `grey70-three-meanings`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** Probed live: Tk's stock `entry -readonlybackground` and `-disabledbackground` are grey70 = #b3b3b3; `listbox -selectbackground` is grey70; classic `scrollbar -troughcolor` is #b3b3b3. All three appear in ASE-L and none is a palette value. (a) Edit Simulator's Name field is `-state readonly` (src/ase_window.tcl:5039) — measured in simdlg_editor.png, the field body is #B3B3B3. (b) Save All's Levels field is `-state disabled` (:5734) — measured in saveall.png, 2800 px of #B3B3B3. (c) Load State's Library and Cell listboxes (:6120-6121 set `-background` and `-font` but NOT `-selectbackground`) — measured in loadstate.png, the selected "sky130_tests_ase" and "tb_bandgap" rows are both #B3B3B3. Selection against the white listbox ground computes to 1.87:1. (d) logwin.png carries 4830 px of #B3B3B3 in the scrollbar trough. Separately the ttk scrollbars in the same Load State dialog have `-troughcolor #c3c3c3` — a different grey again, and its ttk::label column titles sit on #d9d9d9 (measured, loadstate.png).
- **Why it hurts.** The user opens Load State to change benches — one of the highest-stakes gestures in the tool, since OK discards the session — and the two panes that say which library and cell are chosen mark that choice with the same slab of grey that Edit Simulator uses to say "this field is locked". One colour, two opposite meanings, both live in a five-minute workflow. And the palette already owns the right answer: selectbg #4a6984 / selectfg #ffffff, 5.76:1, which is what every ttk Treeview in ASE-L already uses for exactly this — so Load State's listboxes and the panes disagree about what "selected" looks like inside one application.
- **Fix.** Give apply_theme a `Listbox` arm: `-background [ase::theme table] -foreground [ase::theme fieldfg] -selectbackground [ase::theme selectbg] -selectforeground [ase::theme selectfg] -font AseEntryFont` (this also picks up the Select Bus Bits list at :1411-1414, which today sets neither `-background` nor `-font` and renders stock grey80 in DejaVu Sans 10). Add to the `Entry` arm `-readonlybackground [ase::theme disabledbg] -disabledbackground [ase::theme disabledbg]`. Add `-troughcolor` to the Scrollbar arm and configure a matching `Ase.Vertical.TScrollbar -troughcolor`, so the two scrollbar flavours stop disagreeing.
- **Risk.** No new palette values — this only spends four colours the user already picked, so it needs no ruling except the trough tone (the palette has none; derive it with `ase::shade` to avoid adding a key and breaking the palette stubs at test_calc_widgets.tcl:1212 / test_rdw_window_1245.tcl:4570). Moves any suite that reads a listbox `-selectbackground` or the Select Bus Bits list's `-font` back: grep tests/headless for `loadst` and for the bus-bits dialog before landing.
- **ADE-L.** ADE-L's library/cell/view browser marks the chosen row with the platform selection colour, the same one its tables use — one meaning per colour.

## [MAJOR] Ase.TCombobox declares a white field and never renders one: both readonly comboboxes — including the control that picks which simulator runs — paint the palette's own disabled grey

- **id** `readonly-combobox-paints-disabled`  ·  **effort** trivial
- **Evidence.** src/ase_window.tcl:194 configures `Ase.TCombobox -fieldbackground [ase::palette table]` (#ffffff) but declares no state map. Probed live after `ase::theme`: `ttk::style lookup Ase.TCombobox -fieldbackground` = #ffffff, while `ttk::style map Ase.TCombobox -fieldbackground` is EMPTY, so the base `ttk::style map TCombobox -fieldbackground` wins — `readonly #d9d9d9 disabled #d9d9d9`. Both `-state readonly` comboboxes render it: Setup > Simulators "Use this one:" (:4550) — sampled from simulators.png, 4812 px of #D9D9D9; and Edit Simulator "Case:" (:5019) — sampled from simdlg_editor.png, 1736 px of #D9D9D9. #d9d9d9 is literally `ase::palette disabledbg`. The Calculator hit this exact trap, diagnosed it and fixed it: tests/headless/test_calc_skeleton.tcl:1271-1292 — "THE STYLE OPTION IS NOT WHAT PAINTS A READONLY COMBOBOX ... `ttk::style configure Calc.Field.TCombobox -fieldbackground` reported #ffffff while the widget rendered the stock #d9d9d9 (sampled from the live window: (217,217,217))" — and locks the map for both Calc styles. ASE-L never got it.
- **Why it hurts.** "Use this one:" in Setup > Simulators is the control that decides which binary runs, and it is painted in the same grey the application uses to mean "you cannot touch this" — sitting directly under a white table and above white Entry fields, so the contrast with its neighbours reads as inertness. The user's own ruling (registering is environment, choosing is state) makes that combobox the single most consequential widget in the dialog; it should not look dead.
- **Fix.** Two lines in `ase::theme`, copied from the Calculator's already-shipped answer: `ttk::style map Ase.TCombobox -fieldbackground [list readonly [ase::palette table] disabled [ase::palette disabledbg]] -foreground [list readonly [ase::palette fieldfg] disabled [ase::palette disabledfg]]`.
- **Risk.** NO RULING — it makes the widget render the value the palette already declares. The test pattern is written and proven: test_calc_skeleton.tcl:1279-1292's `stmap` helper asserts the map for the state the widget is actually in, plus a companion check that the widgets really are in that state; mirror it into test_ase_window.tcl. Nothing currently asserts Ase.TCombobox's map (probed: empty today), so there is nothing to break.
- **ADE-L.** In ADE-L the simulator choice is a live, editable-looking control, not something that reads as greyed out.

## [MINOR] A dark theme is blocked by two missing lines in apply_theme — RDW and the Calculator are already built and tested for one, ASE-L's own window is not

- **id** `dark-theme-blocked`  ·  **effort** medium
- **Evidence.** PROVEN LIVE, not argued: with a dark stub palette installed the way test_rdw_window_1245.tcl:4614 does it (panel #202020, table #303030), `ase::ui::apply_theme` returned Label fg=`black`, Button fg=`black`, Checkbutton fg=`black` on #202020, Entry fg=`black` on #303030, and the Listbox still grey80 — screenshot at scratchpad/lens2/dark_demo.png, script at scratchpad/lens2/dark.tcl. Computed: 1.29:1 for the classic widgets and 1.59:1 for the Entry — both below the 1.45:1 the user personally rejected in commit 19f8e351 as "practically unreadable". Cause: src/ase_window.tcl:227-239 sets `-background` and `-font` and sets `-foreground` for exactly one class (Labelframe, the accent, :231). The palette NAMES `fieldfg` and paints it in only two places tree-wide — the Ase.Treeview style (:207) and the rsel `plain` tag (:3881) — never on a single classic widget. Meanwhile RDW derives every colour from `ase::palette` and flips its shade direction on a dark ground (src/rdw.tcl:2498-2515), and its suite installs #202020 and #303030 palettes and asserts the window follows (test_rdw_window_1245.tcl:4614-4618); the Calculator reads through `calc::color` -> `ase::palette` (src/calculator.tcl:375-383) and its suite stubs the palette too (test_calc_widgets.tcl:1211-1248).
- **Why it hurts.** Not today's pain — nothing renders wrong on the shipped light palette — but it is the reason the answer to "can we have a dark ASE-L?" is currently no. And it matters for coherence right now: ase_root.png shows ASE-L's #f2f2f2 panel sandwiched between a black schematic canvas and a black waveform viewer with bright green/red traces. The designer's eye re-adapts on every window switch, all day. The family is two thirds ready and the flagship window is the third that is not.
- **Fix.** Add `-foreground [ase::theme fieldfg]` to apply_theme's classic arm (:227-233) and to the `Entry` (:234) and `Text` (:237) arms. After that, plus the Listbox arm from grey70-three-meanings and the status colours from status-colour-red-green, `ase::palette` becomes the single thing a theme has to change — which is what RDW and the Calculator already assume it is.
- **Risk.** THE DARK VALUES THEMSELVES ARE A USER RULING, including whether a dark ASE-L is wanted at all; the plumbing above is not — painting `fieldfg` where the palette says to paint it changes no pixel on the shipped palette (fieldfg is #000000, which is what every classic widget already renders). Note the accent is a light-theme-only value: #8b0000 on #202020 computes to 1.63:1, so a dark palette needs a different accent, a second ruling. Adding keys breaks the palette stubs at test_calc_widgets.tcl:1212/1244 and test_rdw_window_1245.tcl:4570.

## [MINOR] The palette's disabled pair computes to 1.79:1, worse than the tree-wide convention it departs from, and today it paints nothing at all

- **id** `disabled-pair-1.79`  ·  **effort** trivial
- **Evidence.** Computed: disabledfg #a3a3a3 on disabledbg #d9d9d9 = 1.79:1; on white it is 2.52:1. Both far below 3:1, and within 0.34 of the 1.45:1 the user rejected 24 hours ago (commit 19f8e351, "practically unreadable - orange on beige"). The pair is applied only to the Ase.Treeview state map (src/ase_window.tcl:209-213); grepped src/ase_window.tcl for a treeview put into `disabled` state — none, so the pair currently renders on zero pixels. Meanwhile the tree-wide convention is `option get . disabledForeground` = grey50 = #7f7f7f, which computes to 3.20:1 on #d9d9d9 and 5.32:1 on white — src/rdw.tcl:2411 names it "the tree-wide convention for greyed text" and src/calculator.tcl sets `-disabledforeground` from it on twelve widgets (:513, :521, :752, :1347, :1422, :1433, :1455, :1478, :1532, :1570, :2096, :2111). src/ase_window.tcl sets `-disabledforeground` on zero.
- **Why it hurts.** Latent rather than live, and marked minor for that reason — but it is the value the next greyed row inherits, in a window family that has already had one unreadable colour reported by the user in production. The second half is live and small: ASE-L's own disabled text is stock grey50 by accident, not by the palette, so the palette does not actually own the greyed state anywhere.
- **Fix.** Bring `disabledfg` to the tree-wide `disabledForeground` (grey50 / #7f7f7f), which clears 3:1 against `disabledbg` and matches what RDW and the Calculator already paint — one value, and it makes the family agree. Then set `-disabledforeground [ase::theme disabledfg]` on ASE-L's classic widgets the way calculator.tcl does, so the palette owns the state instead of Tk's default happening to be right.
- **Risk.** #a3a3a3 IS USER-LOCKED — moving it is a ruling, and the measured reason to ask is 1.79:1 versus the 1.45:1 the user already called unreadable, plus the fact that the sibling windows use a different, readable value for the same role. No literal assertion of #a3a3a3 exists outside the two palette stubs (test_calc_widgets.tcl:1215/1248, which hand-copy the dict and would need the new value); test_calc_skeleton.tcl:411's S13 role list is calc's own roles and is unaffected.


---

# layout-resize  (12 findings)

**Headline.** ASE-L asks the window system for nothing — no position, no minimum, no default size (the string "geometry" does not occur once in ase_window.tcl or ase.tcl) — and asks ttk for the wrong column policy, so its dialogs land in a corner of the desktop and sink behind the window that spawned them, while the panes ratchet their own columns out of shape a pixel at a time until the arguments of the sweep you are about to run are clipped off the right edge with no scrollbar to reach them.

**The one slick move.** One line — `catch {wm transient $w $top}` in `ase::ui::dialog_frame` (src/ase_window.tcl:1665) and at the eight hand-built `toplevel` sites (:3830 rsel, :4044 confirm, :4218 chana options, :4539 simulators, :5285 listdlg, :6114 loadstate, :7008 logwin). MEASURED, same dialog, same session, parent at 798x502+500+300: without it the Simulators dialog opens at `891x376+1027+0`, and `wm stackorder .` after clicking back on the ASE window reads `. .x1 .ase4.simdlg .ase4` — the dialog is UNDER its own parent. With it: `891x376+254+278`, centred on the parent, and the stack reads `. .x1 .ase4 .ase4.simdlg`. Nothing else in this dimension changes so much for so little: no test in tests/headless asserts `wm transient`, no palette value moves, no user-facing sentence is minted, and the fix reaches all seventeen dialogs. Every other finding here costs the user width or pixels; this one costs them the dialog itself — a 891px form dropped at the screen origin while they work at +500+300, that then hides behind the window they clicked, is the difference between an application that feels assembled and one that feels like it is fighting you. The two dialogs in this file that already do it (`ask_save_close` :399, `bus_dialog_build` :1403) prove the author knows the idiom; xschem.tcl uses it 33 times and library_manager.tcl 7. ASE-L is the outlier in its own tree.

## [CRITICAL] No ASE-L dialog sets wm transient or wm geometry, so the WM scatters them across the desktop and they sink behind their own parent

- **id** `no-transient-no-geometry`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** src/ase_window.tcl — `grep -n geometry src/ase_window.tcl src/ase.tcl` returns NOTHING; `wm transient` appears twice (:399 ask_save_close, :1403 bus_dialog_build) against 17 dialog toplevels (8 direct `toplevel` at :3830 :4044 :4218 :4539 :5285 :6114 :7008 plus `dialog_frame` :1667 serving 9 call sites at :1698 :1747 :1814 :4097 :4362 :5012 :5356 :5684 :6330). MEASURED live on :99, parent deliberately at 798x502+500+300 with the schematic also mapped: simulators `891x376+1027+0`, loadstate `541x363+0+0`, logwin `941x460+0+0`, rselect `597x430+1321+625`, chana `249x126+1580+437`, addvar `+0+485`, saveall `+0+603`, designdlg `+0+763`; every one reports `transient=` empty. The prompt's own screenshot geometries agree (simulators/loadstate/rselect/logwin all +0+0). Stacking, measured: with the Simulators dialog open, `raise .ase4` then `wm stackorder .` -> `. .x1 .ase4.simdlg .ase4` — the dialog is below its parent. Same dialog with only `wm transient $w $top` added: `891x376+254+278` (centred over the parent) and `wm stackorder .` -> `. .x1 .ase4 .ase4.simdlg`. Mechanism: ASE-L asks for no position, so placement is 100% the window manager's; /etc/xdg/openbox/rc.xml declares `<policy>Smart</policy><center>yes</center>`, which searches for a free rectangle and centres in it — a 249x126 dialog finds a gap near the parent and looks correctly placed by accident, while a 891x376 or 941x460 one fits in no gap at all and degrades to the monitor origin. On the user's real display (the HC-Consult Windows X server over TCP, per CLAUDE.md's three-server table) a different WM decides, so the position is not merely wrong, it is UNSPECIFIED per machine.
- **Why it hurts.** Every dialog open — twenty-plus times a working session — throws the form to a corner of a 1920x1080 desktop, up to 1580 px from the window that was just clicked. Then, because the dialog is not a transient, clicking back on ASE-L to re-read a value buries the dialog under it: the user is looking at the window they opened the dialog from, with the dialog gone. On a multi-monitor Windows X server the corner may not be the corner they are looking at.
- **Fix.** Add `catch {wm transient $w $top}` in `ase::ui::dialog_frame` (after `toplevel $w`, :1667) and at each of the eight hand-built toplevel sites. That alone gives centre-on-parent under openbox and stay-above-parent everywhere. If a deterministic position is wanted independent of WM, follow the tree's own idiom (xschem.tcl:11252 `wm geometry .dialog "+$X+$Y"`) and set `+[expr {[winfo rootx $top]+40}]+[expr {[winfo rooty $top]+40}]` after `update idletasks`.
- **Risk.** NO test in tests/headless asserts `wm transient` (grep: zero hits), so nothing reds. The real risk is semantic and is precedented in this very file: :5730 records that registering something in the shared `dialog_frame` "would change WM-close semantics for ~8 dialogs at once, none of them covered by a test" (issue 0651). A transient is iconified with its parent and on most WMs cannot be minimised independently — for the LOG window (:7008), which a designer may deliberately park separately while a long transient runs, that is a behaviour change the user should rule on. Recommend transient everywhere EXCEPT `$top.logwin`, and record a `rule` debt for the Log so the user decides whether it is a companion window or an independent one.
- **ADE-L.** Virtuoso forms open as transients over the ADE window they belong to and stay above it; they never fall behind the window that raised them.

## [CRITICAL] Every treeview column is -stretch 1, so column widths ratchet on each resize and never return — 'Save Options' clips permanently after one drag

- **id** `stretch-ratchet`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** src/ase_window.tcl:849 `$pf.tv column $c -width [dict get $widths $c] -anchor w -stretch 1` — no -minwidth, -stretch 1 on all 11 columns of the three panes. MEASURED hysteresis on :99, real bench, resizing 798x502 -> 560x360 -> 798x502 four times:  start ana: num=32 type=63 enable=63 args=262 / outs: name=120 value=110 plot=50 save=50 saveopts=90;  cycle 1 ana: num=60 type=62 enable=63 args=235 / outs: saveopts=87;  cycle 2 num=60 type=61 args=236;  cycle 3 num=60 type=60 args=237;  cycle 4 num=60 type=59 args=238. The one-digit `#` column ratchets 32 -> 60 px on the FIRST cycle and stays; `Type` bleeds 1 px per cycle indefinitely; `Save Options` goes 90 -> 87 while the heading needs 89 px in AseLabelFont (`font measure`), so after one resize it renders as `Save Option` and never recovers. Cause: ttk distributes slack EQUALLY IN ABSOLUTE PIXELS across stretchable columns and clamps each at the 20 px default minwidth on the way down, so the clamp is not undone on the way up. At 560x360, measured: ana num=20 type=23 enable=23; outs plot=20 save=20 — all at or near the floor.
- **Why it hurts.** There is no way back. The user drags the window once — to see the waveform viewer, to put ASE-L beside the schematic — and the table they read forty times a day is permanently mis-proportioned, with a heading truncated and a one-digit column twice the width of the column holding `tran`. Nothing in the UI resets it, there is no saved layout to reload, and closing and reopening the session rebuilds at the natural request, which the user then has to re-drag.
- **Fix.** Per column, declare `-minwidth` from measured content and `-stretch` from whether the column can USE slack. Measured (AseLabelFont headings / AseEntryFont cells, this display): `#` head 8 -> minwidth 34, stretch 0. `Type` head 32 / `tran` 31 -> minwidth 46, stretch 0. `Enable` head 45 / glyph 16 -> minwidth 56, stretch 0. `Arguments` head 72 / gold dc string 308 -> minwidth 120, stretch 1. Outputs: `Name` head 38 / `TEMPERAT` 98 -> minwidth 110, stretch 1; `Value` 37 -> minwidth 90, stretch 1; `Plot` head 26 -> minwidth 38, stretch 0; `Save` head 33 -> minwidth 44, stretch 0; `Save Options` head 89 -> minwidth 100, stretch 0. Variables: `Name` minwidth 110 stretch 0, `Value` minwidth 130 stretch 1. The pattern is ALREADY IN THIS FILE 3000 lines away: `ase::ui::rsel_build_list` :3896-3897 does `column mark -width 22 -stretch 0` / `column result -width 320 -stretch 1`.
- **Risk.** Zero named suites. `grep -n 'column .* -width\|-stretch' tests/headless/test_ase_*.tcl` returns nothing — no test reads a column width, minwidth or stretch flag. The 32 test references to `$top.body.<pane>.tv` are to the widget path and the -columns list, both unchanged. No palette value, no user-facing sentence.
- **ADE-L.** ADE-L's setup tables keep user-set column widths for the life of the session rather than redistributing them on every window resize.

## [MAJOR] The column policy spends the window's width on a digit and two checkboxes and starves the one column with unbounded content

- **id** `width-spent-on-checkboxes`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** MEASURED at 1500x900 (ase_large.png and probe output): the Analyses table is 890 px wide and allocates num=177, type=176, enable=180, args=355 — 536 px (60%) to a single digit, a 2-to-4-character word and a 16 px checkbox glyph. Outputs at the same size: plot=144, save=145 — 289 px for two checkbox columns against name=214. ase_large.png shows the digit `1` alone in a 177 px column. The starved column is the only one whose content is unbounded: the suite's OWN golden dc-row Arguments string, tests/headless/test_ase_window.tcl:527 `source=V2 start=0 stop=1.8 step=0.01`, measures 308 px in AseEntryFont — the Arguments column is 262 px at the default 798x502 and 235 px after one resize cycle, so an enabled DC sweep's arguments are CLIPPED IN THE DEFAULT WINDOW. Same defect twice more: `simulators_dialog` :4545-4547 gives `problem` (empty) 420 px and `path` 300 px while the real value `/home/analog/dev/ngspice/build-ver_50/src/ngspice` measures 410 px — simulators.png shows `/home/analog/dev/ngspice/build-ver_` cut mid-path; and the shared `listdlg` engine :5292 gives EVERY column a flat `-width 170 -stretch 1`, so modelfiles_main.png shows `$::SKYWATER_MO` for a value measuring 339 px while `Section` holds `tt_mm` (5 chars) in 170 px.
- **Why it hurts.** The three tables exist to answer three questions: what will I sweep, what will I save, which model file and which simulator binary am I about to run. In each case the pixels go to the column that answers nothing and the answer itself is cut. A designer checking a DC sweep before a 20-minute run cannot see the sweep; a designer chasing a wrong-corner result cannot see which .lib section is selected past `$::SKYWATER_MO`.
- **Fix.** The same change as the ratchet fix applied at all three sites: `-stretch 0` plus a measured `-minwidth` on every fixed-content column (#, Type, Enable, Plot, Save, Save Options, Section, Name-in-simulators), `-stretch 1` on exactly the content column (Arguments, Program, File, Value). Then all slack lands where it can be read and the tables stop growing checkbox columns.
- **Risk.** None measured. No suite reads any column width. `Problem` in simulators_dialog is a real (currently empty) column and must keep a minwidth wide enough for the sentences `ase::sim_why` mints into it — read that proc before pinning it; that is a read, not a ruling.

## [MAJOR] There is not one horizontal scrollbar in ASE-L, so anything clipped is simply unreachable

- **id** `no-horizontal-scrollbar`  ·  **effort** medium
- **Evidence.** `grep -n 'orient horizontal\|xscrollcommand\|xview' src/ase_window.tcl` returns exactly one hit, :6116, and it is a `ttk::panedwindow`, not a scrollbar. Every treeview is built with `-yscrollcommand` only (:844, :3889, :4541, :5288) and probed live returns `xscrollcmd=` empty on all three main panes. The log window :7010 is `text $lw.t -height 24 -width 84 -state disabled -wrap none` with a vertical scrollbar only — `-wrap none` and no way to scroll right. `load_state_dialog`'s three listboxes (:6120) are `-width 16` with vertical scrollbars only; loadstate.png shows `ngspice_verilog_cos` and `test_carry_lookahea` cut. For contrast in the same tree: `grep -c 'orient horizontal'` gives library_manager.tcl 1, calculator.tcl 2, xschem.tcl 5.
- **Why it hurts.** Clipping is normally an inconvenience; without a horizontal scrollbar it is data loss. A 410 px simulator path in a 300 px column, a 339 px model-file path in a 170 px column, an ngspice error line longer than 84 columns in the log — none can be brought into view except by widening the window, which the ratchet then punishes. Column-separator dragging does work (verified: `pane_click` returns 0 for heading-area clicks, so the ttk class binding survives the `break`), but it is invisible, undiscoverable and thrown away on the next resize.
- **Fix.** Give each table an `$pf.hsb` (`scrollbar -orient horizontal -command [list $pf.tv xview]`, treeview `-xscrollcommand [list $pf.hsb set]`), gridded rather than packed so the corner is clean; combine with the minwidth policy above so the tables have a real content width to scroll. Give the log `text` an hsb too — its `-wrap none` guarantees the need. Auto-hide with the tree's own idiom (rdw.tcl:2141 `pack forget .rdw.s.sb`) so a fitting table shows no bar.
- **Risk.** Adding `$pf.hsb` moves no asserted path: no ase suite calls `winfo children` on a pane frame (the only `winfo children` calls in test_ase_window.tcl are :107/:117/:126 over `.`, :135 over a generic $w, and :1390/:1441/:1451 over `$top.strip`). Do NOT add anything to `$top.strip`: W1s2/W1s2b (test_ase_window.tcl:1441-1454) require every child of the strip to carry a `strip_tips` entry, so a separator frame there reds two checks.

## [MAJOR] wm minsize is 1 1: below 256 px tall the waveform button is unmapped, below 228 px the Stop button is unmapped, and nothing says so

- **id** `no-minsize-buttons-vanish`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** Probed live: `wm minsize .ase4` -> `1 1` for the session window AND for all eight dialogs. Height sweep in 4 px steps on :99 at 798 wide: h=200 unmapped strip buttons {stop plot}; h=228 unmapped {plot}; h=256 unmapped {}. So the `~` (Waveform Viewer) button leaves the window below 256 px of window height and `!` (Stop) below 228. `winfo reqheight $top.strip` = 232 against a 27 px toolbar and a 19 px status bar. Width sweep: the `State: <view>` status label is dropped entirely below w=368-384 and is silently CUT (no ellipsis) anywhere below the bar's `reqwidth` of 531. At 120x100 the Analyses and Outputs panes themselves report `ismapped 0`.
- **Why it hurts.** This is the exact defect rdw.tcl:2769-2825 documents as issue 1382 — 'at 243 the packer starves the widget it allocated LAST ... the text-size control the user asked for by name is GONE' — recurring in a window that never declared a minimum at all. Here the widget that goes first is the Stop button for a running simulation. A designer who has parked ASE-L short beside a waveform viewer has no Stop, no visible reason why, and the menu path (`Simulation > Stop`, which issue 1389's refusal text names) is the only way out.
- **Fix.** Copy the shape the tree already uses twice — `rdw::min_floor` + `rdw::apply_minsize` (rdw.tcl:2803-2827) and `calc::min_floor` + `calc::apply_minsize` (calculator.tcl:259-269): a floor pair plus a derivation that raises it from `winfo reqheight $top.strip` (232) + tb (27) + status (19) + one pane's minimum, and a width floor of max(status reqwidth 531, strip 72 + the sum of the pane minwidths). Call it once at the head of `ase::ui::build` and once at the foot after `apply_theme`, pumping `update idletasks` — rdw's comment records why it must be twice. Set a default `wm geometry` too, so the window is not merely whatever the -width dict happens to sum to.
- **Risk.** No suite calls `wm geometry` or `wm minsize` on an ASE toplevel (grep over tests/headless/test_ase_*.tcl: zero hits), so nothing reds. CLAUDE.md caution: never measure against AUDIT_SCREEN=1600x1200. The floor VALUE is a judgement about how small the user may make their own window — derive it, do not pin it by hand, or it stops following the strip the way rdw's did twice.

## [MAJOR] Clipping is silent and mid-glyph: at 560x360 nine strings lose characters, and the default window already clips a design variable's value

- **id** `silent-midglyph-clipping`  ·  **effort** medium
- **Evidence.** ase_small.png at 560x360, read off 3x crops of the live shot and confirmed by `font measure` against the probed column widths. Headings: `Type` -> `Typ`, `Enable` -> `Ena`, `Plot` -> `Plo`, `Save` -> `Sa`, `Save Options` -> `Save Op`. Cells: `tran` -> `tra`, `op` -> `o`, `dc` -> `d`, `VCCGAUSS` -> `VCCGAUS` (needs 101 px in a 100 px column), `agauss(1.8, 'ABSVAR', 1)` -> `agauss(` (needs 204 px in 81), `TEMPERAT` -> `TEMPERA` (needs 98 px in 88). And at the DEFAULT 798x502, ase_main.png already shows the Design Variables Value cell as `agauss(1.8, 'A` — 204 px of content in a 120 px column. ttk::treeview in Tk 8.6 does not ellipsise; it cuts the glyph.
- **Why it hurts.** `agauss(1.8, 'ABSVAR', 1)` is a Monte Carlo variable: the sigma reference and the sigma count are exactly the characters that fall off. The user cannot tell a clipped cell from a short one — no `…`, no cell tooltip, no horizontal scrollbar. `tra` for `tran` and `d` for `dc` in the Analyses pane means the analysis TYPE, the first thing you check before a run, is unreadable at a size an ordinary drag reaches.
- **Fix.** Two halves. (a) The minwidth policy above stops the columns ever reaching these sizes. (b) Where content still exceeds a column, ellipsise in the cell and put the whole string in a tooltip — the file already has both pieces: `ase::ui::output_display_name` already does 21+`...` truncation for output expressions (golded at test_ase_window.tcl:504-508), and `rsel_tip` (:3901) already hangs a per-row tooltip off `<Motion>` over a treeview. Apply the same pair to the Variables Value cell and the Analyses Arguments cell.
- **Risk.** (a) is test-free. (b) changes displayed cell TEXT, which several suites read: test_ase_window.tcl:1376 asserts `[$top.body.vars.tv set $vgsit value]` is exactly `1.8` and :1380/:1382 assert empty Value/saveopts cells. Those strings are short and would not truncate, but a truncation rule on the vars pane must be checked against W1p. The truncation THRESHOLD and the `...` spelling are user-facing and unratified — reuse `output_display_name`'s existing 21+`...` rather than minting a second rule.

## [MAJOR] The three panes have no sash: the split is frozen at 1:2 wide and 1:1 tall, so Design Variables owns 89% empty space while Outputs is capped at half the right column

- **id** `no-sash-frozen-split`  ·  **effort** large
- **Evidence.** src/ase_window.tcl:333-337 — `grid columnconfigure $top.body 0 -weight 1` / `1 -weight 2`, `rowconfigure 0 -weight 1` / `1 -weight 1`, three labelframes gridded into a plain `frame`. No panedwindow, no sash, no way to change it. MEASURED on the real bench at 1500x900: vars treeview 496x827 holding 3 rows = 90 px of content, 737 px empty (89%); ana 890x402 with 4 rows = 113 px content, 289 px empty; outs 890x402 with 5 rows = 136 px content, 266 px empty. ase_large.png shows it: an almost entirely white left column three quarters of the window tall. `load_state_dialog` :6116 already uses `ttk::panedwindow -orient horizontal` for its three browser columns, so the idiom is in the file.
- **Why it hurts.** The ratio is backwards for real work. A bench has 3-8 design variables (this one has 3), 2-5 analyses, and 20-50 outputs once a designer has clicked a dozen nets with Select On Design. Outputs is the pane that needs to grow and it is welded to half the right column forever; Design Variables never needs to grow and owns a third of the width at full height. No drag, no collapse, no memory.
- **Fix.** Make `$top.body` a `ttk::panedwindow -orient horizontal` holding the vars labelframe and a nested vertical `ttk::panedwindow` holding ana and outs, each `add`ed with a `-weight` and pane `-minsize`s derived from content the way `calc::pane_min` does. Persist the sash positions with the window geometry (the Calculator already saves and restores `wm geometry .calc`, calculator.tcl:2496-2511) so the user sets the split once.
- **Risk.** THIS is the expensive one. A ttk::panedwindow manages only its direct children, so ana and outs must move under an intermediate pane: `$top.body.ana` -> `$top.body.right.ana`.
