That moves 32 references across FIVE named suites — tests/headless/test_ase_window.tcl (20), test_ase_dialogs.tcl (4), test_ase_interact.tcl (4), test_ase_launch.tcl (3), test_ase_persist.tcl (1). `$top.body.vars` can stay put if vars is the first pane. Sash positions and whether the split persists across sessions are user-visible layout decisions — record a `rule` debt, do not choose here. If that cost is not worth paying now, the path-preserving 80% is: keep the grid, add `grid rowconfigure/columnconfigure -minsize`, and give row 1 (Outputs) `-weight 2`, which moves no test at all.
- **ADE-L.** ADE-L separates its Design Variables / Analyses / Outputs areas with draggable splitters and remembers the split; the user sets the proportion once for the bench they work on.

## [MINOR] Six padding numbers in the whole 356-line build proc, all 1 or 2 px — the main window is flush to the edge while its own dialogs use 8

- **id** `flush-to-edge-2px`  ·  **effort** trivial
- **Evidence.** src/ase_window.tcl, `ase::ui::build` :487-843, ALL padding in the proc: `-padx {6 2} -pady 2` (temp entry :732), `-pady 2` (status :746), `-padx 2 -pady 1` (strip buttons :782), `-padx 2 -pady 2` ×3 (the three labelframes :330-332). Nothing else; `pack $top.body -side top -fill both -expand 1` carries no padx. MEASURED: body at x=0 y=27; the first labelframe 2 px from the window's left edge; the action strip's right edge 0 px from the window's right edge at every size tested (798, 1500, 400). By contrast `ase::ui::dialog_row` :1677-1678 uses `-padx {8 6} -pady 2`, `dialog_buttons` :1691 `-padx 8 -pady 6`, and `load_state_dialog` :6135-6137 `-padx 8 -pady 6`.
- **Why it hurts.** It is why the window reads as a Tk demo rather than a tool: content touching the frame on three sides, panes 4 px apart, action-strip buttons 2 px from the window border. ase_root.png next to the waveform viewer and the schematic makes it plain — ASE-L is the one window with no margin. It also costs legibility: with 2 px between the vars pane and the ana pane the two tables read as one grid.
- **Fix.** One consistent scale. `pack $top.body ... -padx 6 -pady {0 4}`; labelframes at `-padx 4 -pady 4`; strip `pack $top.strip -side right -fill y -padx {4 6} -pady 4`; status `-padx 6`. Keep the dialogs' existing 8/6 so the two surfaces agree.
- **Risk.** None. No suite measures a gap or a widget x/y; nothing moves a path. Purely additive arguments to existing pack/grid calls.

## [MINOR] Six classic Tk scrollbars with sunken 3D relief bolted to flat ttk treeviews, permanently mapped with nothing to scroll

- **id** `classic-3d-scrollbars-always-mapped`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** src/ase_window.tcl uses classic `scrollbar` at :852 (the three main panes), :1414, :3898 (rsel, on a ttk::treeview), :4548 (simulators, on a ttk::treeview), :5294 (listdlg, on a ttk::treeview), :7012 (log) — and `ttk::scrollbar` exactly once, :6123 in load_state_dialog. Four of the six classic bars are attached to ttk widgets. Probed live: all three main-pane bars report `relief=sunken bd=1 ismapped=1 w=13` with 3, 4 and 5 rows in an 8-row-high table — nothing to scroll, thumb filling the trough. A 5x crop of ase_main.png shows the Motif-era sunken trough, raised thumb and triangular arrow button immediately beside the flat ttk table and the mid-glyph-clipped `agauss(1.8, 'A`. `apply_theme` :244 additionally paints the trough with the panel colour, which is what makes the bevel read so strongly against the white table.
- **Why it hurts.** It is the loudest visual inconsistency in the window and it costs real width: 13 px per pane, 39 px of the 726 px body, permanently, for three bars that scroll nothing on a typical bench. rselect.png shows the same on two panes holding one row each.
- **Fix.** Swap the four bars attached to ttk::treeview widgets to `ttk::scrollbar` (:852, :3898, :4548, :5294) — one word each, and :6123 proves they theme correctly here. Then auto-hide: on `-yscrollcommand`, if the fractions are `0 1`, `pack forget` the bar and re-pack when they are not (rdw.tcl:2141 is the tree's existing `pack forget` idiom). `apply_theme`'s `Scrollbar` arm at :244 needs a `TScrollbar` sibling or the ttk bars fall through unstyled.
- **Risk.** `apply_theme` is asserted by W1's themed-widget legs (test_ase_window.tcl around :548-556, palette + named fonts across the tree); a ttk::scrollbar ignores `-background`, so the `catch` at :244 swallows it silently but that assertion should be re-read before the swap. Auto-hide changes `winfo ismapped $pf.sb` — nothing asserts it today. The scrollbar TROUGH colour is not one of the nine locked palette entries; a ttk::scrollbar picks up the ambient ttk `default` theme, so the swap does change a pixel the user did not choose — worth telling them rather than changing silently.

## [MINOR] The toolbar band is a full-width strip that is 87-93% empty, with an unlabelled entry floating at its left end

- **id** `empty-toolbar-band`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** src/ase_window.tcl:731-737 — `frame $top.tb` holding exactly two widgets, `entry $top.tb.temp -width 7` and `label $top.tb.degc -text "°C"`, both `-side left`, then `pack $top.tb -side top -fill x`. MEASURED: tb is 27 px tall and spans the full window width; its content ends at x=102, leaving 696 px empty at the default 798x502 and 1398 px empty at 1500x900. The file's own comment at :745 says of the entry: 'This entry is the only widget in the window carrying no word of its own -- the `°C` label beside it gives the unit, not the subject.' ase_main.png and ase_large.png both show a bare `27 °C` adrift at the top-left with an empty grey band running to the right edge.
- **Why it hurts.** A 27 px band across the top of every ASE-L window, at every size, carrying one seven-character field. It is the most valuable real estate in the window — directly under the menubar, where every other window in this application puts a toolbar (xschem's own schematic window has an icon row there, visible in ase_root.png) — and it holds 13% of a line of content, while the run controls are eight unlabelled glyphs stacked 232 px down the right edge and the design/state identity is 19 px tall at the bottom.
- **Fix.** Layout half only: label the temperature field so the band carries a subject (a `Temperature:` label before the entry, `°C` after — the balloon at :747 already holds the words), and either right-pack something useful into the band or stop making it full-width. WHAT else goes in that band is a design decision for the user, not a layout fix.
- **Risk.** Adding a label mints a user-facing string — per house rule, a decision the user ratifies; record a `rule` debt. It also adds a child to `$top.tb`; no suite enumerates `winfo children $top.tb` (only `$top.tb.temp` is asserted), so the path is safe. Anything MORE than a label is a proposal, not a fix.
- **ADE-L.** ADE-L keeps a horizontal toolbar row directly under the menubar as well as the right-hand vertical icon column — so the vertical strip is correct and should not move; it is the band's emptiness that is the anomaly.

## [MINOR] dialog_buttons throws OK and Cancel to opposite edges — 561 px of dead air between the buttons of one decision

- **id** `buttons-at-opposite-ends`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** src/ase_window.tcl:1685-1690 — `pack $w.btns.proceed -side left -padx 5` / `pack $w.btns.cancel -side right -padx 5` inside a frame gridded `-sticky we`, so the two buttons are pinned to the dialog's two edges and the gap grows with the dialog. Same idiom hand-written in `simulators_dialog` :4570-4576 and `load_state_dialog` :6135. MEASURED on the live Simulators dialog (891x376): Add x=5, Edit x=83, Remove right edge 243, Close x=804 — a 561 px gap between Remove and Close. simulators.png shows it. chana_opt_dc.png (456 px wide) shows OK at the far left and Cancel at the far right of the same row.
- **Why it hurts.** Confirm and cancel are one decision and the mouse has to cross the whole dialog to change its mind. At 891 px that is a deliberate trip. It also reads as unfinished: no toolkit convention puts the two halves of a choice at opposite ends of a form.
- **Fix.** Cluster them: `pack $w.btns.cancel -side right -padx {6 8}` then `pack $w.btns.proceed -side right`, so OK/Cancel sit together at the trailing edge and destructive verbs (Remove) stay left with the other list actions. One edit in `ase::ui::dialog_buttons` covers nine dialogs; the three hand-written rows need the same.
- **Risk.** Button ORDER is a convention the user may hold a view on (OK-then-Cancel vs Cancel-then-OK differs by platform), so the order is a `rule`, not a fix; the CLUSTERING is not. No suite asserts a button's x — the ase suites invoke buttons by path (`$top.strip.var invoke` etc.), unaffected. `dialog_buttons` is the shared scaffold, so this carries the same nine-at-once exposure :5730 records for issue 0651: no test covers those dialogs' button geometry.

## [MINOR] The status bar loses its right-hand segments with no ellipsis, and the window's 1x1 minimum makes it reachable by an ordinary drag

- **id** `status-bar-clips-silently`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** src/ase_window.tcl:739-754 — nine `label`s packed `-side left` in a frame packed `-fill x`, no `-wraplength`, no truncation, no `-anchor` policy. MEASURED: `winfo reqwidth $top.status` = 531 px; `State: ngspice_state1` alone measures 146 px in AseLabelFont and sits at x=381. Width sweep in 4 px steps: at w=384 everything is mapped; at w=368 the `state` label is dropped by the packer; at w=300 the preceding `|` separator goes too. Between 384 and 531 the label is mapped but CUT — at 400 px only 19 px of a 150 px label is on screen. ase_small.png at 560 wide is the last size at which the bar is whole.
- **Why it hurts.** Three of the five things the status bar exists to tell you — T, simulator, state view — are the right-hand three, and they are the ones that disappear first. A session named `ngspice_state1` versus `debug_st1` (both exist in this bench, loadstate.png) is the difference between the run you meant and the run you did not, and the label vanishes with no mark to say it did.
- **Fix.** Comes largely free with the minsize finding (a 531 px width floor keeps the bar whole). Beyond that: give the last segment `-anchor w` in an expanding cell so it truncates at the END rather than being dropped whole, and reuse the ellipsis+tooltip pair proposed for the table cells.
- **Risk.** The status text is composed by `refresh_status`/`status_text` (:6743-6789) and its exact strings are asserted by the ase suites (`$top.status.stat`, `.sim`, `.state` across test_ase_window.tcl and test_ase_launch.tcl). Truncating a segment changes what `cget -text` returns and WOULD red those — so truncate at the DISPLAY level only (clip via `-width` in characters, or a separate rendering) and leave the label's text intact, or accept moving those checks.


---

# action-strip  (9 findings)

**Headline.** The strip is eight un-guessable ASCII placeholders whose only explanation — the tooltips added by issue 1391 — I measured being repainted to invisible by ASE-L's own theme walk and covering 81% of the next action button, while four exact 24x24 icons for these very actions are already loaded, unused, in the same interpreter.

**The one slick move.** Replace the eight ASCII placeholders with eight 24x24 icons and fix the two tooltip defects in the same pass — but if it is truly ONE change, it is the icons, because everything else on this list is downstream of them. Four of the eight are free and already in the process (imgEditDelete for X, imgSimulate for Run, imgWaves for Plot, and imgNetlist as the base for Netlist-and-Run — I rendered all four and they are exactly right), Stop is six lines of `photo put` with no asset at all, and the remaining three plus a proper doc+play glyph go through the SVG->GIF->base64 pipeline that resources.tcl already documents, under a license the tree already carries. Measured, the swap costs ONE test row (test_ase_window.tcl:1391 W1s) because all 51 other strip references drive by widget path; it narrows the strip from 72 px to 30 px, giving 42 px back to the columns that are clipping at 560x360; it makes `N&>` and `>` two different pictures instead of two adjacent punctuation marks, which is the one place in this window where a misread costs a wrong number rather than a wasted click; and it ends the state where ASE-L is the only toolbar in a tool full of icon toolbars that ships its buttons as `-text` placeholders the source itself has called placeholders since it was written. The proposed strip, in the spec's own ADE-L order, paths unchanged: .ana (new: axes with an OP dot and a tran curve) 'Analyses > Choose…' F2 | .var (new: x= in a box) 'Add Variable' F3 | .out (new: probe arrow onto a wire) 'Add Output' F4 | gap | .del imgEditDelete 'Delete Selection' Delete | gap | .netrun (new: document with a play triangle) 'Simulation > Netlist and Run' F6 | .run imgSimulate 'Simulation > Run' Shift+F6 | .stop (drawn square) 'Simulation > Stop' F7 | gap | .plot imgWaves 'Tools > Waveform Viewer' F8. Every tooltip string above is the constant that already ships (ase::ui::strip_tips, src/ase_window.tcl:5588-5598) — no new sentence, nothing to ratify there. The four new glyphs and the eight accelerators ARE new user-visible decisions and belong in front of the user before they ship.

## [CRITICAL] `N&>` sits directly above `>`, they differ by two characters, and the wrong one silently simulates a stale deck

- **id** `run-pair-stale-deck`  ·  **effort** medium
- **Evidence.** src/ase_window.tcl:772-779 packs `.strip.netrun` (-text {N&>}) immediately above `.strip.run` (-text {>}), both -width 5, 2 px apart — visible in ase_main.png down the right edge and at 300% in the crop I took (strip_zoom.png): the two adjacent cells read `N&>` and `>`. src/ase.tcl:7048-7066 `ase::run_existing` checks only `file isfile <rundir>/<cell>.spice` and then runs it; there is no comparison against the schematic's mtime and no warning. Measured live on :99 (both buttons, no run in flight, no netlist artifact): `state=normal` on all eight, always — `grep 'strip\.' src/ase_window.tcl` returns only the eight build lines and the tooltip loop, no `-state` anywhere in the file.
- **Why it hurts.** This is the button an analog designer presses twenty times an hour. You change a device width, aim one row low, and get yesterday's deck re-simulated with today's confidence: a plausible number, no error, nothing on screen that says the deck is older than the schematic. The two most-pressed controls in the window are separated by one character of ASCII and two pixels of padding, and the recovery — noticing the answer did not move — is exactly the failure mode analog debugging is worst at catching.
- **Fix.** Three parts. (a) Give the two different 24x24 pictures rather than different punctuation — `.run` can use the shipped `imgSimulate` (a solid play triangle, verified by rendering it), `.netrun` needs a real doc+play asset (see the icon finding; my 12x12 subsample composite of imgNetlist+imgSimulate measurably does not read — mock_strip_big.png row 6). (b) Put a pack gap between the setup group and the run group, the shipped in-tree precedent being the RDW's button column (issue 1382 separates three groups with two gaps). (c) `-state disabled` on `.run` when `<rundir>/<cell>.spice` does not exist, so the stale-deck button is not even live before the first netlist. A staleness MARKER (deck older than the .sch) would be a new user-visible sentence and is the user's to ratify, not mine to invent.
- **Risk.** Gating `.run` and `.netrun` moves no test row — the only state assertions in the suites are on `.plot` (tests/headless/test_ase_window.tcl:1393, tests/headless/test_ase_plot.tcl:378 and :576, all three asserting `.strip.plot` is normal / not disabled). Changing the two `-text` values reds exactly one row, test_ase_window.tcl:1391 W1s `{OP,TR = --> X N&> > ! ~}`. A staleness sentence needs ratification.
- **ADE-L.** ADE-L gives Netlist-and-Run and Run distinct pictorial icons on its own right-hand vertical toolbar; they are never distinguished by a single character of text.

## [CRITICAL] ASE-L's own theme walk repaints a live tooltip to panel colour on panel colour — the strip's only words vanish while still on screen

- **id** `tip-repainted-invisible`  ·  **effort** trivial
- **Evidence.** Measured by me live on :99 in the current tree, hovering `.ase4.strip.del` and calling apply_theme with the tip up:
  before: bg=lightyellow font=fixed border=black
  after : bg=#f2f2f2   font=AseLabelFont border=#f2f2f2
Mechanism: `balloon_show` (src/xschem.tcl:14948) makes the tip a CHILD toplevel named `$w.balloon` with a `Label` inside it; `ase::ui::apply_theme` (src/ase_window.tcl:224-251) matches Toplevel/Label in its switch and then recurses `foreach c [winfo children $w]` with no exclusion, so the live tip is inside the walk. Reachable in one gesture: hover `X` for 300 ms (tip appears), click it — `ase::ui::delete_selection` (:991) calls `ase::ui::populate` (:1010), which ends at :1594 with `ase::ui::apply_theme $top`. 15 call sites of populate, 24 of apply_theme.
- **Why it hurts.** Issue 1391 exists because the eight glyphs are not guessable; the tooltip is the entire remedy. A tip repainted to #f2f2f2 text-panel on a #f2f2f2 window with a #f2f2f2 border is not a degraded tooltip, it is an invisible rectangle that still owns the pointer — so the user who reached for the explanation gets nothing back and does not even see that something appeared. The failure is silent, and no headless row can see it because the suites read the `<Enter>` binding string and never render a tip.
- **Fix.** One line at the top of `ase::ui::apply_theme`, before the switch: return when the widget is a balloon — `if {[winfo name $w] eq {balloon}} { return }`. `balloon_show` always names it exactly `balloon` (src/xschem.tcl:14948 `set top $w.balloon`), so the test is exact and it skips the tip's Label child too, since the walk never descends.
- **Risk.** No suite asserts anything about a balloon's colours or font; the theming rows (W1p, the heading-colour row at test_ase_window.tcl:1385) read Treeview style, not balloon widgets. No user-visible copy changes, no palette change, so no ratification. Already recorded, measured, as an unpaid look debt (`owed.sh list`, entry `ASE-L_tooltips__two_pixel_effects_no_headless_row_can_see`) — the user has been told, so fixing it does not open a new question.

## [MAJOR] Every tooltip lands on top of the next action button — 81% coverage, measured, and `winfo containing` returns the tooltip where the button should be

- **id** `tip-covers-next-button`  ·  **effort** trivial
- **Evidence.** Measured live on :99, the `X` (Delete Selection) tip against the `N&>` button below it, window at 798x502+100+100:
  tip rect      x 829..971  y 292..316
  N&> button    x 829..897  y 294..321
  OVERLAP       68x22 px = 81% of the N&> button's area
  winfo containing at the N&> centre (863,307) = .ase4.strip.del.balloon.txt
Cause: src/ase_window.tcl:806 calls `::balloon $top.strip.$sfx $tip 1 0 300` — pos=1 — and `balloon_show` anchors a pos-1 tip at `[winfo rooty $w]+[winfo height $w]` (src/xschem.tcl:14961-14962), i.e. directly below the widget. On a vertical column, 'below the widget' is always another live control. The rdw.tcl call this was copied from is packed -side bottom with nothing beneath it.
- **Why it hurts.** You cannot read the strip. Hovering a glyph to learn what it is paints the answer over the next glyph you were about to hover, so learning the strip is eight separate hover-move-wait cycles with the target occluded each time, and the tip you are reading is sitting on the control you are aiming at. This is the tree's first pos-1 tooltip placed over another live control, and it is a direct consequence of the strip being a vertical column.
- **Fix.** Change the pos argument from 1 to 0 at src/ase_window.tcl:806 — a pointer-anchored tip. `balloon_show` already carries issue 1368's edge clamp AND its explicit guard that pushes a tip clear of the pointer (src/xschem.tcl:14999+), which is the flicker hazard pos 0 used to have, so the pos-0 path is safer now than when this line was written. The alternative fix is geometric: lay the strip out horizontally (see the dead-column finding), which makes 'below the widget' empty again and keeps pos 1.
- **Risk.** ZERO test rows. I checked the reader: `w_tip` (tests/headless/test_ase_window.tcl:1412-1419) extracts only `[lindex [lindex $scr [expr {$i+2}]] 0}]` — the tip STRING — and never looks at the pos argument. W1s3 (:1468) tests only that both the balloon and the temp_commit halves are present in the composed <FocusOut>. No copy changes, no ratification. Also recorded, measured, in the same unpaid look debt as the repaint.

## [MAJOR] What the eight glyphs actually are: an English sentence, three operators, a punctuation mark and a literal ampersand Tk draws as an ampersand

- **id** `glyph-vocabulary`  ·  **effort** medium
- **Evidence.** Read off src/ase_window.tcl:762-779 with each -command traced:
  OP,TR -> choose_analyses (the Choose Analyses dialog, four radios)
  =     -> add_variable_dialog (Add Variable)
  -->   -> output_editor -1 (Add Output)
  X     -> delete_selection (:991, no confirm)
  N&>   -> do_run (:7328, Netlist and Run)
  >     -> do_run_existing (:7424, run the deck on disk)
  !     -> do_stop (:7460)
  ~     -> open_viewer (:3030)
The source itself calls them 'text placeholders' at :756; the spec calls them 'text placeholders for now — real icons welcome where Tk can do them' at doc/claude/specs/ase_l.md:906-907. On the `&`: Tk's classic Button has no Windows-style mnemonic parsing (`-underline` is index-based and defaults to -1, measured), so the ampersand is drawn literally — `font measure AseLabelFont {N&>}` = 28 px against `{N>}` = 18 px, and it is plainly visible in my 300% crop strip_zoom.png. `N&>` is an abbreviation of the English sentence 'Netlist and Run' rendered as a letter, a conjunction-symbol and a comparison operator; nothing about it points at simulation.
- **Why it hurts.** Of the eight, exactly one is guessable to someone who has not read the source: `X` for delete. `>` is a play triangle in every other tool but here it is the RARE run (existing netlist), while the common one is `N&>`. `~` is a waveform in someone's head and a bitwise-NOT in an analog designer's. `=` and `-->` are both 'add something' with no plus sign between them. `OP,TR` names two of the four analyses the dialog behind it offers (op, dc, ac, tran — visible in ase_main.png's Analyses pane), so the button's own label under-describes its dialog. A daily user memorises positions rather than meanings, which is exactly how the wrong one gets pressed.
- **Fix.** Replace -text with -image (see the shipped-icons finding) and keep the widget paths. If icons are rejected, the fallback is words: the strings already exist as ratified constants (`ase::ui::lbl_add_variable`, `lbl_add_output`, `lbl_delete_selection`, `lbl_netlist_and_run`, `lbl_run`, `lbl_stop`, `lbl_waveform_viewer`, src/ase_window.tcl:5531-5556) so a word-labelled strip invents no new sentence — but 'Netlist and Run' is 188 px in AseLabelFont, so a word strip has to be horizontal, not a column.
- **Risk.** tests/headless/test_ase_window.tcl:1391 W1s asserts the exact list `{OP,TR = --> X N&> > ! ~}` — one row, rewritten. Everything else that touches the strip drives it by path: 17 references in test_ase_dialogs.tcl, 6 in test_ase_plot.tcl, 2 in test_ase_interact.tcl, 26 in test_ase_window.tcl, and all of them are `$top.strip.<sfx> invoke` or `cget -state`, so they survive unchanged as long as the eight widget names stay.
- **ADE-L.** ADE-L's equivalent toolbar is pictorial icons with hover tooltips, not ASCII text — this is the one place ASE-L's transcription of ADE-L stopped at the layout and skipped the artwork.

## [MAJOR] No strip button ever changes state: Stop, Run and Plot are always live and report their refusal into the CIW, where nobody is looking

- **id** `strip-never-changes-state`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** `grep 'strip\.' src/ase_window.tcl` returns the eight `button` lines (:762-779), the pack (:780), and the tooltip loop (:806) — and nothing else in 7491 lines. No `-state` is ever configured on any strip child. Measured live with no run in flight and no netlist artifact: all eight `state=normal`. `ase::ui::do_stop` (:7460-7487) with nothing running ends at `catch {::ase::echo "ase: no simulation running for this session"}` — a line in the CIW, not on the window the user is looking at. `ase::run_existing` with no artifact raises 'ase: no netlist artifact: <path>' the same way.
- **Why it hurts.** The strip tells you nothing about what the session can do right now. A designer mid-run cannot see from the window whether Stop will do anything, and pressing it when it will not costs a trip to another window to read why. Worse in the other direction: after `run_started` there is no visual difference between a session that is simulating and one that is idle except the status-bar word, so `N&>` invites a second press — the very thing issue 1389's run guard had to be written to refuse in software because the button would not refuse it in pixels.
- **Fix.** Set `-state` from the two lifecycle points that already exist: `ase::ui::run_started` disables `.netrun` and `.run` and enables `.stop`; `ase::ui::run_finished` reverses it. `.run` additionally disabled while `<rundir>/<cell>.spice` is absent; `.plot` disabled while the session has no raw. The predicate for 'is something running' already exists and is shared — `ase::run_in_flight` on `ase::run_lock_key`, which do_stop itself uses at :7477.
- **Risk.** Three rows lock `.plot` as never-disabled and would have to move: tests/headless/test_ase_window.tcl:1393 ('W1s plot button normal (item 13: ~ live)') and tests/headless/test_ase_plot.tcl:378 and :576 ('P5 ~ strip button not disabled'). Those were written to fence item 13's promise that `~` is live, so re-pointing them at 'live when there is something to plot' is a change to a shipped promise and worth a ruling. Gating `.run`/`.stop`/`.netrun` moves nothing.

## [MAJOR] Four exact 24x24 icons for these very actions are already loaded in the same interpreter, unused, with a documented pipeline and a license for the rest

- **id** `icons-already-shipped-unused`  ·  **effort** medium
- **Evidence.** Measured live inside the running binary, before ASE-L opens: 33 `img*` photo images, all 24x24, created by `setup_toolbar` (src/xschem.tcl:19935, unconditional at GUI startup) which sources src/resources.tcl at :15472. I dumped and looked at them (iconstrip.png): `imgNetlist` is an arrow into a document, `imgSimulate` is a solid play triangle, `imgWaves` is a waveform on axes, `imgEditDelete` is a clean ✕ — those are precisely Netlist, Run, Plot waveforms and Delete Selection. Light AND dark variants both ship (resources.tcl:91 `if {$dark_gui_colorscheme == 0}`), the artwork is entypo-derived, Neil Johnson, CC BY-SA 4.0 (resources.tcl:57-62), and the authoring pipeline (SVG -> png -> transparent GIF -> base64) is written out at resources.tcl:33-51. Tk 8.6.17 here also takes base64 PNG directly via `image create photo -data` (verified: my first probe failed only because my test blob was corrupt; a real 424-byte base64 PNG loaded 16x16 fine). I built the alternative strip live: eight flat icon buttons measure 30x240 against the shipped 72x452 with 232 px used — narrower by 42 px and no taller.
- **Why it hurts.** 798 px is the natural window width (ase_main.png) and 72 of them are eight one-character buttons. The icons that would replace them are sitting in the same process, already themed for the same two colour schemes, and cost nothing to draw. Meanwhile the strip is the one part of ASE-L that looks unlike the schematic editor it launched from — xschem's own toolbar is 24x24 icons, flat relief, balloon tips (`toolbar_add`, src/xschem.tcl:15481-15497), which is the house style ASE-L's strip is the only toolbar in the tree not to follow.
- **Fix.** Switch the eight to `-image ... -relief flat -bd 0 -takefocus 0 -padx 0 -pady 0`, matching toolbar_add. Reuse `imgEditDelete` for `.del`, `imgSimulate` for `.run`, `imgWaves` for `.plot`. Draw Stop in place with no asset at all — `image create photo aseStop -width 24 -height 24; aseStop blank;` then twelve `put` rows for a filled square (verified working, renders cleanly in mock_strip_big.png). The remaining four — Choose Analyses, Add Variable, Add Output, and a real Netlist-and-Run — must be AUTHORED as 24x24 base64 GIFs through resources.tcl's own documented pipeline; say so honestly rather than shipping a composite, because I tried the composite (imgNetlist + imgSimulate subsampled to 12x12) and at 300% it is measurably unreadable.
- **Risk.** One test row (test_ase_window.tcl:1391 W1s, the -text list); all 51 other strip references drive by widget path and survive. No new dependency — the images are already in the process and the pipeline needs inkscape/imagemagick at AUTHORING time only, never at runtime. The palette is untouched: a GIF's transparent ground takes the button's own -background, so the locked #f2f2f2 panel stays. New artwork is a user-visible artefact and the four new glyphs should be shown to the user before they ship.
- **ADE-L.** ADE-L's toolbar is icons for exactly this action set; the ASE-L order at doc/claude/specs/ase_l.md:906-917 already transcribes ADE-L's, so this is finishing a transcription that stopped one layer short, not inventing a new design.

## [MAJOR] ASE-L has zero keyboard accelerators and its menubar's accelerator column is empty on every entry

- **id** `no-accelerators`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** `grep -c accelerator src/ase_window.tcl src/ase.tcl` = 0 and 0. Read live off the running menus: Simulation[0..5] — Netlist, Netlist and Run, Run, Stop, Log, Options… — every one `accel=` empty. The only key bindings on the ASE-L toplevel are `<Control-w>`/`<Control-W>` for close (src/ase_window.tcl:322-323); the other 19 `<Return>` and 7 `<Key-Escape>` binds in the file all belong to dialogs. By contrast xschem's own menus carry roughly thirty `-accelerator` strings.
- **Why it hurts.** The inner loop of an analog session is edit -> Netlist and Run -> look at waves -> edit, dozens of times an hour, and in ASE-L every lap of it requires taking a hand off the keyboard and hitting a 68x27 target that is one row away from a button that runs a stale deck. The schematic editor the user just came from is almost entirely keyboard-driven, so the mode switch is jarring as well as slow.
- **Fix.** Eight `bind $top <Key-...>` lines plus `-accelerator` on the five twinned menu entries so the keys are discoverable in the menu rather than in a manual. Use FUNCTION keys, not letters: the temperature entry `$top.tb.temp` (:726-729) takes focus and owns `<Return>` and `<FocusOut>`, so a letter accelerator on the toplevel would be typed into it or would have to fight the Entry class binding. Avoid F1 (help), F10 (Tk posts the menubar) and F11 (WM fullscreen). Proposed, in strip order: F2 Choose Analyses, F3 Add Variable, F4 Add Output, Delete for Delete Selection, F6 Netlist and Run, Shift+F6 Run, F7 Stop, F8 Plot.
- **Risk.** Every accelerator is a new user-visible decision and the letters are the user's to pick, not mine — this needs a ruling before it ships. No existing test asserts accelerators (there are none to assert). Adding `-accelerator` to a menu entry does not change its `-label`, so W1s4/W1s4b/W1s4c and W1s5 (which read `entrycget -label`) are untouched.

## [MINOR] The strip is a fixed 72 px column that never yields: 220 px of dead grey at the natural size, ~630 px at 1500x900, and full width while the tables clip at 560x360

- **id** `fixed-width-dead-column`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** Measured live: strip 72x452, the eight buttons using 232 px, leaving 220 px of empty panel. In ase_large.png (1500x900) the eight buttons sit at the top of a column of empty grey running the full height of the window. In ase_small.png (560x360) the strip is still 72 px — 13% of the window — while Design Variables shows 'VCCGAUS:' and 'VCCGAU\' truncated, and Outputs' headings are cut to 'Plc' 'Sa' 'Save Op'. Cause: `pack $top.strip -side right -fill y` at src/ase_window.tcl:783 with -expand 0, packed before the expanding body, so the tables are always the widget that gives. Related: `-width 5` sizes the box in CHARACTERS — 40 px — while the longest label `OP,TR` measures 42 px in AseLabelFont, so the strip's widest content has zero headroom (no clip is visible on :99 at 800% zoom, checked; but the user's display is a different X server that may substitute a wider family).
- **Why it hurts.** Every pixel the strip holds is a character of a variable name or an expression the designer cannot read. At 560x360 the expression column is the first thing to go and the strip is the last, which is exactly backwards: eight one-character buttons are not more valuable than 'agauss(1.8, ...' being legible. At 1500x900 a third of the right edge is empty grey doing nothing.
- **Fix.** Icons alone take it from 72 to 30 px (measured: 30x240 for eight flat icon buttons) and cost nothing else. Beyond that, ASE-L already HAS a horizontal toolbar row — `$top.tb` at src/ase_window.tcl:709-733, holding a 7-character entry and a °C label and using about 110 px of 798 (the wide empty band across the top of ase_main.png). Re-packing the strip as `-side top -fill x` under it, with the inner buttons `-side left`, gives the tables the whole width back, makes pos-1 tooltips land on empty space instead of the next button, and puts the toolbar where xschem's own toolbar is.
- **Risk.** Repacking `$top.strip` keeps the widget path, so all 51 test references survive; I grepped and no suite asserts `pack info` or `winfo manager` on the strip. Actually reparenting the buttons INTO `$top.tb` would rename every path and move all 51 — do not do that. A horizontal toolbar reverses the spec's stated 'right vertical action strip' (doc/claude/specs/ase_l.md:906) and diverges from ADE-L's layout, so it is a ruling, not a refactor; the icon-narrowing half is not.

## [POLISH] The tooltip — the strip's only words — is drawn in `fixed`, a server-supplied alias, making a third font family in one window and one that ignores tk scaling

- **id** `tooltip-font-is-fixed`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** `balloon_show` hardcodes `-font fixed` on the tip label (src/xschem.tcl:14957). Measured live on :99: `font actual fixed` = DejaVu Sans 12, linespace 20 — while ASE-L's own AseLabelFont is Nimbus Sans 10 bold and TkDefaultFont on the same display is DejaVu Sans 10. Same string, three widths: 'Simulation > Netlist and Run' is 246 px in fixed, 188 px in AseLabelFont, 203 px in TkDefaultFont. `fixed` is an X core font alias resolved by the server, not by Tk, so it does not follow `tk scaling` (1.3879 here) and on the user's actual display — the HC-Consult Windows X server over TCP, not :99 — it may resolve to a 6x13 bitmap.
- **Why it hurts.** The eight explanations are rendered in the one font in the application nobody chose and nobody can predict, on the one display nobody has measured. If it lands as a 6x13 bitmap on the user's real X server the tips are a different size from everything around them and do not grow with the display.
- **Fix.** Replace `-font fixed` with a named font in balloon_show. Do NOT do it as part of an ASE-L change: balloon_show is the tree's single tooltip renderer with 44 call sites across six files (recorded in the ledger), so this is a tree-wide decision with its own blast radius, and the honest ASE-L-scoped move is to record it and hand it to the user.
- **Risk.** One word of code, six files of consequence. Every tooltip in xschem, rdw, calculator, wave_viewer and the file browser changes size at once, which will move the clipping thresholds issue 1368 measured and the 1384 clipped-tip arming test. Needs its own issue and a ruling; it is not this strip's to change unilaterally.


---

# widget-idiom  (8 findings)

**Headline.** ASE-L is 110 classic Tk widgets with 12 ttk widgets scattered through them and a theme walker that covers neither completely, so the user-locked palette leaks stock #d9d9d9 grey into four of the eight screenshots — measured, not inferred — while the one gesture a designer repeats all day (flipping Plot/Save/Enable) is a text glyph that no keyboard can reach and no test covers.

**The one slick move.** Make `ase::theme`/`ase::ui::apply_theme` own EVERY widget class the window actually contains, and collapse the scrollbar split. Concretely, ~25 lines: (1) `ttk::style map Ase.TCombobox -fieldbackground [list readonly white disabled #d9d9d9]` — without the map the `configure` on :194 is dead for the two readonly comboboxes; (2) `Radiobutton` and `Listbox` arms in the :227 switch (Listbox also carrying `-selectbackground/-selectforeground` from the locked palette); (3) `ttk::style configure Ase.TFrame/Ase.TLabel/Ase.TScrollbar/Ase.TPanedwindow -background [ase::theme panel]` plus `-font AseLabelFont` on Ase.TLabel, and arms that apply them; (4) the six classic `scrollbar` constructors become `ttk::scrollbar` so one scrollbar look sits beside the treeviews. Why this one: it is the only change in this dimension that costs no user-facing sentence, no palette decision (it applies colours the user ALREADY locked), no widget path, and no test — grep shows zero ASE suites cget a scrollbar, a Radiobutton, a Listbox or a ttk container — and it deletes every stock-grey patch this audit measured in one pass. The idiom to commit to, and the migration order: **classic Tk for chrome (button/label/entry/checkbutton/radiobutton/frame/labelframe/menu), ttk ONLY where classic Tk has no equivalent (Treeview, Combobox) plus ttk::scrollbar as the deliberate exception because it is the only scrollbar that matches a Treeview, and ase::theme owns the styles for all of them.** That direction, not the reverse, because the census is 110:12 classic, because RDW and CIW (the sibling windows, same author, same palette) are 100% classic, and because the status light (`$top.status.stat cget -background` Green/orange/red, asserted in 5 places across 4 suites) and the whole palette apply through classic `-background`, which ttk cannot do without a named style per colour. Order: (1) finish the theme, above; (2) scrollbars; (3) `dialog_buttons` — adjacent equal-width pair, `-default active` on OK, `<Return>` on the toplevel; (4) `WM_DELETE_WINDOW` into `dialog_frame` (issue 0651); (5) checkbox-cell hot zone + a keyboard route (needs a ruling); (6) last and largest, extract ONE lib/cell/view browser shared by library_manager, save_as_form and ASE-L.

## [CRITICAL] The Plot/Save/Enable cells are text glyphs: no keyboard can reach them, a click anywhere in a 63 px column silently edits the deck instead of selecting the row, there is no undo, and no suite covers the gesture

- **id** `checkbox-cells-are-glyphs-mouse-only-and-a-silent-mutation-zone`  ·  **effort** medium
- **Evidence.** src/ase_window.tcl:1541 `chk_glyph` returns ☑/☐ as CELL TEXT; :858-860 binds `<Button-1>` to `pane_click` and the bind script `break`s; :917-933 `pane_click` toggles on ANY x/y inside the column and returns 1, so the class binding never runs and the selection never moves. Measured live on :99: the Enable cell bbox is `96 18 63 23` while the glyph is 16 px wide — 47 px of that cell toggle-on-click with no glyph under the cursor. Outputs row is 420 px wide (columns 120+110+50+50+90) so plot+save = 100 px = 24% of every row. The Treeview class bindings already own `<Key-space>` and `<Key-Return>` (selection toggle, printed live) and the instance bindings are only `<Button-3> <Double-Button-1> <Button-1> <<TreeviewSelect>>` — no key toggles a flag. `grep -c undo src/ase.tcl src/ase_window.tcl` = 1 and 1, both in comments: ASE-L has no undo. The Outputs menu's two cascades (:556-562) hold ONE entry each, `Select On Design` — there is no menu twin for the toggle. `grep -rn 'toggle_flag|pane_click' tests/headless/*.tcl` returns one COMMENT and zero drivers: the most-repeated gesture in the window has no coverage at all.
- **Why it hurts.** A designer selecting an output row to delete it clicks mid-row, lands in Plot or Save, and silently flips it. Nothing is highlighted (the break suppresses selection), nothing is undoable, and the damage shows up as a missing or extra trace after an 8-second run. A keyboard user cannot enable an analysis at all.
- **Fix.** Three parts, in Tk 8.6 terms. (a) Narrow the hazard without a new sentence: set the three checkbox columns to `-anchor center` in build_pane:850 and, in pane_click, toggle only when the click is within ~12 px of the cell's horizontal centre (from `$tv bbox $item $col`); otherwise return 0 and let the class binding select the row. ~6 lines, zero new labels. (b) Give it a keyboard: an instance `bind $pf.tv <Key-space>` that toggles the focus row's flag (Enable for the Analyses pane) — which key handles Outputs' two columns is a user-visible choice, so file a rule debt rather than inventing `p`/`s`. (c) Do NOT try to put real check widgets in the cells: a ttk::treeview value column cannot hold an image or a widget in Tk 8.6 (only the #0 tree column takes `-image`), so the glyph is the toolkit ceiling, not laziness — say so in the source instead of leaving it looking like a shortcut.
- **Risk.** Zero test movement measured: no suite drives pane_click by coordinate, asserts a column `-anchor`, or calls toggle_flag. The keyboard key choice and any menu twin are new user-facing decisions — ratification needed (owed.sh add rule). Centring the three columns is a visible change to the panes the user has been looking at since 2026-07-21; worth naming in the same ruling.
- **ADE-L.** ADE-L renders Plot and Save as real check-boxes in the Outputs table and keeps row selection separate from the toggle, so a click that means "select this row" cannot mean "change what gets saved".

## [MAJOR] Two of the four comboboxes render on stock #d9d9d9 because the white field was set with `configure` and readonly comboboxes are painted by the state MAP — the exact defect the Calculator already measured and fixed

- **id** `readonly-combobox-renders-stock-grey`  ·  **effort** trivial
- **Evidence.** src/ase_window.tcl:194 `ttk::style configure Ase.TCombobox -fieldbackground [ase::palette table]`. Read live: `ttk::style configure Ase.TCombobox -fieldbackground` = #ffffff, `ttk::style map Ase.TCombobox -fieldbackground` = EMPTY, inherited `ttk::style map TCombobox -fieldbackground` = `readonly #d9d9d9 disabled #d9d9d9`. `$w.use state` = readonly (simulators, :4550) and `$w.casemode state` = readonly (row editor, :5019); the other two (:4366 Design, :6334 Save State) are normal. Pixels: simulators.png (400,245) and (600,245) = srgb(217,217,217) = #d9d9d9, between the treeview above it at (400,100) = 255,255,255 and the panel below at (20,355) = 242,242,242. simdlg_editor.png (300,72) — the Case combobox — = 217,217,217, directly under the Program entry at (300,40) = 255,255,255. tests/headless/test_calc_skeleton.tcl:1270-1282 records this identical measurement for the Calculator, verbatim: "the widget rendered the stock #d9d9d9 (sampled from the live window: (217,217,217) … Assert the map, for both styles, on the state the widgets are in." The lesson was never carried across to ASE-L.
- **Why it hurts.** The Simulators dialog's whole point is the "Use this one:" chooser, and it is the one control in that window painted stock Tk grey — it reads as disabled, in a dialog where the row above it is also greyed for being genuinely read-only. Same class, same app, two field colours depending on which dialog you opened.
- **Fix.** One line in ase::theme after :194 — `ttk::style map Ase.TCombobox -fieldbackground [list readonly [ase::palette table] disabled [ase::palette disabledbg]]` — and, because a readonly combobox also maps -foreground, add the matching `-foreground` map from `fieldfg`/`disabledfg`. Both colours already exist in the locked palette; nothing is invented.
- **Risk.** No suite reads Ase.TCombobox. Uses only palette values the user already locked, so no ratification. The Calculator's Calc.*.TCombobox styles are separate and untouched.
- **ADE-L.** ADE-L is one toolkit end to end; a chooser field never changes colour because of which form it sits in.

## [MAJOR] `apply_theme`'s class switch omits Radiobutton, Listbox and every ttk container, so Choose Analyses shows four stock-grey pills and the Load State browser's headers and sashes are the #d9d9d9 the palette exists to escape

- **id** `apply-theme-misses-radiobutton-listbox-and-every-ttk-container`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** src/ase_window.tcl:224-252: the switch covers Toplevel/Frame/Labelframe/Menu/Button/Label/Checkbutton, Entry, Text, TCombobox, Treeview, Scrollbar — and nothing else. Live cget: `.chana.types.op` Radiobutton bg=grey80 font=TkDefaultFont, against `.chana.enable` Checkbutton bg=#f2f2f2 font=AseLabelFont 20 px below it; `.loadst.pw.lib.h` TLabel bg={} font={} (falls to ttk defaults); `.loadst.pw.lib.lb` Listbox selectbackground=grey70 where the panes' Ase.Treeview selected background is the locked #4a6984. Style lookups: TFrame/TLabel/TScrollbar/TPanedwindow -background = #d9d9d9, -font = TkDefaultFont (DejaVu Sans 10). Pixels: analyses.png row y=10 scans F2F2F2×12, CCCCCC×48, F2F2F2×8, CCCCCC×47, F2F2F2×8, CCCCCC×47, F2F2F2×8, CCCCCC×59, F2F2F2×12 — four grey80 rectangles whose widths (48/47/47/59) are exactly the four radiobuttons' measured reqwidths. loadstate.png (60,8),(250,8),(460,8) and both sashes (168,150),(352,150) = srgb(217,217,217), against (20,340) = 242,242,242 in the same dialog. The spec says the palette exists because "stock = #d9d9d9 grey" and that there is "No widget left on Tk defaults" (doc/claude/specs/ase_l.md:873,880).
- **Why it hurts.** Choose Analyses is the dialog a designer opens before every simulation, and it is the least finished-looking surface in the program — four grey pills in a different font from the checkbox under them. Load State's column headers are in DejaVu regular while its own status line is Nimbus bold, and its selection is grey where every other ASE list selects in the locked blue.
- **Fix.** Add arms to the :227 switch — `Radiobutton` alongside the Checkbutton group; a `Listbox` arm setting `-background table -font AseEntryFont -selectbackground selectbg -selectforeground selectfg`; `TLabel/TFrame/TScrollbar/TPanedwindow` arms applying `-style Ase.<class>` — and configure those four Ase.* styles in ase::theme with `-background [ase::palette panel]` (and `-font AseLabelFont` on Ase.TLabel). All values come from the existing locked palette.
- **Risk.** Nothing to ratify (locked palette values only). Test movement: none found — grep for d9d9d9/grey80/grey70 in tests/headless hits only the Calculator suites, which pin their OWN widgets. Worth adding ASE checks modelled on test_calc_skeleton S14/S15/S20, which exist precisely because "it can revert to stock grey80 … with every other check green".
- **ADE-L.** ADE-L has one control look; a radio group and a check-box in the same form cannot come from different eras.

## [MAJOR] The same job is done both ways in the same file: six classic scrollbars and one ttk::scrollbar, twenty-nine classic labels and one ttk::label — 3D relief butted against flat ttk in every screenshot

- **id** `six-classic-scrollbars-and-one-ttk-scrollbar-in-one-file`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** src/ase_window.tcl scrollbar constructors: classic at :852, :1414, :3898, :4548, :5294, :7012; ttk::scrollbar at :6123 — one file, one role, two toolkits. Labels: 29 classic `label` constructors, one `ttk::label` (:6119). Live class census of the main window: 64 widgets, of which ttk = 3 (Treeview); the rest classic (Button 8, Entry 1, Frame 4, Label 10, Labelframe 3, Scrollbar 3, Menu 31). Measured chrome: classic scrollbar relief=sunken width=11 reqwidth=13 against `ttk::style lookup TScrollbar -width` = 12; labelframe relief=groove bd=2; strip buttons relief=raised bd=1 at 68x27. Visible in ase_main.png: a flat white ttk table with a flat header strip, inside a 3D groove labelframe, with a sunken 3D arrow scrollbar down its right edge — and loadstate.png shows the same role rendered flat. Whole-file constructor census: ttk 12, classic 110. For comparison, in the same tree: library_manager.tcl ttk 66 / classic 4, copy_form.tcl 47/2, save_as_form.tcl 15/1, rdw.tcl 0 ttk (all `::button`/`::label`), ciw.tcl 0 ttk.
- **Why it hurts.** There is no single answer to "what does a control look like here", so nothing reads as one program. The designer moves between ASE-L, RDW (pure classic) and the Library Manager (pure ttk) in one task and gets three different applications.
- **Fix.** Commit to the idiom named in slick_move and convert the six classic scrollbars to `ttk::scrollbar` (they sit beside ttk::treeviews; the flat trough is the match), and the one stray `ttk::label` at :6119 to a classic `label` so the browser's headers inherit AseLabelFont like every other ASE label. Layout delta measured at 1 px (reqwidth 13 → 12).
- **Risk.** No ASE suite references a scrollbar path (`grep '\.sb\b' tests/headless/test_ase*.tcl` — no hits) or the loadst header. Do NOT extend this to buttons/entries/labels: `$top.status.stat cget -background` (Green/orange/red status light) is asserted in test_ase_persist:650, test_ase_interact:421 and test_ase_window:2546/2557/2892/3236, and `$top.tb.temp cget -background/-font` in test_ase_window:555-556 — ttk widgets reject `-background`, so a wholesale ttk migration would move those and lose the light.
- **ADE-L.** n/a — a single-toolkit application cannot have this defect.

## [MAJOR] OK is 49 px at the far left and Cancel 74 px at the far right with a ~230 px gulf between them, no dialog has a default button, and three different button vocabularies are in play

- **id** `button-row-split-to-opposite-edges-with-no-default-button`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** src/ase_window.tcl:1681-1694 `dialog_buttons` packs `proceed -side left` and `cancel -side right` into a frame gridded `-sticky we`. Measured reqwidths, identical in every scaffold dialog: proceed 49, cancel 74, btns frame 143 — inside toplevels of 411 (output editor), 456 (row editor), 339 (Save All), 541 (Load State). Seen in outputedit.png, saveall.png, analyses.png, simdlg_editor.png, loadstate.png. `cget -default` is `disabled` on every Button in all eight dialogs probed — there is no default ring anywhere in ASE-L. Vocabularies: OK/Cancel (scaffold), Select/Close (rsel, :3862-3866), Add…/Edit…/Remove … Close (simulators, :4574-4581). The tree's own precedent is the opposite: rdw.tcl:7131-7135 builds `ok`/`cancel` at `-width 8` and packs `cancel ok -side right -padx 4` — adjacent, equal width, right-aligned.
- **Why it hurts.** The two buttons of a two-button decision are a hand-movement apart, they are visibly different sizes, and the eye has to travel the width of the dialog to find the second one. Return does not commit in six of the eight dialogs (see the next finding), so there is no keyboard escape from the trip either.
- **Fix.** In `dialog_buttons`: create both with `-width 8`, `pack $w.btns.cancel -side right -padx 5` then `pack $w.btns.proceed -side right -padx 5` (adjacent, right-aligned, matching RDW), set `-default active` on proceed, and add `bind $w <Return> $okcmd` centrally so every scaffold dialog commits on Return by construction. Bring rsel and simulators into the same shape. Whether the cluster sits right (RDW's shape) or contiguous-left (the Cadence form banner, which puts OK/Cancel/Apply together at the left and only Help at the right) is a one-line ruling for the user.
- **Risk.** Widget paths do not move, so the 14 suites that drive `.btns.proceed`/`.btns.cancel`/`.b.ok` by path and `invoke` keep passing unchanged. test_ase_dialogs.tcl:64 reads `grid info` on a dialog — check that row. Adding a default ring changes the pixel size of the OK button (Tk reserves the ring), which can shift a dialog's reqheight by ~4 px; nothing asserts dialog geometry. Left-vs-right cluster is a user decision.
- **ADE-L.** Every Cadence form carries the same fixed banner: the action cluster contiguous at one end and Help alone at the other — never one button per edge, never two sizes.

## [MAJOR] Load State focuses the View list on purpose and then leaves Return and double-click dead there; the Simulators list does not open Edit on double-click

- **id** `return-and-double-click-dead-where-the-code-puts-the-focus`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** Live: `bind .ase4.loadst <Return>` = '' ; `bind .ase4.loadst.pw.view.lb` = '' (no bindings at all); `bind .ase4.loadst.pw.view.lb <Double-1>` = '' ; the Listbox class has no `<Key-Return>`. And src/ase_window.tcl:6190 `loadst_default_to_session` deliberately does `catch {focus $w.pw.view.lb}` with the comment "so the pick needs no mouse trip". Live: `bind .ase4.simdlg <Return>` = '' and `bind .ase4.simdlg.tv <Double-1>` = '' — the only `<Double-1>` bindings in the file are :863 (panes) and :3902 (rsel). Return exists on the toplevel in only 4 places (:410, :1431, :4054, :5722); everywhere else it is bound per-entry (:1704-5, :1755-6, :1835-6, :4142, :4249-50, :4369, :5058-9, :5363, :6347), so it is dead whenever focus is on a listbox, treeview, checkbutton, combobox or button. doc/claude/specs/ase_l.md:1088 states the rule this misses: "Return = proceed".
- **Why it hurts.** Load State is the dialog a designer opens to switch between the states of the cell they are simulating — several times an hour. The code walks their focus to the View list, and then the two gestures every list in every application answers to do nothing; they have to mouse back to an OK button 300 px away. Double-clicking a simulator row to edit it is the same dead end.
- **Fix.** Bind `<Return>` on the dialog TOPLEVEL in dialog_buttons (see previous finding) so it is inherited by construction — a child's bindtags include its toplevel, the same mechanism `bind_dialog_esc` (:1658) already relies on. For the two non-scaffold dialogs, add `bind $w <Return>` to the same command as OK/Select, and `<Double-1>` on the Load State View listbox → `load_state_ok`, on the Simulators treeview → `simdlg_edit`.
- **Risk.** Adding `<Return>` to loadstate is exercised by test_ase_dialogs.tcl G9-G9d (lines 726-824), which currently invoke `.b.ok` directly and will keep passing. `send_return` helpers exist in that suite; a new Return path may want a check added rather than moved. No new user-facing text.
- **ADE-L.** Double-click commits in every Cadence lib/cell/view browser, and Return is the form's OK.

## [MINOR] ESC and Cancel agree; the title-bar X does not — it bare-destroys eight of the ten dialogs and leaves their per-window records set

- **id** `wm-close-bypasses-the-cancel-path-on-eight-of-ten-dialogs`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** `grep -n 'wm protocol' src/ase_window.tcl` returns exactly four hits: :321 (the session toplevel), :3874 (rsel), :6001 (the one-line registrar) and :5732 (its only caller, save_all_dialog). Live: `wm protocol $w WM_DELETE_WINDOW` is '' for simulators, simdlg_editor, loadstate, addvar, output editor and choose-analyses. The shared scaffold `dialog_frame` (:1665) registers nothing, while :1651-1656 documents the requirement it does not meet — ESC "dismisses through the SAME command as its Cancel/Close button — never a bare destroy that would leak the per-window records (edrow/edchk/dlg)". The source at :5724-5731 already states the measurement and names the gap: issue 0651, filed, still open. Also measured: `wm transient` is '' for ALL ten dialogs (only :399 and :1403, the two modal helpers, set it) — which is why every large dialog lands at +0+0 instead of over its parent, and why an ASE dialog can sink behind the ASE window.
- **Why it hurts.** Closing a dialog with the window button — the most natural gesture on a floating window — takes a different code path from ESC and from Cancel, so state a designer thinks they discarded is still set, and the next open can come back pre-dirtied. The missing `transient` costs them a window hunt every time a dialog opens at the corner of a 1920-wide screen.
- **Fix.** Move `ase::ui::dialog_close_protocol $w $cancelcmd` into `dialog_buttons` (which already knows the cancel command and already wires ESC there), and add `catch {wm transient $w $parent}` plus a centre-on-parent in `dialog_frame`. Register the protocol explicitly in the four non-scaffold dialogs.
- **Risk.** 0651's own text warns this "would change WM-close semantics for ~8 dialogs at once, none of them covered by a test" — so land it with a check per dialog. `wm transient` changes stacking and, under a WM, placement: it will move the recorded +0+0 geometries, so any suite asserting dialog geometry must be re-baselined (none found in tests/headless/test_ase*.tcl). Adding transient also makes dialogs iconify with the parent — a behaviour change worth naming to the user.
- **ADE-L.** Cadence forms close through their own Cancel path from the window button, and every form is transient over the window that spawned it.

## [MINOR] ASE-L's Load State browser is the tree's THIRD copy of the same Library/Cell/View widget, and the only one whose selection is grey and whose buttons are classic

- **id** `third-copy-of-the-lib-cell-view-browser`  ·  **effort** large
- **Evidence.** src/ase_window.tcl:6118-6131 and src/save_as_form.tcl:232-244 are the same block down to the loop header `foreach {col title} {lib Library cell Cell view View}`, the same `ttk::frame`/`ttk::label -padding {4 2}`/`listbox -exportselection 0 -activestyle dotbox`/`ttk::scrollbar` construction, and the same grid/rowconfigure/columnconfigure/`$w.pw add $f -weight 1`. src/library_manager.tcl:101-115 is the third, built on `ttk::treeview -show tree` with per-row bold tags for revision-controlled cells, a sunken ttk status label and a full ttk button bar. The three differ where it shows: ASE's listbox selection is grey70 (measured) against the locked #4a6984 the panes use; ASE's buttons are classic OK/Cancel split to the edges while save_as_form's is a single ttk Cancel and library_manager's is a five-control ttk bar.
- **Why it hurts.** The same gesture — pick a library, a cell, a view — looks and behaves three different ways inside one application, and a fix to any of them (bold for tracked cells, type-to-filter, double-click to commit) reaches only a third of the users of it. ASE-L got the least capable of the three.
- **Fix.** Extract one browser — the library_manager treeview version, which is the most capable and already carries per-row tags — into a shared `lcvbrowse::` namespace (a `build $parent` returning the three widget paths, plus `selection`/`fill` accessors), and have library_manager, savebrowse and ASE's load_state_dialog all call it. Migrate ASE last, since it is the one with the test coverage.
- **Risk.** The heaviest test movement in this report: test_ase_dialogs.tcl G9/G9a-d (lines 726-824) and GE11 (1240-1243) drive the ASE browser through listbox APIs directly — `$lb get 0 end`, `$lb size`, `$lb selection clear`, plus `ase::ui::lb_sel` / `lb_select_value` — roughly 20 checks that must be rewritten against the treeview API or against widened helpers. test_save_as_form.tcl and test_lib_manager_bold.tcl / test_lib_manager_history.tcl cover the other two copies. Do this last, behind the cheap wins.
- **ADE-L.** ADE-L reaches the ONE Library Browser from every form that needs a cellview — it is the same widget, with the same keys and the same look, wherever it appears.

# menus-ia  (9 findings)

**Headline.** ASE-L wears Cadence ADE-L's nine-menu bar over a third of ADE-L's contents — the first menu is a greyed "(placeholder)", two menus hold exactly one entry each, the most-used operations (Add Output, Delete) have no menubar door at all, and the whole window has zero keyboard accelerators against the sibling waveform viewer's eleven.

**The one slick move.** Give ASE-L eight accelerators — Ctrl+R Netlist and Run, Ctrl+Shift+R Run, Ctrl+Period Stop, Ctrl+L Log, Ctrl+S Save, Ctrl+O Load State, Ctrl+W Close, Ctrl+4 Direct Plot (that one already exists) — bound on an `AseL` bindtag exactly as `wave_viewer.tcl:14902` binds `WaveViewer`, and print each one in its menu entry's `-accelerator`. One change, three payoffs: the run loop stops being a mouse trip four menus deep; the menubar turns from a place you hunt into the legend that teaches the keys; and the eight mute glyphs in the action strip finally acquire names the user can read off the menu beside them. It is small (one proc, ~30 lines), it needs no C, no csv row and no palette change, and no test in the tree reads `-accelerator` on an ASE-L entry — measured: `grep -c accelerator` is 0 in `src/ase_window.tcl`, 11 in `src/wave_viewer.tcl`, 115 in `src/xschem.tcl`.

## [CRITICAL] Zero keyboard accelerators in the window you run simulations from — and the stated reason is measurably false

- **id** `no-accelerators`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** grep -c '\-accelerator': src/ase_window.tcl = 0, src/wave_viewer.tcl = 13 (11 on menu entries), src/xschem.tcl = 115, src/rdw.tcl = 1. No `bind $top` of any kind exists in ase_window.tcl (grep 'bind \$top' returns nothing outside the temperature entry's two binds at :729-730). src/ase_window.tcl:633 states the reason: "a KEY CHORD would need a csv row AND an action_registry[] entry in callback.c". That is false for this window: src/wave_viewer.tcl:14902 binds Ctrl+N/W/Q/C/V on a plain Tk bindtag `WaveViewer` with no csv row and no C entry, and src/cadence_style_rc:311 already binds `<Control-Key-4>` straight to `ase::direct_plot_for_current` from an rc file. The csv+callback.c requirement applies to chords on the schematic CANVAS, not to a bind on ASE-L's own toplevel.
- **Why it hurts.** Netlist and Run, Run, Stop and Log are the four gestures of every simulation loop, done dozens of times a day. All four sit under Simulation, the seventh cascade from the left, three of them reachable only as unlabelled glyphs (N&>, >, !) on the action strip. In ADE-L a designer runs from the keyboard or from a bindkey; here every run costs a menu trip or a glyph the tooltip has to explain. And the one chord that DOES exist — Ctrl+4 for Direct Plot, live in the shipped cadence profile — is invisible: Results > Direct Plot carries no -accelerator, so ASE-L's own menu fails to teach ASE-L's own key.
- **Fix.** Add `ase::ui::install_bindings` creating an `AseL` bindtag on the toplevel (the wave_viewer.tcl:14895-14922 pattern verbatim, each script ending in `break`), then set -accelerator on the matching entries so the menu prints them: Simulation > Netlist and Run = Ctrl+R, Run = Ctrl+Shift+R, Stop = Ctrl+Period, Log = Ctrl+L; Session > Save = Ctrl+S, Load State = Ctrl+O, Close = Ctrl+W, Design Window = Ctrl+D; Results > Direct Plot = Ctrl+4 (LABEL ONLY — the chord exists already at src/cadence_style_rc:311; do not re-bind it); Tools > Waveform Viewer = Ctrl+Shift+W. Avoid Ctrl+V, Ctrl+A and Ctrl+C: Tk's Entry and Treeview class bindings own those and the temperature entry is in this window. No collision with the schematic canvas is possible — a different toplevel has the focus.
- **Risk.** The chord ASSIGNMENTS are user-visible decisions and need a ruling (Ctrl+Period for Stop and Ctrl+W for Close are the two I would ask about — the waveform viewer uses Ctrl+W for close TAB and Ctrl+Q for close WINDOW, so Ctrl+W here is coherent only because ASE-L has no tabs). No test breaks: grep for 'accelerator' across tests/headless/test_ase*.tcl and test_results_dialog.tcl returns nothing, and every ASE menu check reads -label, -command or -state only (test_ase_window.tcl:571-598, test_results_dialog.tcl:545-555).

## [CRITICAL] A 28 KB ASE-L tutorial ships and installs, and no menu in the program opens it

- **id** `no-help-cascade`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** doc/ase_l_tutorial.html exists — 28,537 bytes, <title>ASE-L Tutorial — Analog Simulation Environment for xschem</title>, 33 h2/h3 sections — and doc/Makefile:9 installs `*.html` into $(XDOCDIR). `grep -rn ase_l_tutorial --include=*.tcl --include=*.c .` matches ONLY tests/headless/test_wave_grid.tcl:463,469. Nothing in src/ opens it. ase_root.png: ASE-L's menubar ends at Tools; the schematic window directly below it in the same screenshot ends at Help. src/xschem.tcl:17861-17870 is that Help menu — Help, Keys, Show Keybindings, Keybindings (from table), Command palette, About XSCHEM. doc/waveform_viewer_guide.html is in the same position: shipped, installed, referenced only by tests.
- **Why it hurts.** A designer who wants to know what `N&>` does, what Save All actually saves, or how Direct Plot picks a net has documentation written for exactly that — and no path from the tool to it. They ask a colleague or read the source. Inside one application the schematic window teaches its keys six ways and ASE-L teaches nothing, which reads as "this window is not finished" every time someone looks for help and finds nine menus none of which is Help.
- **Fix.** Add a Help cascade after Tools with three entries: `ASE-L Tutorial` opening doc/ase_l_tutorial.html by the existing in-tree/installed fallback idiom at src/xschem.tcl:11787-11789 (`execute 0 xdg-open file://$XSCHEM_SHAREDIR/../doc/ase_l_tutorial.html`, falling back to .../doc/xschem/...); `Keys` showing the accelerator table once the chords above exist; `About ASE-L` naming the XSCHEM version, the simulator in force (ase::sim_label) and the state file path. Do the same for the waveform viewer and doc/waveform_viewer_guide.html — same defect, same fix.
- **Risk.** Adding a tenth cascade moves test_ase_window.tcl:571 (W1m asserts the exact list {Launch Session Setup Analyses Variables Outputs Simulation Results Tools}) — one check, one suite, and it moves anyway if Launch goes. The About text is new user-facing copy and needs ratification; the two doc-opening entries reuse an existing sentence-free mechanism and do not.

## [CRITICAL] The menubar's foreground is the only part of ASE-L the palette does not own — under dark_gui_colorscheme it is white on #f2f2f2

- **id** `menubar-fg-unthemed`  ·  **effort** trivial
- **Evidence.** src/ase_window.tcl:493 creates the menubar with no colour options at all (`menu $top.mb -tearoff 0`); ase::ui::apply_theme's Menu arm at :227-231 sets -background and -font and NEVER -foreground/-activeforeground/-disabledforeground. src/xschem.tcl:19107-19110 (the dark branch, selectable by `set dark_gui_colorscheme 1`, documented at src/xschemrc:296) does `option add *foreground white startupFile`. MEASURED live on :99 with that option branch reproduced: `MENUBAR fg=white bg=#f2f2f2 afg=white dfg=grey50 font=AseLabelFont` / `SUBMENU fg=white bg=#f2f2f2` — contrast ratio ~1.07. src/calculator.tcl:502-509 diagnoses this exact bug in a code comment and fixes it for the Calculator ("which the browser's menubar forgets to do"). doc/claude/specs/ase_l.md:880 requires "No widget left on Tk defaults". src/wave_viewer.tcl:18807-18809 has the same hole.
- **Why it hurts.** One rc line the shipped xschemrc documents makes ASE-L's entire menubar and every menu entry invisible at rest — nine cascade labels and 27 entries, readable only while the mouse is over them. It is the same class of defect as commit 19f8e351 ("the `note` colour was a contrast ratio of 1.45 and the user could not read it"), only worse by a factor of the whole remaining range.
- **Fix.** In the Menu arm of ase::ui::apply_theme, set -foreground and -activeforeground to [ase::theme fieldfg] and -disabledforeground to [ase::theme disabledfg], alongside the -background already there. THIS NEEDS NO PALETTE CHANGE AND NO NEW COLOUR: fieldfg #000000 and disabledfg #a3a3a3 are already two of the nine locked entries, and on the light scheme the values are byte-identical to what renders today — the fix is only that the palette, not the ambient option database, decides. Apply the same three lines to wave_viewer.tcl:18807.
- **Risk.** None to the locked palette (existing keys, no new values). No suite reads menu -foreground: test_ase_window.tcl's theme checks read $top.body.vars -foreground (:551) and the temperature entry's -background/-font (:554-556). Worth one new check pinning the three colours so it cannot rot back.

## [MAJOR] Adding or deleting an output has no menubar path — the Outputs menu is three entries, none of which edits an output

- **id** `outputs-no-add-door`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** src/ase_window.tcl:559-566 builds Outputs as exactly {To Be Saved > Select On Design}, {To Be Plotted > Select On Design}, {Save All…}. ase::ui::output_editor (the add/edit-by-expression dialog, defined :1801) is called from only three places, none of them the menubar: the `-->` strip button (:769), the outputs pane's right-click Add… (:881), and a pane double-click (:966). ase::ui::delete_selection is called from the `X` strip button (:771) and the three pane context menus (:885) — no menubar entry anywhere. Confirmed structurally by the source's own tip table: ase::ui::strip_tips (:5592) gives five of the eight strip buttons a COMPOSED MENU PATH as its tooltip and gives `var`, `out`, `del` a bare label instead — because those three have no menu path to compose.
- **Why it hurts.** Typing an output expression is the single most common ASE-L edit after picking analyses, and deleting a row is the second. Both are reachable only by a right-click on the right pane or by decoding an unlabelled `-->` / `X` glyph (ase_main.png, right strip). A Cadence user opens Outputs looking for Setup… — ADE-L's own first entry there — finds three entries about saving and plotting, and concludes ASE-L cannot add an output by hand. Discoverability is the whole job of a menubar and this one hides the operation the window exists to perform.
- **Fix.** Outputs becomes: `Setup…` (ADE-L's own label, wired to ase::ui::output_editor $key -1), `Edit…` (edit_output_first), `Delete` (delete_selection scoped to the outs pane), separator, To Be Saved >, To Be Plotted >, separator, Save All…. NOTE the scoping trap: ase::ui::delete_selection (:989) scans ALL THREE panes for a selection, so a menubar Delete under Outputs would happily delete a selected VARIABLES row. Either give it a pane argument or grey the entry from a -postcommand unless that pane holds the selection.
- **Risk.** No suite pins the Outputs entry list — test_ase_window.tcl:815-834 (W1r) finds the Save All entry by -command and reads the cascade label from the constants, and test_ase_interact.tcl:196/308/321/352 and test_ase_persist.tcl:614 invoke `{Select On Design}` by label, so additions are safe and renames are not. The three new labels are ADE-L's words, not invented ones, but they are still user-facing copy and want a ruling.

## [MAJOR] The leftmost menu in the menubar is permanently greyed and its only entry is literally "(placeholder)"

- **id** `launch-dead-menu`  ·  **effort** trivial
- **Evidence.** src/ase_window.tcl:496-500: `$top.mb.launch add command -label {(placeholder)} -state disabled` then `add cascade -label Launch ... -state disabled`. doc/claude/specs/ase_l.md:1017 — "**Launch** — placeholder menu, ignore for now." Visible in ase_root.png: `Launch` renders grey at the far left of the ASE-L menubar, immediately left of Session, while every other cascade is black. In Cadence, Launch is where ADE-L jumps to ADE XL / Assembler / Explorer; ASE-L has no second environment to jump to, so the slot has no possible content today. Nothing but test_ase_window.tcl:571-572 depends on it.
- **Why it hurts.** It is the first thing the eye hits in the menubar of a window a designer opens twenty times a day, and it is dead. It trains the user to skip the left edge of the bar, costs one of nine top-level slots, and is the single clearest "unfinished" tell in ASE-L — the one thing in this window that says out loud that it is a placeholder. Menubar real estate at the far left is the most valuable in the window; ADE-L parity is a reason to keep the ORDER of the menus that exist, not to reserve a slot for a product that does not.
- **Fix.** Delete the cascade until there is something to launch. Two other readings are available and both are the user's to pick: (b) keep the slot and fill it with what ASE-L can genuinely start — Library Manager, Command Window (CIW), Schematic Editor — which is a different meaning from Cadence's; (c) keep it greyed and accept the cost. Recommend (a) and file the ADE-XL slot as a note in the spec rather than as pixels on the bar.
- **Risk.** USER RULING REQUIRED — removing a visible menu is a decision, and the spec fixes the v2 tree. Test impact is exactly two checks in one suite: test_ase_window.tcl:571 (the 9-cascade list) and :572 (`W1m Launch cascade disabled`). Nothing else in tests/headless greps `Launch` against ASE-L's bar (the other 12 hits are the schematic's `Tools > Launch ASE-L`, a different menu).

## [MAJOR] 3 of 27 menu entries can ever grey out; Stop is black with nothing running, and its refusal is printed in a different window

- **id** `no-menu-state`  ·  **effort** medium
- **Evidence.** `grep -n postcommand src/ase_window.tcl` finds exactly one live one, at :646 on the Results > Annotate submenu (ase::ui::annot_menu_sync). Every other entry is built enabled and stays enabled. ase::ui::do_stop (:6820-ish) with no run in flight does `::ase::echo "ase: no simulation running for this session"` and returns; edit_variable_first/edit_output_first/edit_analysis_first (:3923-3971) each echo "ase: nothing selected". ase::echo -> xschem::notify_safe (src/xschem.tcl:17499) -> the status-bar sink at src/ciw.tcl:132-139, which writes `[xschem get top_path].statusbar.12` — the SCHEMATIC window's bar. ASE-L's own status bar (ase::ui::refresh_status, :6743-6756) carries only win# | Status | T= | Simulator | State and is never a notice sink; ase_main.png shows it reading `4 | Status: Ready | T=27 C | Simulator: ngspice-v50 | State: ngspice_state1`.
- **Why it hurts.** The menubar cannot answer "what can I do right now?" — Stop looks identical whether a run is in flight or not, Results > Select… and Direct Plot look identical with no results loaded, Edit… looks identical with nothing selected. The user clicks, nothing visibly happens, and the sentence explaining why lands in the schematic window's status bar, the CIW and the log — three places, none of them the window they just clicked in. On a two-monitor bench the schematic is often on the other screen. ADE-L greys Stop; that greying IS the feedback.
- **Fix.** Give Session, Analyses, Variables, Outputs, Simulation and Results a -postcommand that greys by predicate: Stop by `ase::run_in_flight`, Run/Netlist by whether a design is bound, Results > Select…/Direct Plot/Annotate by `ase::has_results` (the predicate annot_menu_sync already uses at :2634), every Edit…/Delete by whether that pane holds a selection. Separately, and cheaply: make ASE-L's own `.status.stat` a sink for `ase::echo` so a refusal is legible in the window that refused.
- **Risk.** The greying is behaviour change on entries several suites invoke unconditionally — test_ase_interact.tcl:412 and test_ase_persist.tcl:642 invoke `{Netlist and Run}`, :694 `{Direct Plot}`, test_ase_dialogs.tcl:539 `Model Files…`, test_ase_simdlg_0937.tcl `Simulators…` ×14 — a Tk menu `invoke` on a disabled entry is a silent no-op, so any predicate that is false under a headless fixture turns a green suite red with no error. Land it predicate by predicate, and check each suite's fixture state before greying the entry it drives.

## [MAJOR] Nine cascades carrying 26 live entries — two of them hold exactly one — because ADE-L's menu BAR was copied without ADE-L's menu CONTENTS

- **id** `menus-too-thin-not-too-many`  ·  **effort** medium
- **Evidence.** Counted off src/ase_window.tcl:493-700: Launch 1 (dead), Session 4, Setup 3, Analyses 1, Variables 1, Outputs 3, Simulation 7, Results 5, Tools 2 = 26 live leaves under 9 cascades, i.e. 2.9 entries per menu; Analyses and Variables are single-entry menus ("Choose…" at :539, "Edit…" at :547). In ADE-L the same nine menus carry 60-odd entries: Analyses = Choose/Delete/Disable/Enable, Variables = Edit/Copy From Cellview/Copy To Cellview/Delete/Delete All, Outputs = Setup/Save All/To Be Saved/To Be Plotted/Delete/Delete All. Every one of those ASE-L operations already EXISTS and is already wired — delete_selection (:989), toggle_flag …analyses enabled (:940), add_variable_dialog, edit_variable_first, edit_analysis_first, output_editor — but only to right-click context menus (:866-887) and the eight glyph buttons.
- **Why it hurts.** A single-entry menu is a click that buys one choice; two of them means two of the nine slots on the bar teach the user nothing about what the window can do. The read is backwards from the fix, though: the answer is not fewer menus — the taxonomy is the benchmark and it is the Cadence user's muscle memory — it is that ASE-L already implements Enable/Disable/Delete/Add and hides them behind a right-click nobody discovers. A menubar's job is to be the complete inventory of the window's operations; this one lists roughly half.
- **Fix.** Keep the ADE-L taxonomy and FILL it. Proposed tree, entry by entry (NEW marked; accelerators from the no-accelerators finding):  Session: Design Window Ctrl+D | --- | Load State Ctrl+O, Save NEW Ctrl+S (wired to the already-written, currently-unreachable ase::ui::save_state), Save State (unchanged — it is the Save-As), Revert NEW (ase::ui::revert_state) | --- | Close Ctrl+W.  Setup: Design…, Model Files…, Simulators… (unchanged).  Analyses: Choose… Ctrl+Shift+A, Edit… NEW | --- | Enable NEW, Disable NEW, Delete NEW.  Variables: Add… NEW, Edit…, Delete NEW.  Outputs: Setup… NEW, Edit… NEW, Delete NEW | --- | To Be Saved >, To Be Plotted > | --- | Save All….  Simulation: Netlist >(Recreate, Display), Netlist and Run Ctrl+R, Run Ctrl+Shift+R, Stop Ctrl+Period | --- | Log Ctrl+L, Options….  Results: Select… | --- | Direct Plot Ctrl+4, Annotate >(3, unchanged).  Tools: Waveform Viewer Ctrl+Shift+W, Calculator.  Help NEW: ASE-L Tutorial, Keys, About ASE-L.  Launch: removed.  DO NOT add ADE-L's Variables > Copy From/To Cellview — ase_window.tcl:41 records that the harvest model was deliberately removed.
- **Risk.** Every NEW label is user-facing copy needing ratification, though all of them are ADE-L's own words. Rename nothing: the existing labels are invoked by string in test_ase_persist.tcl:568/614/642/694/713/820, test_ase_interact.tcl:70/196/308-376/412/482, test_ase_savestate_adopt.tcl:165, test_ase_dialogs.tcl:539/674/686/705, test_ase_dirty.tcl:213 and test_ase_simdlg_0937.tcl (14 invokes). Only two exact-list checks exist and both are on menus this proposal touches: test_ase_window.tcl:571 (cascade list — moves for Help and Launch) and test_results_dialog.tcl:550 (SEL389 pins Results as {"Select…" separator {Direct Plot} Annotate} — moves only if Results changes; this proposal leaves it alone). Additions elsewhere break nothing: those suites look entries up by label or by -command, never by index.

## [MINOR] An in-place Save and a Revert are written, work, and have zero callers in the UI

- **id** `save-revert-unreachable`  ·  **effort** small
