- **Evidence.** ase::ui::save_state (src/ase_window.tcl:6816) snapshots the viewer and calls ase::session_save, which writes the session's OWN path (src/ase.tcl, `ase::state_save [dict get $s path]`). ase::ui::revert_state (:6825) restores the last-saved state and repopulates. Measured across all of src/*.tcl: `ase::ui::save_state\b` and `ase::ui::revert_state` have 0 references outside their own definitions — the comment at :6813 says so out loud ("kept as plain scripting/test seams (W7 uses revert_state)"). test_ase_window.tcl:565 records the intent: "the Simulation tree; no Revert". The only save door is Session > Save State, which src/ase_window.tcl:6326 documents as "ALWAYS Save-As" — a three-field dialog (savestate.png) every single time.
- **Why it hurts.** Two of the four things anyone does to a document — save it where it already is, and throw away what I just did — are implemented, tested, and unreachable. Saving costs a modal and a Return keypress where a keystroke would do; discarding a bad edit costs Close > No > relaunch, and the window has a dirty marker and a save-on-quit prompt (ase::ui::close_request, :6813 area) precisely because the user is expected to care about that state.
- **Fix.** Session gains `Save` (Ctrl+S -> the existing ase::ui::save_state) above the existing `Save State`, and `Revert` (the existing ase::ui::revert_state) below it. No new machinery — both procs already exist and both are already exercised by tests. Keep `Save State` meaning Save-As exactly as it does today.
- **Risk.** USER RULING — `Save` next to `Save State` is a two-label distinction the user has to bless, and it is a deliberate departure from ADE-L (which has no plain in-place save). Changing what `Save State` DOES would break test_ase_dialogs.tcl:671-705 (G7 asserts the prefilled Save-As on invoking `{Save State}`) and test_ase_savestate_adopt.tcl:165 — so add beside it, never repoint it.

## [MINOR] "Design Window" and "Design…" are two entries in two different menus, and the wrong one is on the left

- **id** `design-window-vs-design`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** src/ase_window.tcl:505 puts `Design Window` first in Session (raise-or-open the schematic, ase::ui::design_window); :519 puts `Design…` first in Setup (choose Library/Cell/View — designdlg.png shows the three-combobox picker). ase_root.png: Session sits immediately left of Setup on the bar, so the entry that RAISES a window is the first Design-shaped thing a left-to-right reader meets and the entry that CHANGES the design is one menu further right. ADE-L has only the second of the two.
- **Why it hurts.** A user who wants to point ASE-L at a different cell opens Session first, reads "Design Window", picks it, and gets a schematic window raised instead of the picker they wanted. Two near-identical labels for two unrelated verbs, ordered so the rarer one is found first. Cheap to confuse, cheap to fix, and it costs a context switch to another window every time it happens.
- **Fix.** Rename Session's entry to name its verb rather than its noun — `Raise Schematic` or `Show Design Window` — leaving Setup > Design… as the only "Design" in the bar. Label choice is the user's.
- **Risk.** USER RULING (new copy) and the widest rename in this report: `Design Window` is invoked or asserted by string in tests/headless/ase_design_window.tcl, test_ase_window.tcl, test_ase_core.tcl, test_ase_final.tcl, test_ase_final_gf180.tcl, test_remap_verify.tcl and test_op_annot.tcl — seven suites. The spec calls the Session labels v2-fixed. Given that cost I would do this only if the user has actually been bitten; otherwise leave it and note it.


---

# task-flow  (12 findings)

**Headline.** The loop's two ends are both broken: the "Analysis Options…" form silently throws away everything you type into it before the deck is written, and the Outputs Value column stays empty even when the answer is already sitting on disk and ASE-L's own parser recovers all five numbers from it in one call.

**The one slick move.** Make the Outputs Value column show the numbers that already exist, whenever the window can see them — not only in the eight seconds after this session's own Run.

Right now the loop's payoff never arrives unless you are standing there when it does. I loaded the real bandgap bench with its completed run, its 69 MB raw file and its log full of answers, and all five Value cells were blank (`PROBE session results attr = ''`). Then I fed that same log to ASE-L's own `result_probe` and got every number back in one call: `VBG 1.177085e+00 ENN 0.000000e+00 START 1.803088e+00 Icc 2.895431e-05 TEMPERAT 2.700000e+01` — 5 of 5. Nothing needs inventing. `refresh_output_values` exists, `result_probe` works, the log path is one backend hook away; the entire defect is that src/ase_window.tcl:7246 is the function's only caller in the tree. Four lines lifted out of run_finished and called from ase::ui::open, rsel_commit and load_state_commit turn ASE-L from a form you fill in into a bench that remembers.

That is what "slick" means for a daily user. Open your testbench and the answers are there. Point Results > Select at yesterday's run and the column changes to match — which is what a person expects that menu item to do and what it conspicuously does not do today. It costs one small proc, no new user-facing sentence, no widget path moved, no ruling required beyond a staleness guard, and it makes every other window in the loop feel like it belongs to the same tool.

Runner-up, for the other end of the loop: in-place editing of the Analyses pane's Arguments cell. `step=10n stop=200u` is already sitting there in the table; double-click-to-edit-in-place would delete an entire dialog from the most repeated gesture in the tool. And one thing must be fixed before either, because it is not a polish item at all: the Analysis Options form (finding 1) accepts settings, displays them as if they were in force, and does not put them in the deck. Slickness on top of a window that misreports the simulation is the wrong order of work.

## [CRITICAL] Analysis Options… stores your keys, prints them in the Arguments column, and never puts them in the deck

- **id** `analysis-options-is-a-dead-form`  ·  **effort** medium
- **Evidence.** MEASURED live on the real bandgap state (probe run under DISPLAY=:99). I added the three keys a designer would actually type into chana_opt_tran.png's Name/Value grid and rendered the deck:
  PROBE analyses-after:  {type tran enabled 1 stop 200u step 10n uic 1 tstart 5u tmax 1n}
  PROBE deckline: |tran 10n 200u|
  PROBE uic-in-deck: 0     PROBE tstart-in-deck: 0
  PROBE arg_summary: step=10n stop=200u uic=1 tstart=5u tmax=1n
The emitter is src/ase.tcl:10966 `tran { lappend lines "tran [dict get $a step] [dict get $a stop]" }` — two fields, nothing else, and the same for dc (:10963) and ac (:10964). The source admits it at src/ase_window.tcl:4211-4212: "Extra keys round-trip through the state file and show in the Arguments summary … DECK emission of extra keys stays deferred (v1 limit, documented here)." chana_opt_tran.png shows the form with no warning text of any kind.
- **Why it hurts.** A bandgap with a start-up circuit is the canonical `uic` case, and this bench IS a bandgap. The designer opens Options…, types uic=1, presses OK, sees `step=10n stop=200u uic=1` in the Analyses pane's Arguments column, runs, and gets a run that did not use uic. Every displayed confirmation says it worked. This is not a missing feature, it is the window reporting a simulation setting that is not in force — a silently wrong answer, the most expensive class of defect in a simulation front end. Corroborating: across 105 committed .state files and 420 analysis rows in this tree, not one carries an extra key. Nobody has ever successfully used this dialog.
- **Fix.** Two honest options, and which one is chosen is the user's ruling.
(a) Emit them: extend the three switch arms in src/ase.tcl:10962-10966 to append the row's extra keys after the positional fields, per type, with a per-type whitelist of the spellings ngspice accepts (tran: uic, tstart, tmax; ac: the sweep word; dc: the second source triple). Risk is nil in-tree — no committed state file has an extra key, so tests/headless/test_ase_core.tcl's byte-exact D1 golden deck is unchanged.
(b) If emission is genuinely out of scope for now, the dialog must stop claiming: put one sentence at the top of the Analysis Options toplevel (src/ase_window.tcl:4213, above the treeview) saying these keys are recorded but not yet sent to the simulator, and strip them out of ase::ui::arg_summary so the Arguments column stops showing them as if they were live.
Doing neither is not an option; the current state is the one that misleads.
- **Risk.** Option (a): a new user-facing surface for a key set that has never been exercised — needs a ruling on WHICH spellings are legal per type, because accepting anything means handing arbitrary text to the simulator. tests/headless/test_ase_core.tcl D1/D2 assert deck text (unchanged for the committed fixtures). Option (b) mints a new user-facing sentence — needs ratification — and moves arg_summary output, which tests/headless/test_ase_dialogs.tcl and test_ase_persist.tcl read.
- **ADE-L.** ADE-L's Choosing Analyses form has no untyped escape hatch at all: every transient control (Stop Time, accuracy preset, Save Initial Transient Results / skipstart, Enabled) is a named typed field, and everything on the form reaches the netlist. There is nowhere in ADE-L to type a setting that the simulator will not see.

## [CRITICAL] The Outputs Value column only ever fills at the end of a run in this session; the numbers are on disk and the shipped parser recovers 5 of 5 from them

- **id** `value-column-empty-with-the-answer-on-disk`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** ase_main.png: the Outputs pane lists VBG, ENN, START, Icc, TEMPERAT with a Value column and all five cells blank, on a bench with a completed run whose raw file the same process had just read ("Raw file data read: /home/analog/.xschem/simulations/tb_bandgap_ase.raw, points=20502"). Live probe of the same window:
  PROBE outrow 0 = VBG {} ☑ ☑ {}   (…all five blank)
  PROBE session results attr = ''
logwin.png shows the numbers plainly: VBG = 1.177085e+00, START = 1.803088e+00, i(VCC) = 2.895431e-05, TEMPERAT = 2.700000e+01.
Cause, measured: `ase::ui::refresh_output_values` (src/ase_window.tcl:1600) has exactly ONE caller in the tree, src/ase_window.tcl:7246 inside run_finished. Neither Session > Load State, nor Results > Select (ase::ui::rsel_commit, src/ase_window.tcl:3549 — it never touches the Outputs pane), nor re-opening the window fills it, and src/ase_window.tcl:7244 says results are "display-only, never serialized to the state file".
And the recovery is one call away — measured:
  set res [[ase::backend_hook ngspice result_probe] $st $logtext]
  PROBE recovered-from-existing-log: VBG 1.177085e+00 ENN 0.000000e+00 START 1.803088e+00 Icc 2.895431e-05 TEMPERAT 2.700000e+01
  PROBE n = 5 of 5 outputs
- **Why it hurts.** This is the column the designer opened the window to read. Today the only way to see it is to re-run the simulation — 8.12 s on this bench, minutes on a real one — for numbers that were computed an hour ago and are still in <cell>_ase.log next to the state. Come back tomorrow, load the state, and ASE-L shows you a form with no answers in it. It also makes Results > Select feel broken: you deliberately choose a results file and the pane that shows results does not change.
- **Fix.** Extract the four lines of src/ase_window.tcl:7243-7247 into `ase::ui::refresh_results_from_disk $key`: resolve `[ase::backend_hook $sim log_file] $state`, read it if it exists, run `[ase::backend_hook $sim result_probe] $state $data`, `ase::session_setattr $key results` and call refresh_output_values. Then call it from three more places: the end of ase::ui::open (src/ase_window.tcl:276) after populate, the end of ase::ui::rsel_commit (:3613, on a non-refused select), and load_state_commit. run_finished keeps its in-memory path unchanged. Exactly the sequence the probe above ran, which returned 5/5.
- **Risk.** Values from an OLD log could be shown against a state edited since — the fix must not claim a number belongs to the current settings. Cheapest honest guard: only adopt the log's results when the log is newer than the state file, else leave blank as today. tests/headless/test_ase_window.tcl and test_ase_persist.tcl assert the Outputs pane's cell contents on open and would need a fixture-relative log; test_ase_result_case.tcl covers result_probe itself and is untouched. No new user-facing sentence.
- **ADE-L.** ADE-L evaluates the Outputs pane against whatever results database is currently selected. Load a state, point Results > Select at a results directory, and the Value column fills — no re-simulation. The pane is a view onto the selected result, not a side effect of having pressed Run in this session.

## [MAJOR] Four analysis types exist and no more; a fifth type in the state is dropped from the deck silently while the pane shows it enabled

- **id** `no-noise-analysis-and-silent-drop`  ·  **effort** large
- **Evidence.** src/ase_window.tcl:4104 `foreach t {op dc ac tran}` builds the radio set (four radios, visible in analyses.png), src/ase_window.tcl:4072-4079 `chana_fields` knows only those three field sets, and src/ase.tcl:10863 `set anorder {op dc ac tran}` is the emit loop. MEASURED: a state whose only analysis is `{type noise enabled 1 output v(VBG) source v1 points 10 start 1 stop 1meg}` renders a .control block containing only the five `print` lines —
  PROBE noise-in-deck: 0
  PROBE control-block: |.control| |set appendwrite| |print VBG| … |.endc|
— no analysis at all, no warning, exit would be 0, and `ase::ui::arg_summary` happily renders `output=v(VBG) source=v1 points=10 start=1 stop=1meg` in the pane beside a ticked Enable box.
- **Why it hurts.** Input-referred noise is the second or third measurement an analog designer takes on a bandgap or an op-amp, right after tran and ac. In ASE-L it cannot be reached at all: not from the dialog, not by hand-editing the state file, not by any route. And the failure mode of trying is the worst one available — a run that completes, writes a file and produces nothing, with the pane still showing the analysis as enabled.
- **Fix.** Two separable pieces. (1) The cheap correctness half, today: give the emit loop at src/ase.tcl:10863 a default arm that raises a clean error naming the unsupported type, so a state ASE-L cannot render refuses to run instead of running empty. (2) The feature half: add `noise` to the radio set and chana_fields ({output source points start stop}) and one deck arm `noise <output> <source> dec <points> <start> <stop>`, plus a result path — noise's answer is a spectrum, so it lands in the viewer, not the Value column. Per the memory rule on capability gating, the type list should come from what the registered simulator declares rather than being a second hardcoded list.
- **Risk.** Piece (1) is small and safe. Piece (2) adds a fifth radio at $w.types.noise — tests/headless/test_ase_dialogs.tcl G1/G2/GE4 drive $top.chana.types.tran by path and would need the new row added, not moved. A new analysis type is a spec change (doc/claude/specs/ase_l.md) and the field labels are new user-facing strings needing ratification.
- **ADE-L.** ADE-L's Choosing Analyses form lists tran, dc, ac, noise, xf, sens, dcmatch, stb, pz, sp, envlp, pss/pac/… — every analysis the attached simulator declares. Noise is a first-class row with Output/Input probe pickers.

## [MAJOR] Choose Analyses opens on the wrong type with nothing focused, and silently discards what you typed when you touch another radio

- **id** `choose-analyses-fights-you`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** All four measured live on the real bench (src/ase_window.tcl:4094-4146):
  PROBE preselected type: op        — the menu route hardcodes op (src/ase_window.tcl:4098 `if {$type eq {}} { set type op }`) even though this state has op+tran enabled and dc/ac disabled; op has NO fields (chana_fields returns {}), which is exactly what analyses.png shows: a 249x126 box of four radios and an Options button.
  PROBE focus-in-dialog: .ase4.chana   — focus is on the toplevel. No `focus` call exists anywhere in choose_analyses or chana_show; every OTHER editor in the file focuses its first entry (:1707, :1758, :1838, :4256, :4383, :5063).
  PROBE tran cursor after prefill = 4 (len 4) / PROBE selection present = 0 — the prefilled `200u` is not selected and the cursor sits at the end; there is no `selection range` call in the whole 7491-line file.
  PROBE after ac->tran round trip, stop = '200u'  <-- typing survived? 0 — I typed 500u, clicked the ac radio, clicked back, and 500u was gone. chana_show (:4126) destroys the fields and re-inserts from state on every radio press; the code calls this deliberate ("D4: in-form edits of the previous type are DISCARDED").
  PROBE dialog geometry = 382x180+1536+848, transient= — no `wm transient`, no `wm geometry`; the dialog landed in the far bottom-right corner of a 1920x1080 screen.
- **Why it hurts.** Changing a transient stop time is the single most repeated gesture in analog bench work, and via the menubar it costs SEVEN pointer gestures: Analyses > Choose… (2), cross the screen to the corner where the dialog landed, click the tran radio to make the fields exist at all (3), click into Stop because nothing is focused (4), clear four characters by hand (5), type (6), OK (7) — then N&> to run. Two of those seven exist only to undo the dialog's own defaults. And step 3 is a trap: if you were comparing against the ac settings on the way, your typing is gone with no warning and no message.
- **Fix.** Four small independent changes, none of which move a widget path: (1) src/ase_window.tcl:4098 — default `type` to the first ENABLED analysis in the state, falling back to op only when none is enabled. (2) At the end of chana_show (:4145) add `focus` on the first field and `$e selection range 0 end` so a prefilled value is replaced by typing, matching what :1707/:1758/:1838 already do. (3) In chana_show, before rebuilding, harvest the current fields and keep them in a per-type scratch dict so switching away and back restores what was typed (or, cheaper and still honest, refuse the switch with an in-dialog message when a field differs from state). (4) `wm transient $w $top` in ase::ui::dialog_frame (:1665) plus a geometry relative to the parent, so dialogs land beside the window that opened them.
- **Risk.** (1) changes which radio is lit on open — tests/headless/test_ase_dialogs.tcl G1 opens the dialog and cancels, and WF in test_ase_interact.tcl presses proceed immediately; a fixture whose enabled analysis is not op would now commit a different row on a bare OK, so those rows need reading. (3) reverses a decision recorded in the source as D4 ("deterministic, no hidden multi-type writes") — that was a deliberate call and reversing it needs ratification, though "keep the edit" and "warn before discarding" are both compatible with D4's intent. (4) touches every dialog in the file at once.
- **ADE-L.** ADE-L's Choosing Analyses form remembers the analysis you last worked on, opens with the field set of the selected type already showing, and switching type does not discard an unsubmitted edit without saying so. Its Stop Time field is a named, unit-bearing field you tab straight into.

## [MAJOR] Delete has no confirm, ASE-L has no undo, and Revert is not in any menu

- **id** `no-undo-no-confirm-no-revert`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** src/ase_window.tcl:990, the comment on delete_selection itself: "remove those rows from its state list in descending index order, commit, repopulate. No confirm anywhere in this path." The X strip button (:768) and the Delete entry in all three pane context menus (:885) call it. `grep -in undo src/ase_window.tcl` returns exactly one hit, and it is a comment about an unrelated gesture. `ase::session_revert` exists (src/ase.tcl:9475) and restores the last saved state, but the Session menu built at src/ase_window.tcl:502-518 contains only Design Window | Load State | Save State | Close — Revert is reachable only by typing Tcl into the CIW.
- **Why it hurts.** Click a row in the Analyses pane, click X, and your analysis set is gone: no dialog, no undo, no menu-visible way back. Same for the Design Variables pane, which on this bench holds the three parameters the whole bench is built around. The recovery that does exist (Load State, loadstate.png — a 541x363 three-pane library/cell/view browser) is a five-click content-import dialog, not an undo, and it silently costs every unsaved edit made since. A tool where a mis-click destroys work and offers no way back teaches the user to be afraid of its own buttons.
- **Fix.** Cheapest honest pair, both small: (1) route delete_selection through the existing `ase::ui::confirm` helper (src/ase_window.tcl:4039, already used elsewhere) when the selection is more than one row or when the pane is Analyses; (2) add `Revert` to the Session menu above the separator at src/ase_window.tcl:517, wired to the ase::ui::revert_state that already exists at :6832, greyed when the session is not dirty. A real per-gesture undo stack over the state dict is the right answer and is a larger piece of work; the state is a plain dict, so a bounded ring of prior dicts is tractable but is its own design.
- **Risk.** A confirm dialog in delete_selection changes the gesture tests/headless/test_ase_window.tcl drives — those rows invoke delete and assert the row count, and would hang or fail on a modal. Add the confirm behind the multi-row condition and give the tests the single-row path, or give the confirm a scriptable seam. The confirm's wording and the Revert label are user-facing strings needing ratification.
- **ADE-L.** ADE-L confirms destructive list deletions and its state is recoverable from the cellview state; more to the point, nothing in ADE-L's setup panes is one unconfirmed click from gone.

## [MAJOR] When a dialog refuses your input it says so in another window; the dialog just sits there

- **id** `refusals-appear-in-a-different-window`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** Every validation refusal in the editing loop goes to ase::echo, which is the CIW / notice channel (src/ase.tcl:298, `::xschem::notify_safe`): chana_ok's "ase: enabled $type analysis needs a non-empty '$f'" (src/ase_window.tcl:4169), chana_x_add's "ase: option name must not be empty" (:4275), add_variable_ok's empty/duplicate name, variable_editor_ok's "variable name must not be empty" (:1770), output_editor_ok's empty expression. In each case the proc `return`s with the dialog left up and nothing changed on it. Live probe of the dialog's own children confirms there is no message area to put a reason in: `PROBE chana children: .ase4.chana.btns .ase4.chana.enable .ase4.chana.opts .ase4.chana.types`. The pattern already exists elsewhere in the same file — rsel_dialog has an in-dialog status region (ase::ui::rsel_status, :3502), visible in rselect.png as the bold "Using tb_bandgap_ase.raw." line above the buttons.
- **Why it hurts.** You clear the Stop field, press OK, and the dialog does nothing. No colour change, no message, no closed window. The explanation is in a window that may be behind the schematic or closed entirely. The user's read is "OK is broken", and the natural next move is to press it again. It is the same cost every time a name collides or a field is left empty, across six dialogs.
- **Fix.** Generalise rsel_status (src/ase_window.tcl:3502) into `ase::ui::dialog_status $w <msg>`: a label gridded into the row above the button bar of any dialog_frame dialog, created lazily on first use. Then at each of the five refusal sites, call it in addition to (not instead of) the ase::echo — the CIW line stays for the action log and for headless callers, and the person looking at the dialog sees the reason. No widget path moves; the label is new and appears only when a refusal happens.
- **Risk.** Adding a row above the button bar shifts the button bar's grid row in dialogs whose row index is asserted — tests/headless/test_ase_dialogs.tcl has a helper explicitly for "the grid row a widget sits in" and S4 already tracks a row shift from a third checkbox. Grid the status label at a high fixed row (chana already reserves rows 8 and 9 for exactly this reason) and nothing moves. The sentences are already-minted strings being shown in a second place, so no new copy to ratify.
- **ADE-L.** ADE-L reports a rejected field on the form that rejected it, next to the field.

## [MAJOR] AC is hardwired to a decade sweep and its field is labelled "Points:" when the number means points per decade

- **id** `ac-sweep-is-dec-only-and-points-is-mislabelled`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** src/ase.tcl:10964: `ac { lappend lines "ac dec [dict get $a points] [dict get $a start] [dict get $a stop]" }` — the sweep word `dec` is a literal, there is no state key for it and no control in the dialog. The field set is src/ase_window.tcl:4075 `ac { return {points start stop} }` and the label is generated at :4140 as `[string totitle $f]:` → "Points:", with no unit and no qualifier. chana_opt_ac.png shows the same empty Name/Value grid as the other types, i.e. the only escape hatch is the dead form of finding #1.
- **Why it hurts.** A designer who types 100 into a field labelled "Points" for a 1 Hz–1 GHz sweep expects 100 points and gets 900. The plot looks right, the density is wrong, and nothing anywhere says `dec`. Worse, a linear AC sweep — the normal choice for a narrowband measurement around a carrier — cannot be expressed in ASE-L at all, and per finding #1 typing `lin` into Options does nothing.
- **Fix.** Add a `sweep` key to the ac row with a three-value control in the dialog (dec / oct / lin), emitted as the sweep word at src/ase.tcl:10964, defaulting to `dec` so every existing state renders byte-identically. Relabel the field to name its unit — "Points/decade:" under dec, "Points:" under lin. Same treatment for dc's "Source:" field, which today gives no hint that it wants a SPICE source instance name.
- **Risk.** The field labels are user-facing strings and a relabel needs ratification. A new widget in the ac form adds a child under $top.chana — tests/headless/test_ase_dialogs.tcl G1/G2 enumerate and drive chana children by path; adding one is additive, but the ac form's grid rows shift. src/ase.tcl's D1 golden deck in test_ase_core.tcl is unaffected while the default stays `dec`.
- **ADE-L.** ADE-L's AC form has an explicit Sweep Type control (Automatic / Linear / Logarithmic) and the point-count field relabels itself to match — "Points Per Decade" for a log sweep, "Number of Steps" for a linear one. The label always names what the number means.

## [MAJOR] Model Files and Simulation Options can only be edited by right-clicking an unmarked table; .include files and pre-commands have no GUI at all

- **id** `model-files-and-includes-have-no-door`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** modelfiles_main.png and simoptions_main.png: both dialogs are a table and a single "Close" button. Nothing else. The only way to add, edit or delete a row is a right-click on the treeview (src/ase_window.tcl:5305-5309, the ctx menu) or the Delete key (:5311) — src/ase_window.tcl:5279-5316 builds no Add/Edit/Delete buttons at all. modelfiles_main.png also shows the File column truncating the one row it has to `$::SKYWATER_MO▌`.
And two state keys have zero GUI anywhere: `grep -n "pre_commands\|includes" src/ase_window.tcl` returns NOTHING, while both are read and emitted by the deck renderer (src/ase.tcl:10804 for pre_commands, and the `.include` loop in render_deck). The source names the concrete victim at src/ase.tcl:34-39: "IHP SG13G2 needs four of them for its psp103va/mosvar/r3_cmc models".
- **Why it hurts.** Open Setup > Model Files on a fresh session and you get an empty white box and a Close button — there is no affordance in the window telling you an addition is even possible. That is a dead end for a first-time user and a hunt for everyone else. And a PDK that needs `pre_osdi` lines (any Verilog-A compiled model — IHP's SG13G2, by the code's own account) cannot be set up from the GUI at all: the user must find the .state file on disk and hand-edit Tcl list syntax into it. There is no worse answer to "how do I use my PDK" than "open the state file in an editor".
- **Fix.** (1) Add a visible Add… / Edit… / Delete button row beside Close in listdlg_open (src/ase_window.tcl:5296-5299), invoking the same three commands the context menu already has — the commands exist, only the buttons are missing. (2) `listdlg` is already a generic config dict (src/ase_window.tcl:115-119, two entries); add `includes` ({file}, one column) and `precmd` (pre_commands, whose rows are bare strings rather than dicts, so listdlg_fill needs a one-line shape adaptation), and two entries in the Setup menu at :492. That is roughly a dozen lines for a capability that today requires a text editor.
- **Risk.** New buttons add children under the listdlg toplevels; tests/headless/test_ase_dialogs.tcl drives these dialogs and asserts their button bar. Two new Setup menu entries move the Setup menu's index positions — W1m in test_ase_dialogs.tcl asserts the menu tree labels, and doc/claude/specs/ase_l.md fixes the v2 menu tree, so the two new entries are a spec change needing ratification. The dialog titles are new user-facing strings.
- **ADE-L.** ADE-L's Model Libraries setup is a full form with Add/Edit/Delete/Up/Down buttons and a file browser, and Setup > Simulator Options / Environment Options expose the simulator's own option set as a typed form — nothing in ADE-L's setup path requires editing a file by hand.

## [MINOR] The column is headed "Value" and shows the operating point's number while you are looking at a transient

- **id** `value-column-does-not-say-which-analysis`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** src/ase.tcl:10885-10938 (the 1243 block) sets the print anchor by `foreach type {dc ac tran op}` — last-enabled-wins with op moved last — so on a state with op AND tran enabled, which is exactly this bandgap bench (`analyses {{type op enabled 1} … {type tran enabled 1 stop 200u step 10n}}`), the printed outputs ride the operating point. The column heading is built at src/ase_window.tcl:832 as the literal `Value` with nothing beside it, and the pane carries no other column naming an analysis (ase_main.png: Name | Value | Plot | Save | Save Options).
- **Why it hurts.** On a bandgap the operating-point VBG and the settled transient VBG at 200 us are different numbers, and the difference is the whole point of running the transient — that is what the start-up circuit is for. A column headed "Value", sitting under an Analyses pane that shows `tran step=10n stop=200u`, reads as "the value of this output in this run". It is not; it is the DC solution. The 1243 comment reasons correctly that showing the OP number beats showing nothing, but it left the label saying neither.
- **Fix.** Name the source. Cheapest: make the heading dynamic — `Value (OP)` when the print anchor resolved to op, `Value` otherwise. Better and barely more work: a narrow fifth column carrying the analysis the number came from, filled from the same anchor computation. Either way the anchor is already computed in render_deck and would need surfacing into the session's results attr alongside the numbers.
- **Risk.** A column heading is user-facing copy and needs ratification. tests/headless/test_ase_window.tcl asserts the outs pane's column set and headings; test_ase_optier_0963.tcl section P pins the 1243 ruling about WHICH analysis is printed — naming it does not change it, but that suite should be read before touching the anchor code.
- **ADE-L.** ADE-L's Outputs pane value is bound to a named expression evaluated against a selected results database, and the analysis is never ambiguous — you chose it when you built the expression.

## [MINOR] The log window pops to the screen corner on every run, cannot scroll sideways, and has no way to find anything in it

- **id** `log-window-is-unreadable-and-uninvited`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** Measured live: `PROBE logwin children = .ase4.logwin.t .ase4.logwin.sb` (one text, one vertical scrollbar — that is the entire window), `PROBE logwin text -wrap = none  -xscrollcommand = ''`, `PROBE logwin geometry = 941x460+0+0`. Source: src/ase_window.tcl:7010 `text $lw.t -height 24 -width 84 -state disabled -wrap none` with only a `-yscrollcommand`; no Find, no Save, no filter, no copy affordance. src/ase_window.tcl:7270 (run_started) calls log_open unconditionally on every run, and log_open (:7002-7005) deiconifies and raises an existing one — so every single Run brings this 941x460 window to the front at the screen's top-left corner, over the schematic, with no preference to stop it.
- **Why it hurts.** `-wrap none` with no horizontal scrollbar means any line longer than the window is simply unreachable — and the lines that matter most are ngspice errors quoting a full PDK model path. logwin.png happens to show short lines; a real model-resolution failure does not. Meanwhile the window that is hardest to read is the one that forces itself in front of your schematic on every iteration of the edit-run-look loop, and the only way out is to close it and have it come back next run.
- **Fix.** Three independent one-liners plus one decision. (a) Add `-xscrollcommand` and a horizontal scrollbar to src/ase_window.tcl:7010-7015 (or set `-wrap word`; pick one, not both). (b) Add a Find entry bound to the text widget — the tree already does this elsewhere and it is a few lines. (c) Make the auto-raise a preference: keep opening the log on first run of a session, but on subsequent runs update it in place without `raise` if the user has moved or lowered it. (d) The run's headline — exit code and elapsed time, which ase::run_log_footer already computes as `=== exit 0 after 8.12 s ===` — belongs in the ASE-L status bar so the log window is not the only place it exists.
- **Risk.** tests/headless/test_ase_window.tcl and test_ase_interact.tcl reference $top.logwin and its children; adding a horizontal scrollbar adds a child under a packed layout and changes the pack order. (c) changes a behaviour the suites may witness through window state. (d) adds a new status-bar segment — ase::ui::status_text (:6775) composes a fixed five-segment line that tests read verbatim, so a sixth segment is a user-visible format change needing ratification.
- **ADE-L.** ADE-L's simulation output window is a scrollable log you open when you want it, and the run's progress lives in the ADE window itself rather than in a window that jumps the stack every iteration.

## [MINOR] Zero menu accelerators in the whole window, and the status says "Ready" both before a run and after a successful one

- **id** `no-keyboard-anywhere-and-ready-means-two-things`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** `grep -c accelerator src/ase_window.tcl` = **0**, against 119 in src/xschem.tcl's own menus. The only key bindings on the ASE-L toplevel are Control-w / Control-W to close (src/ase_window.tcl:322-323). So Run, Netlist and Run, Stop, Choose Analyses and Log have no keyboard route of any kind.
And src/ase_window.tcl:6974-6978: `running` → orange "Running"; `ok` → Green "Ready"; `default` → panel-grey "Ready". Two different states, byte-identical text, distinguished only by a background colour. ase_main.png shows "Status: Ready" on this bench with a completed run behind it — the same words a never-run session shows.
- **Why it hurts.** Netlist-and-run is pressed dozens of times an hour and requires a mouse trip to a five-character button every time. And the status bar — the one always-visible summary line — cannot answer "did that run finish, and did it work?" without a colour, which is gone the moment the window is behind something or the reader is colour-blind. "Ready" is also simply the wrong word for "finished OK": it describes the tool, not the run.
- **Fix.** (a) Add `-accelerator` plus matching toplevel bindings for the four entries that carry the loop: Netlist and Run, Run, Stop, Log. The source at src/ase_window.tcl:630-634 notes a KEY CHORD would need an actions.csv row and a callback.c registry entry for the schematic's own chord system — but a plain `bind $top <Key-...>` on the ASE-L toplevel needs neither, exactly as Control-w already does. (b) Give the ok arm its own word — the run's outcome, with the exit code and the elapsed time ase::run_log_footer already computes.
- **Risk.** Which chords is a user decision (they collide with nothing in ASE-L today, but the user's muscle memory is from ADE-L). The status-bar wording is user-facing copy and ase::ui::status_text's composed line is asserted verbatim by tests/headless/test_ase_window.tcl and read by test_ase_dialogs.tcl — a wording change moves those rows. Both need ratification.
- **ADE-L.** ADE-L is driven from a toolbar and from bindkeys the user configures, and its status line reports the last run's outcome rather than the window's readiness.

## [MINOR] The `>` button runs whatever netlist is on disk with no staleness check and no word that it did not re-netlist

- **id** `run-existing-simulates-a-stale-netlist-silently`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** src/ase.tcl:7048-7065, ase::run_existing: the only test on the netlist artifact is `if {![file isfile $nl]}` — existence. No mtime comparison against the schematic, no hash, nothing. src/ase_window.tcl:7423 comments the intent ("never re-netlists — hand-edited decks survive"), which is a legitimate feature. But the button's whole user-facing explanation is its tooltip, and strip_tips (src/ase_window.tcl:5598) gives `>` the string `[ase::ui::menu_path_run]`, i.e. the menu path "Simulation ▸ Run" — which says nothing about the netlist. The two buttons sit adjacent in the strip as `N&>` and `>` (ase_main.png).
- **Why it hurts.** `>` is the universally-understood play glyph and it sits right next to the cryptic `N&>`. A designer who changes a resistor on the schematic and presses the obvious-looking button gets a clean run, a fresh raw file and a green status, computed from the previous circuit. Nothing anywhere says so. Finding that out costs a debugging session.
- **Fix.** Two options, and (a) is the honest minimum: (a) extend the tooltip so it says what the button does not do — the tip table at src/ase_window.tcl:5592 is one line per button and the composed strings are already the pattern. (b) Better: compare the netlist artifact's mtime against the design schematic's and, when the netlist is older, say so before starting — either as a confirm or as a note in the log window's first line.
- **Risk.** Both options mint a new user-facing sentence, which needs ratification. (b) also introduces a new refusal/confirm point in the run path, and src/ase_window.tcl:7327's 1389 header is explicit that a refusal must not redden a healthy session — a staleness note must be a note, not a status change. tests/headless/test_ase_window.tcl W1s2 asserts that every strip button carries a tip; changing a tip's text moves the rows in test_ase_dialogs.tcl that compare tips against their menubar twins byte for byte.
- **ADE-L.** ADE-L's green arrow netlists and runs; running against an existing netlist is a deliberate, separately-labelled choice, not the button that looks like Play.


---

# feedback-status  (13 findings)

**Headline.** The ASE-L window is mute: all 107 of its user-facing sentences are posted to a CIW that was not even on the desktop in the live screenshot, and the only thing the window can say for itself is one of three words in a coloured box — a box that says "Ready" both before a run and after one, and "Error" when the user presses Stop.

**The one slick move.** Give the ASE-L main window RDW's status surface and make it a delivery sink for ase::echo. Right now ASE-L can say exactly one of three words in a coloured box, while 107 echo sites and 51 ase::sim_why sentences (median 163 characters, max 668) are posted to a CIW that was not on the desktop in the live screenshot and to a 28-character fallback field that was off the bottom of the screen. Everything else in this dimension is downstream of that one absence: the run has no phase to report, the refusals have nowhere to land, the staleness verdict ase::has_results already computes has no surface, the elapsed time the program already stamps at src/ase.tcl:7381 has no home. And the fix is not a design exercise — src/rdw.tcl:2228 ships a non-truncating, auto-fitting, selectable message line in a sibling window of the same application, with its own tests and its own issue history (1365). Lift it, put it under the three panes, route the existing sentences into it, and mint nothing new. The window stops being mute, and every other finding here becomes a small change to a surface that exists rather than a proposal for one that does not.

## [CRITICAL] Every ASE-L sentence is delivered to a window that is not on screen; the ASE-L window itself receives none

- **id** `sentences-land-in-a-window-that-is-not-there`  ·  **effort** medium
- **Evidence.** 107 `ase::echo` call sites (src/ase.tcl 40, src/ase_window.tcl 67; counted with a regex over both files) plus 51 return-sentences inside `ase::sim_why` (src/ase.tcl:985+, median 163 chars, max 668). ase::echo (src/ase.tcl:298) -> xschem::notify_safe -> xschem::notify (src/ciw.tcl:256). SINK 1 is `.ciw.l.t` (ciw.tcl:297), SINK 2 the action-log FILE (ciw.tcl:320), SINK 3 fires ONLY when the CIW is unmapped and writes `[xschem get top_path].statusbar.12` clipped to 28 chars by `xschem::notify_short` (ciw.tcl:112-118). ase_root.png: the live 1920x1080 desktop holds exactly three windows — ASE-L, the waveform viewer, `xschem [3] - untitled.sch` — and NO CIW (the shots were taken with --nolog; xschem.tcl:19964 only creates it without that flag). Cropping ase_root.png at +150+900 shows bare schematic canvas: the schematic window runs off the bottom of the screen, so `.statusbar.12` is not visible either. ciw.tcl:341-352 already concedes the other half (issue 0659): `winfo ismapped` is 1 for a CIW that is merely stacked behind, so the ordinary arrangement reaches zero visible sinks too. Not one of ase_window.tcl's 67 echo sites writes into the ASE-L window. The refusal path makes it explicit: `ase::run_refuse` (src/ase.tcl:7019) echoes and then calls `ase::run_ciw_raise` — the remedy for "you cannot see the message" is to raise a third window, which with no CIW does nothing at all.
- **Why it hurts.** The designer presses N&> and the window is byte-identical to before the press. Whatever ASE-L wanted to say — the model file is missing, the simulator is not executable, the casemode refusal, the co-simulation warning — was said somewhere they are not looking. They conclude the button is broken, press it again, and file the bug that the whole 1389/0643/0616 series is made of.
- **Fix.** Give the ASE-L main window its own message surface and make it a sink. The implementation already exists in this tree and is already tested: `rdw::status` / `.rdw.s.msg` (src/rdw.tcl:2228, height clamp 2031-2042, `count -displaylines` auto-fit 2085-2105, PRIMARY export, never truncates — issue 1365). Build the same widget under the three panes in `ase::ui::build` (src/ase_window.tcl, after the status frame at :753), add `ase::ui::say {key msg tag}` that writes it, and have every ase_window.tcl echo site call both (or add an ASE-L-aware sink inside `ase::echo` keyed on the focused session). Zero new sentences are minted — the 107 existing ones are simply re-delivered where the eyes already are, which keeps the never-invent-a-sentence rule intact.
- **Risk.** No suite asserts the absence of a status surface, so the widget itself is additive. But `ase::ui::status_text` (:6775) joins exactly five named segments and is asserted by tests/headless/test_ase_savestate_adopt.tcl:162/193/195, test_ase_launch.tcl:233, test_ase_simdlg_0937.tcl:1101-1158, test_ase_window.tcl:1626 — do NOT fold the new surface into that string. Routing echo through a second sink touches the `exactly one ::ciw_echo per notify` fence (ciw.tcl PS8/PS19, test_ase_locked_wire_pick_0160:175, test_sod_pick_no_select_0204:138/295): add the ASE sink OUTSIDE notify, not inside it. Whether the surface duplicates or replaces the CIW copy is a user ruling.
- **ADE-L.** In Virtuoso the CIW is the root window of the session — it cannot be closed or absent, so ADE-L's messages land somewhere by construction. In xschem the CIW is optional (--nolog), closable, and was missing in the measured desktop, so ASE-L inherited the delivery model without the guarantee that made it work.

## [CRITICAL] The one-word status segment says "Ready" for both idle and success, "Error" for the user's own Stop, and never clears

- **id** `status-word-is-wrong-in-two-of-four-states`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** `ase::ui::set_status` (src/ase_window.tcl:6968-6981) has four arms: running=orange/"Running", ok=Green/"Ready", fail=red/"Error", default=idlebg/"Ready". Grepping every call site: it is only ever called with running (:7273), ok (:7247), fail (:7261, :7323, :7337, :7410) — the `default` arm is DEAD CODE and `idlebg` (captured at :835) is written once and never read again. So "Status: Ready" on the themed panel grey (the literal -text at :744) and "Status: Ready" on X11 `Green` #00FF00 are the same word; the only difference is a swatch. `ase::ui::do_stop` (:7460) sends SIGKILL, the child exits CHILDKILLED, run_finished's else-arm (:7261) fires, and the bar turns red "Error" — asserted and locked by tests/headless/test_ase_window.tcl:2891 "W7 status light red after Stop" and :2893 "W7 status text Error after Stop". `ase::ui::refresh_status` (:6743) deliberately does not touch `.stat` (its own comment at :6735), and Load State goes through do_load_state_from -> load_state_commit -> populate (:6286) -> refresh_status, so a red Error survives a state import and goes on describing a run belonging to a state the user has left. Four visual states, three words, one of them a lie in both directions.
- **Why it hurts.** The single glance a designer takes at the bottom bar has to answer "did that run finish, and did it work?" It answers "Ready" in the case where nothing has ever run and in the case where a run just succeeded, and it answers "Error" when they themselves hit Stop on a tran they no longer wanted. After the first Stop the red is furniture; after the second, nobody reads the bar at all — which is precisely how a real failure gets waved through.
- **Fix.** Separate the four states properly: idle (no results / never run), running, done, failed, stopped. Add a `stopped` arm to set_status and have do_stop set a session flag that run_finished reads instead of scoring CHILDKILLED as failure. Have populate/refresh_status reset a stale `fail` to the state's real condition (via ase::has_results, see the staleness finding). The words themselves are user-facing and must be ratified: record a `rule` debt with the proposed set (e.g. Ready / Running / Done / Failed / Stopped) rather than minting them.
- **Risk.** Moves tests/headless/test_ase_window.tcl:2546-2558 (W6 asserts background orange then Green and text `Status: Running`/`Status: Ready`), :2891-2894 (W7 red/`Status: Error` after Stop), :3220 and :3236 (a refused second launch must NOT redden), and the set_status stubs in tests/headless/test_ase_core.tcl:1653-1658 and :1714-1722 (RG12/RG14 record the sequence of `what` words). Every new word needs a user ruling. The colours orange / `Green` / `red` are bare X11 names and are NOT in the nine-colour user-locked palette — adding status colours to that palette is also the user's decision.

## [CRITICAL] The Outputs Value column — where the answer lands — is blank for results ASE-L knows it has, then shows the previous state's numbers after a Load State

- **id** `value-column-blank-then-wrong`  ·  **effort** medium
- **Evidence.** ase_main.png: five output rows (VBG, ENN, START, Icc, TEMPERAT), every Value cell empty. Same session: rselect.png reads "Loaded — this session's viewer: tb_bandgap_ase.raw (tran)" with the in-dialog status line "Using tb_bandgap_ase.raw."; ase_root.png shows the viewer plotting from that raw; logwin.png shows the numbers themselves — `VBG = 1.177085e+00`, `START = 1.803088e+00`, `i(VCC) = 2.895431e-05`, `TEMPERAT = 2.700000e+01`. The cause is a single-writer: `ase::session_setattr $key results` occurs exactly ONCE in the whole tree, at src/ase_window.tcl:7245, inside run_finished's `ec == 0` arm. Nothing on session open, nothing on Results > Select. The source comment says so itself at :1545-1547 ("set by run_finished — blank before the first successful run"). The second half is worse: `ase::ui::populate` (:1548) refills the Value column from that same per-session attr, keyed by `ase::ui::output_result_key` (:1032) which is the output's NAME. `do_load_state_from` calls populate at :6286. So run state A, then Session > Load State into state B, and every row of B whose output name matches one in A displays A's number with no marker at all.
- **Why it hurts.** A corner sweep is exactly the workflow where states share output names by construction — ngspice_state1 and a corner state both have VBG. The designer loads the corner, reads 1.177085 off the Outputs pane, and it is the previous corner's number. This is the same silent-wrong-data class that issue 0838 was written about (a raw 5m28s older than its deck annotating 08:52's operating point onto 08:57's netlist), reached by a different door. And in the ordinary case the pane is simply empty, so the designer has to open the log or the viewer to read a number the pane was built to show.
- **Fix.** Make `results` a function of the session's currently-loaded raw rather than of the last in-process run: populate it in `ase::open_state`/`ase::ui::open` and in `ase::ui::rsel_commit` from the same raw that rsel_dialog already resolves, and CLEAR it in load_state_commit before populate runs. Gate the display on `ase::has_results $key` (src/ase.tcl:7691), which already answers "is this raw at least as new as the deck it claims to describe".
- **Risk.** Adds writers to a session attr that today has one; keep the single-writer discipline by giving it one named proc. Touches tests/headless/test_ase_window.tcl's W6 leg (:2550 onward asserts the id row's Value cell is filled from the parsed results after Netlist-and-Run) and test_ase_plot.tcl. Clearing on load is a behaviour change a user should see described; the stale-number marker, if any, is a new user-facing decision.
- **ADE-L.** ADE-L fills the Outputs Value column from the results directory currently selected in Results > Select — you attach an old psf directory and the numbers appear without re-running. Requiring a fresh in-session run to see a number is a restriction ADE-L does not have.

## [MAJOR] Nothing at all happens between the button press and the launch, and that gap can be 30 seconds of frozen Tk

- **id** `blind-gap-between-press-and-launch`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** `ase::ui::do_run` (src/ase_window.tcl:7327) runs synchronously to completion before any status change: run_busy -> design_path -> `ase::stack_level` -> `design_window ifhidden` + a bare `update` (:7392) -> `ase::run` -> `ase::netlist` (the whole hierarchy, plus op_annot::save_cards — the comment at :7355 measures 66 ms + 177 ms on this small bench) -> `ase::run_precheck` (src/ase.tcl:4171) -> `ase::sim_probe_run` (:4213) -> run_deck -> execute. `ase::ui::set_status $key running` fires only inside run_started (:7273), AFTER all of it. The probe's budget is `ase::cap_budget_ms 30000` (src/ase.tcl:137), and src/ase.tcl:3019-3022 records the measurement on the user's own build: "447 ms cold, 0 ms warm -- and 31.2 seconds for a program that exists, is executable and never answers, because ase::cap_budget_ms is 30000 and Tk is frozen for every one of them." Throughout that window the bar reads `Status: Ready` (ase_main.png) and the window does not repaint.
- **Why it hurts.** On a real testbench the netlist step alone is seconds, and the first sign of life is the log window appearing on top of everything. Until then the user has no way to distinguish "working on it" from "the button did nothing", which is exactly the state that makes them press it a second time — the double-launch race that issue 1389 had to be written to survive.
- **Fix.** Set a `preparing` status (and, if a surface exists, one line naming the phase) as the FIRST statement of do_run / do_run_existing, before the design routing, and let run_started promote it to running. The status word is user-facing: record a `rule` debt for it. Separately, the 30 s probe should not run on the click path at all — `ase::sim_caps_have_path` (src/ase.tcl:3025) is already the free peek that the row editor uses for exactly this reason; the precheck can take the same route and defer the launch-probe.
- **Risk.** Adding a status write above the busy gate would redden or re-word a session whose earlier run is alive — the exact defect 1389 fixed (see the run_raised header at :7281-7317, and test_ase_window.tcl:3220/:3236). Put the new write BELOW `run_busy`'s refusal and above everything else. test_ase_core.tcl RG12/RG14 (:1653, :1714) record the exact sequence of set_status arguments and will see a new leading value.

## [MAJOR] You cannot tell an 8-second run from a hung one: no elapsed time, no progress, no control disabled — and the program knows the elapsed time

- **id** `no-elapsed-no-progress-during-run`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** During a run the ASE-L window changes in exactly two ways: `.status.stat` goes orange/"Running" (:7273) and the log toplevel opens and streams raw bytes (attach_trace :7083, log_trace :7102). There is no timer: grep for `after` in the run path returns only `after idle` (:7251, :7259) and `after 120`. Nothing is disabled — `grep 'configure -state'` over src/ase_window.tcl returns only the Launch placeholder, the Annotate submenu, one readonly entry and the log text widget; not a single run control. So N&>, > and ! look and behave identically before, during and after a run, and pressing ! with nothing running emits `ase: no simulation running for this session` (:7470) — untagged, to the CIW. Meanwhile the run's start time IS recorded: `t0 [clock milliseconds]` at src/ase.tcl:7381, with exactly one consumer — the log FOOTER, `ase::run_log_footer` (src/ase.tcl:7473), which formats `=== exit 0 after 8.12 s ===` (:7489) into the log file after the fact. logwin.png shows that line. The analysis arguments on screen (`step=10n stop=200u`, ase_main.png Analyses row 4) are never related to anything the user can watch.
- **Why it hurts.** A transient that normally takes 8 s and today takes 400 s because gmin stepping is thrashing is indistinguishable from a live one. The designer either waits far too long or kills a healthy run. And having no disabled state on the run controls is what makes the accidental second press — the race the run_busy guard exists to absorb — the normal case rather than the rare one.
- **Fix.** Show the clock that already exists: a `T+MM:SS` segment driven by an `after 500` reschedule from run_started to run_finished, reading the same `t0` the footer reads (expose it as `ase::run_elapsed $key`). Grey `N&>` / `>` and un-grey `!` while `ase::ui::run_busy` is non-empty, using the same `-postcommand`/`-state disabled` mechanism the Annotate submenu already uses (:648-690, :2644-2654). No new sentence is needed for the timer; a status word change is.
- **Risk.** Adds a segment to the bottom bar, so `ase::ui::status_text` (:6775) grows a sixth field — that string is asserted by test_ase_savestate_adopt.tcl:162/193/195, test_ase_launch.tcl:233, test_ase_simdlg_0937.tcl:1101-1158, test_ase_window.tcl:1626 (all `string match` on substrings, so most survive an append but the join changes). Greying the strip moves test_ase_window.tcl's W1s tooltip legs (:1430 onward) only if widget state is asserted there; W6/W7 drive those buttons through `invoke`, which still works on a disabled Tk button only if re-enabled first — check before shipping.

## [MAJOR] The log window renders 84 columns of a 250-character ngspice error, with -wrap none and no horizontal scrollbar

- **id** `log-shows-84-of-250-chars`  ·  **effort** medium
- **Evidence.** `ase::ui::log_open` (src/ase_window.tcl:7010): `text $lw.t -height 24 -width 84 -state disabled -wrap none -yscrollcommand ...` with `scrollbar $lw.sb -orient vertical` as the ONLY scrollbar (:7011-7015). No tags are ever configured on it (`grep 'tag configure' src/ase_window.tcl` returns three hits, all in rsel_dialog at :3879-3881). Measured live on :99 by rebuilding that exact widget at the log's own 941x460: the text area is 928 px = exactly 84 columns of Nimbus Mono PS 13 (Courier is not installed); a realistic `Error: unable to find definition of model "sky130_fd_pr__nfet_01v8_lvt" referenced from instance XM7.XM1 ... section tt does not define it` is 250 chars = 2750 px; `xview` returns `0.0 0.3353`; `bbox 2.200` returns EMPTY — that character is not rendered anywhere. 66% of the sentence is off the right edge with no scrollbar, no wrap and no keyboard affordance; only an undiscoverable middle-button scan reaches it. logwin.png confirms the absence of any toolbar, search box, colour or wrap control.
- **Why it hurts.** The half of an ngspice error that names the model library, the section, the instance path or the include file is the half that is cut off — the log is at its least useful exactly when it matters. And the one line ASE-L itself writes into that log, `*** ASE-L: CO-SIMULATION PROBLEM (...): ... The results of this run cannot be trusted. ***` (:7237), renders in the same plain black 13pt mono as every routine `Note:` line above it, so the sentence that says the numbers are wrong looks like the sentence that says dc value used for op.
- **Fix.** Three changes to log_open, none of which touches a sentence: add a horizontal scrollbar (`-xscrollcommand`, `scrollbar $lw.xsb -orient horizontal`) or default to `-wrap word` with a toggle; add `tag configure` for error / warning / ase (foreground from the palette's accent for ASE-L's own lines, and a distinct one for `Error:`/`Warning:`) and tag on insert in `log_append` (:7030); add a Find entry bound to Ctrl-F using the Text widget's own `search -forwards -nocase`. A `Save log as…` and `Copy` are the fourth. The 13pt mono size and the wrap default are user-visible: record a `rule` debt (RDW's own `aA` control, src/rdw.tcl:2242-2258, is the precedent for a per-window private font rather than resizing TkFixedFont globally).
- **Risk.** tests/headless/test_ase_window.tcl:2550 and :2859 read `$top.logwin.t get 1.0 end` and `string match "*No. of Data Rows*"` — content assertions, unaffected by wrap or tags. :2544/:2839-2857 assert the toplevel exists and Ctrl-W closes it — adding a horizontal scrollbar as a sibling of `$lw.t` does not move those paths, but packing order does matter (`pack $lw.sb -side right` before `$lw.t`). Colour choices sit outside the nine-colour user-locked palette and are a user decision.

## [MAJOR] The log window opens itself on every single run, un-transient, at the screen corner, on top of the ASE-L window

- **id** `log-window-covers-the-window-that-launched-it`  ·  **effort** trivial
- **Evidence.** `ase::ui::run_started` (:7267-7274) calls `ase::ui::log_open` unconditionally on every launch, then log_clear. log_open creates `toplevel $lw` (:7005) with no `wm transient` — the only two `wm transient` calls in the file are :399 (the dirty-save prompt) and :1403 (the small-dialog helper). Measured geometry from the task's own facts: the log window lands 941x460+0+0, the ASE-L window is 798x502 at the screen's top-left (ase_root.png shows its title bar at 0,0). The log is 143 px wider and covers 460 of the ASE-L window's 502 rows — the menubar, the temperature entry, all three panes.
- **Why it hurts.** Press N&> and the window you pressed it in disappears. Every run costs a window-management gesture to get back to the Analyses and Outputs panes, and a designer sweeping a corner does that dozens of times an hour. It also means the status segment and the Outputs Value column — the two things a finished run updates — are behind the log at the exact moment they change.
- **Fix.** Do not auto-open on every run; open it on the FIRST run of a session and thereafter only raise it if it is already open (the `winfo exists` arm of log_open already does the right thing — the change is to stop calling log_open from run_started when the user has closed it). Failing that, `wm transient $lw $top` and place it beside rather than over the parent, the way the small dialogs already land at +77+71 relative to the parent. Auto-open on a nonzero exit is the compromise worth ratifying.
- **Risk.** tests/headless/test_ase_window.tcl:2544 asserts "W6 log toplevel appears" immediately after a Netlist-and-Run, and :2839 asserts it is open before the Ctrl-W leg — a first-run-only rule keeps both green in a fresh session but breaks the second run inside one test process. Check W6c's ordering before changing. Whether the log auto-opens at all is a user preference, not a bug fix: record a `rule`.

## [MAJOR] Keep the log open across a run and you never see the exit code or the elapsed time; close it and reopen and you get both

- **id** `log-content-depends-on-when-it-was-opened`  ·  **effort** trivial
- **Evidence.** Two different fillers. Live: run_started -> log_open + log_clear + attach_trace; `log_trace` (:7102) pushes only the delta of `::execute(data,$id)` — raw simulator bytes. `run_finished` (:7217) appends the last delta and the co-sim banner, and stops; it never appends the framing. The header and `=== exit 0 after 8.12 s ===` are written by `ase::run_log_write` / `ase::run_log_footer` into the log FILE (src/ase.tcl:7473-7510). Post-hoc: `ase::ui::show_log` (:7041) on a CLOSED window reads that file (:7069-7073) and shows header + body + footer — but on an already-open window it only deiconifies and raises (:7044-7048) and refills nothing. logwin.png shows the footer line, so that shot was taken by reopening after the run; a user who left the log up through the same run sees the identical simulator output with no exit code and no time.
- **Why it hurts.** The two most compressible facts about a run — did it exit 0, and how long did it take — are visible only to the user who happened to have the log closed. The habit that gets you the information is the opposite of the habit the tool encourages (it opens the log for you on every run, and then withholds the summary from the window it opened).
- **Fix.** Append the footer in `run_finished` after the final delta, from the same `ase::run_log_footer` the file uses — one builder, two consumers, exactly the pattern `ase::run_log_body`/`run_log_footer` were split out to support (src/ase.tcl:7457-7466 names that requirement). Prepend the header at run_started for symmetry.
- **Risk.** `ase::run_log_body`'s contract is that the simulator's bytes reach the FILE untouched (src/ase.tcl:7457) — this change touches only the widget, not the file, so the 0618 committed log goldens are unaffected. tests/headless/test_ase_window.tcl:2550 and :2859 match on substrings of the widget text and tolerate appended lines.

## [MAJOR] The status bar silently cuts its rightmost segment — the state name — and keeps the bare window number

- **id** `status-bar-amputates-the-state-segment`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** src/ase_window.tcl:738-754 builds nine labels and packs them `-side left` in the order win, |, stat, |, temp, |, sim, |, state, with no `wm minsize` anywhere in the file (grep: 0 hits). Tk's packer drops what does not fit from the END. Measured on :99 by rebuilding that exact frame with AseLabelFont (which resolves to Nimbus Sans 10 bold, Arial not being installed): the shot's own line is 495 px; an ordinary corner line (`Simulator: ngspice-v50-mpi`, `State: corner_ss_125C_minvdd`) is 656 px. At a 560-wide window the `State:` label renders 133 px of its requested 212; at 460 it renders 33 px — the word "St". No ellipsis, no tooltip, no reflow, and no warning from Tk. ase_small.png (560x360) shows the same amputation in the treeview headers: "Typ|Ena" for Type/Enable, "Plc|Sa|Save Op" for Plot/Save/Save Options. The one segment that never clips is the bare leading window number "4" — 12 px, first in the pack order.
- **Why it hurts.** `State:` is the segment that answers "which of my six corner setups am I editing?", and it is the first thing to vanish. A truncated state name that still reads plausibly (`corner_ss_125C_...` cut to `corner_ss_1`) is worse than a missing one: the designer edits the wrong setup and the bar agreed with them. Meanwhile the bare `4`, which is the least load-bearing thing on the line, is guaranteed real estate.
- **Fix.** Replace the nine-label pack with a grid: fixed-width columns for win/stat/temp, and a weighted column for sim and state that elides in the MIDDLE (`.../corner_ss_125C_minvdd` keeps the discriminating tail) with the full text on a `balloon` tip — the file already uses ::balloon with a 300 ms delay at :795-807. Set `wm minsize` on the session toplevel to the reqwidth of the status frame. Middle-elision is a rendering rule, not a sentence, so no ruling is needed for the mechanism; the balloon's 300 ms figure inherits the unratified rule debt 1368.
- **Risk.** `ase::ui::status_text` (:6775) reads `cget -text` from the five named widgets and joins them with ` | ` — if elision is applied to the -text, that string changes and test_ase_savestate_adopt.tcl:162/193/195, test_ase_launch.tcl:233, test_ase_simdlg_0937.tcl:1158 and test_ase_window.tcl:1626 all move. Keep the full text in a variable and elide only what is drawn, or have status_text read the model rather than the widget.

## [MAJOR] ase::has_results already answers "are these numbers from the deck I am looking at", and the answer reaches nothing the user can see

- **id** `staleness-is-computed-and-never-shown`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** `ase::has_results` (src/ase.tcl:7691) is `last_rawfile ne {} && !results_stale`, where results_stale is `mtime(raw) >= mtime(deck)` — issue 0838's gate, written after a measured incident recorded at src/ase.tcl:7703-7712: with every analysis unticked, a fresh deck was written, ngspice refused it, the PREVIOUS raw survived, file-existence said "has results", and annotation painted 08:52's operating point onto 08:57's netlist "with nothing on screen to distinguish it from a good run". Its ONLY consumer in the tree is greying the Results > Annotate submenu (src/ase_window.tcl:2644-2654, `-postcommand` PULL at :613/:666). The status bar does not read it. The Outputs Value column does not read it (it reads the per-session `results` attr instead — see the value-column finding). The window title does not read it.
- **Why it hurts.** The question "is what I am reading current?" is the one a designer asks fifty times a day, the program computes a correct staleness-aware answer, and the answer is expressed as one greyed submenu three menus deep. Everything else on screen — the plotted waveform, the Value column, the annotated schematic — goes on presenting the old run with full confidence.
- **Fix.** Make has_results the source of a visible state: a sixth status segment or a badge on the Outputs labelframe reading current / stale / none, refreshed from `ase::ui::refresh_status` (which already runs on every session mutation via session_changed at :6789). Same predicate, one more consumer — which is the invariant the file already keeps for has_results ("the ONE named predicate", :2644).
- **Risk.** The words are user-facing and must be ratified: record a `rule`. Adding a segment moves the `status_text` join (see the amputation finding for the exact test list). Calling has_results on every refresh adds two `file mtime` calls per repaint — cheap, but refresh_status_all (:6764) fans it across every open session.

## [MAJOR] Unsaved changes are announced by a single asterisk in the window-manager title and nowhere else

- **id** `dirty-is-one-asterisk-in-the-wm-title`  ·  **effort** trivial
- **Evidence.** `ase::ui::refresh_title` (src/ase_window.tcl:6723-6731) is the whole of it: `Analog Sim Environment <cell>`, plus ` (unsaved)` when the session was never saved, plus ` *` when `ase::session_dirty` (src/ase.tcl:9420 — a serialize-and-compare against the saved snapshot) is true. `wm title` is the only writer. ase_root.png / the title crop: openbox centres "Analog Sim Environment tb_bandgap" in a ~12 px title bar; a trailing ` *` there is a few pixels wide, is drawn by the window manager rather than the application, and disappears entirely under a tiling WM, a maximised window, a WM that truncates long titles, or a taskbar-only view. The bottom bar — the strip whose entire job is session state, and which carries a `State: ngspice_state1` segment — reads identically whether the state on disk matches the state in the window or not.
- **Why it hurts.** The dirty state gates a modal prompt on close (:456) and on xschem quit (:473), so the user meets it as a surprise dialog rather than as something they had been watching. Between a variable edit and that dialog there is no reliable sign that the session and the disk have diverged — and on a corner sweep the whole point is knowing which of six states you have unsaved edits in.
- **Fix.** Put dirtiness in the `State:` segment where the state name already lives (the marker character is a user-facing decision — record a `rule` with the options, e.g. `State: ngspice_state1 *` vs a colour change on that segment vs an explicit word). refresh_status already runs on the right event: session_changed (:6789) fires refresh_title and refresh_status together on every session mutation.
- **Risk.** `status_text` gains a suffix on the `state` segment, which is exactly what test_ase_savestate_adopt.tcl:193/195 asserts on — :195 checks that a saved session's status_text does NOT match `*(unsaved)*`, so a new marker must be distinct from that string. test_ase_launch.tcl:233 asserts `*State: (unsaved)*` for the untitled case. tests/headless/test_ase_dirty.tcl owns the dirty semantics.

## [MINOR] 54 of 107 user-facing sentences are emitted with no tag, so they render as ordinary black text in the CIW

- **id** `half-the-sentences-carry-no-severity`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** Counting every `ase::echo` / `::ase::echo` call across src/ase.tcl and src/ase_window.tcl: 107 sites — 48 tagged `error`, 5 tagged `note`, 54 with no tag at all. The CIW colours only what is tagged: `tag configure error -foreground red` (src/ciw.tcl:451), `note -foreground {dark red}` (:490), `input` blue, `result` gray30 (:449-450). An untagged sentence is plain black, visually identical to a command echo. Untagged examples, all of them things a user needs to notice: `ase: no simulation running for this session` (src/ase_window.tcl:7470), `ase: Stop is not available on Windows` (:7474), `ase: no simulation log yet: <path>` (:7075), `ase: no netlist yet: <path>` (:7490).
- **Why it hurts.** In a scrolling CIW pane the only cue that a line is about the user rather than about the machine is its colour. Half of ASE-L's lines forfeit that cue, so even the user who does have the CIW in front of them scrolls past the answer.
- **Fix.** Tag every echo site. This is a mechanical pass with no new sentences: `note` for the informational ones, `error` for the refusals. Worth a fence like the strip-tooltip loop (:791-807) — a test that every ase::echo in ase_window.tcl passes a tag, so a 108th site cannot ship bare.
- **Risk.** Low. Tags reach `xschem::notify -tag` and are recorded by notify_record; some ASE suites stub `::ciw_echo` and assert on `$tag` (test_ase_log_seam_0207, test_ase_core NT-rows) — check those before changing a site from untagged to `error`, since 'exactly one error-tagged sentence' is an asserted property on some paths (e.g. src/ase_window.tcl:6279-6281's two mutually-exclusive error arms, row H4d).

## [MINOR] Three windows of one application, three different status-bar models

- **id** `three-status-bars-three-conventions`  ·  **effort** medium
- **Evidence.** ASE-L (src/ase_window.tcl:738-754): nine labels, ` | ` separators, all bold Nimbus Sans 10, one coloured segment, single line, silently truncating — `4 | Status: Ready | T=27 C | Simulator: ngspice-v50 | State: ngspice_state1` (statusbar crop of ase_main.png). Waveform viewer: four-space separators, no pipes, no window number — `Plot: single    x: 1.5u    y: 900m` (tests/headless/test_wave_snap.tcl:362; visible as `Plot: single` at the foot of the viewer in ase_root.png). RDW (src/rdw.tcl): a wrapping text surface `.rdw.s.msg` that auto-fits 1 to 4 display lines (`rdw::status_max_lines` :2031, `_status_show` :2085-2105), never truncates a sentence (issue 1365), exports the sentence to the X PRIMARY selection so it can be pasted, and carries every gesture's verdict prefixed with the gesture's own label (:7247, :7304-7438).
- **Why it hurts.** The three windows sit side by side on the same desktop (ase_root.png) and teach three different reading habits. More concretely: RDW's model is strictly better than ASE-L's — it is the one that never loses a sentence — and it is already written, already tested and already in this tree, so ASE-L's users are getting the worse of two designs the same repository ships.
- **Fix.** Adopt RDW's surface in ASE-L (see the first finding) and align the separator convention across the three bars. Which convention wins is a user decision — record a `rule`.
- **Risk.** Aligning the viewer's separators moves tests/headless/test_wave_snap.tcl:358-384, which asserts the exact `Plot: single    x: 1.5u    y: 900m` string in eight rows. Aligning ASE-L's moves the status_text assertions listed above. Neither is worth doing on its own; do it as part of the surface work.


---

# keyboard-a11y  (9 findings)

**Headline.** ASE-L has literally zero keyboard accelerators and zero menu mnemonics across all 26 of its commands, its Outputs Plot/Save checkboxes are provably unreachable without a mouse, and the Tab chain that does exist is invisible because the focus ring is turned off application-wide — so the one window an analog designer lives in all day is the only window in xschem you cannot drive from the keyboard.

**The one slick move.** Bind `<Control-Shift-Key-P>` on the ASE-L toplevel to a command palette whose rows are built by walking `$top.mb` — every cascade/entry pair rendered as the menu path string ASE-L already composes for its tooltips (`ase::ui::menu_path_stop` and friends, issue 1391). One chord, and all 26 verbs become two keystrokes: Ctrl+Shift+P, type "net", Return. It is the single highest-leverage change in this dimension because it is the only one that mints nothing the user has to ratify: Ctrl+Shift+P is ALREADY this application's palette chord (src/xschem.tcl:16770, `bind $topwin <Control-Shift-Key-P> "command_palette $parent; break"`), so it is coherence rather than a new decision; the palette dialog is already keyboard-first (type-to-filter, Up/Down, Return, Escape — src/action_registry.tcl:593-627); and the row text is the menu path the user can already read on screen, so no new user-facing sentence is invented. Reuse `fuzzy_subseq_score` but NOT `palette_refilter`, which is hard-wired to the global `action_table` (action_registry.tcl:499,506) that ASE-L's hand-built menubar has no rows in. It also sidesteps the whole chord-table ratification problem: ship the palette now, and let the user name the six or eight chords they actually want afterwards, from a window where they can already reach everything.

## [CRITICAL] Not one accelerator and not one mnemonic in the entire window — 26 commands, all mouse-only, while the sibling waveform viewer ships 11

- **id** `no-accelerators-no-mnemonics`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** `grep -n "accelerator" src/ase_window.tcl` returns NOTHING (exit 1) across 7491 lines; `grep -n "\-underline" src/ase_window.tcl` likewise returns nothing. Confirmed live on :99 by walking the built menubar: every one of the 9 cascades and all 26 entries reports `accel=<> underline=<-1>` (probe dump, scratchpad/l9/probe.out lines 7-38). `bind all <Alt-Key>` IS armed (`tk::TraverseToMenu %W %A`, measured) and `bind all <Key-F10>` IS armed (`tk::FirstMenu %W`) — F10 works and correctly skips the disabled Launch cascade, landing on Session (measured: active entry = 1) — but Alt+letter can never match anything because no entry has an underline. Contrast src/wave_viewer.tcl:18824-18921 and :14902-14921: the same application's waveform viewer declares AND binds Ctrl+N, Ctrl+W, Ctrl+Q, Ctrl+C, Ctrl+V, Ctrl+B, Ctrl+D, Ctrl+E, Ctrl+G, E and e. src/rdw.tcl binds Ctrl+C and the digits 1-4. ASE-L binds exactly three keys on its toplevel: `<Control-Key-w>`, `<Control-Key-W>`, `<FocusIn>` (ase_window.tcl:318-323), and Ctrl+W carries no `-accelerator` label on its own `Session > Close` entry, so even the one chord that exists is undiscoverable.
- **Why it hurts.** A bench user runs, reads the log, tweaks a variable and runs again dozens of times an hour. Every single one of those is a mouse trip to a menubar. Cadence ADE-L users drive that loop from the keyboard; here the only keyboard door is F10 followed by arrow-hunting through nine cascades, which nobody does twice. This is the reason ASE-L feels like a form and the waveform viewer feels like a tool.
- **Fix.** Two independent halves, both cheap.
(a) `-underline` on the nine cascades, giving a distinct Alt letter each: Launch=L(0), Session=S(0), S**e**tup=e(1), Analyses=A(0), Variables=V(0), Outputs=O(0), S**i**mulation=i(1), Results=R(0), Tools=T(0). This costs nothing but nine integers — `bind all <Alt-Key>` already routes it, measured — and needs no new binding at all.
(b) A Tier-1 chord set bound on the ASE-L toplevel and mirrored as `-accelerator` on the twin menu entry. CONTROL CHORDS ONLY, never a bare letter: measured on :99, a `bind $top <Key-r>` on the ASE-L toplevel fires WHILE the user types 'r' into the temperature entry (the character lands in the entry AND the toplevel script runs — Entry's class `<KeyPress>` is `tk::EntryInsert` with no `break`, and the toplevel's name is in the entry's bindtags: `.ase4.tb.temp Entry .ase4 all`). The same probe with `<Control-Key-r>` left the entry text untouched at `27`. This is the identical trap src/cadence_style_rc:170-181 documents for `.drw`.
Proposed Tier 1, chosen to collide with nothing in wave_viewer.tcl, rdw.tcl or keybindings.csv (every row of which is ctx `canvas` or `graph`, i.e. a different toplevel — verified, zero `global` rows in src/keybindings.csv):
  Ctrl+R          Simulation > Netlist and Run
  Ctrl+Shift+R    Simulation > Run
  Ctrl+period     Simulation > Stop
  Ctrl+L          Simulation > Log
  Ctrl+S          Session > Save State
  Ctrl+O          Session > Load State
  Ctrl+D          Session > Design Window
  Ctrl+W          Session > Close  (ALREADY BOUND at :322 — this is a label, not a binding)
Everything else stays menu-only until the user asks, which is what the command palette in `slick_move` makes tolerable.
- **Risk.** THE CHORD TABLE IS A USER RULING and must not be minted silently — file a `rule` debt via `owed.sh add rule` before shipping (b).
