The `-underline` half (a) is pure win and needs no ruling. NO TEST SUITE MOVES for either half: test_ase_window.tcl:566-571 builds `mlabels` by reading `-label` only, and W1s at :1391 asserts the strip buttons' `-text` only; nothing anywhere reads `-accelerator` or `-underline` off an ASE-L widget (`grep -ln 'accelerator\|underline' tests/headless/*.tcl` lists 13 files, none of them an ASE suite). One coherence question for the user: Ctrl+D would mean `Design Window` in ASE-L and `Graph > Clear All` in the waveform viewer (wave_viewer.tcl:14736, :18889). Also note `bind $top <Control-W>` (:323) matches Ctrl+SHIFT+W on X11, so that combination is already spent on a duplicate of Ctrl+W and cannot be assigned.
- **ADE-L.** ADE-L is menu-driven but every entry carries a mnemonic and the run/stop verbs carry accelerators; the whole simulate loop is reachable without the mouse. A window with zero accelerators is a restriction ADE-L does not have.

## [CRITICAL] The Outputs Plot and Save checkboxes cannot be toggled by keyboard at all — measured, and there is no menubar door to the output editor either

- **id** `outputs-checkboxes-mouse-only`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** MEASURED both directions on :99 against the real tb_bandgap bench, so the comparison is apples-to-apples. Mouse path: `ase::ui::pane_click $key ana <cx> <cy>` on the Enable cell's own bbox returns 1 and the state flips `enabled` 1 -> 0. Keyboard path: with `focus -force` confirmed on the treeview and the row both selected and item-focused, `<Key-Return>` then `<Key-space>` leave the flag at 0 — unchanged (scratchpad/l9/probe2.out). The mechanism is visible in the code: the ONLY two callers of `ase::ui::toggle_flag` are ase_window.tcl:927 and :931, both inside `ase::ui::pane_click` (:917), which is reached only from the `<Button-1>` bind at :859-861. The live instance binds on the pane are exactly `<<TreeviewSelect>> <Button-1> <Button-3> <Double-Button-1>` — no key bindings whatsoever. Treeview's CLASS bindings do claim `<Key-Return>` and `<Key-space>`, but both are `ttk::treeview::ToggleFocus %W` (measured), which opens/closes a node and is a no-op on a flat leaf row. And there is no second door: the live Outputs menu is exactly `To Be Saved > | To Be Plotted > | Save All…` — no Edit entry — so `ase::ui::output_editor` (whose dialog DOES carry keyboard-operable Plot/Save checkbuttons) is reachable only from `<Double-1>` (:863) or the `<Button-3>` context menu (:888). Contrast the Analyses pane, whose Enable flag DOES have a keyboard route through `Analyses > Choose…` and its `checkbutton $w.enable` at :4111.
- **Why it hurts.** Choosing which signals get plotted and saved is the single most-repeated edit in a simulation session — more frequent than changing an analysis, far more frequent than saving state. It is the one thing in ASE-L a designer does between every pair of runs, and it is the one thing in ASE-L that has no keyboard path of any kind. A user who has just arrowed down to the row they want has to let go of the keyboard, find a 12-pixel checkbox glyph, and click it.
- **Fix.** Two changes, neither of which touches an existing binding.
(a) `bind $pf.tv <Key-space>` and `<Key-Return>` in `ase::ui::build_pane` (:843), routed to a new `ase::ui::pane_key_toggle` that resolves the row from `[$tv focus]` (not `[$tv selection]`, which can be several rows) and the column from the pane's checkbox set — `enable` for `ana`, and for `outs` a first press on `plot`, Shift+Space or a second column-cycling press on `save`. Both must end in `break` so the class binding's `ToggleFocus` does not also run. `<Key-Return>` on a row whose focused column is NOT a checkbox should instead open the row's editor — the keyboard twin of `<Double-1>` at :863, via the existing `ase::ui::pane_dblclick`.
(b) Add `Outputs > Edit…` wired to `ase::ui::edit_output_first` — the exact twin of the already-shipped `Variables > Edit…` (:544-548, `ase::ui::edit_variables`), which does the same fall-back-to-Add when nothing is selected. That gives the outputs pane the same menubar door the variables pane has had all along, and it becomes the palette row and Alt-mnemonic target for this verb.
- **Risk.** (a) adds instance bindings only; no suite reads `[bind $tv]` on the panes (the only `[bind ...]` reads in the ASE suites are on the schematic canvas, on `$top.tb.temp <FocusOut>`, and on `<Key-Escape>` — tests/headless/test_ase_window.tcl:1467, test_ase_interact.tcl:192-195, test_ase_dialogs.tcl:1276/1284/1295). W3c in test_ase_window.tcl asserts the MOUSE toggle persists and is untouched. New rows should be added for the keyboard path, or it will rot exactly the way the mouse path did not. (b) ADDS an entry to the Outputs menu, which W1m does not currently enumerate for Outputs (it enumerates Simulation, Netlist, Session and Tools) — verify before assuming, and a new menu LABEL is a user-facing string the user ratifies. The Plot-then-Save column-cycling convention in (a) is also a decision, not a fact: file a `rule` debt.
- **ADE-L.** In ADE-L the Outputs table's Plot and Save columns are keyboard-togglable like every other cell in the setup form. Mouse-only cells are a restriction ADE-L does not have.

## [MAJOR] Netlist and Run takes the keyboard out of ASE-L twice — first to the schematic window, then to the new log toplevel — against the standing house rule, with the fix already written in rdw.tcl

- **id** `run-steals-the-keyboard-twice`  ·  **effort** medium
- **Evidence.** MEASURED on :99, both hops, from a known starting focus of `.ase4.body.vars.tv`.
Hop 1: `ase::ui::design_window $key ifhidden` — the FIRST thing `ase::ui::do_run` does after the busy guard (ase_window.tcl:7391) whenever `ase::stack_level` says the design is not on this window's stack — leaves `[focus]` at `.` (the schematic editor toplevel), and `wm stackorder .ase4 isabove .` then returns 0. The line responsible is `catch {focus $tp}` at ase_window.tcl:6923, in `ase::ui::raise_window_entry`, which runs on BOTH raise arms including the deliberately-cheap `ifhidden` one that exists precisely so a visible window is 'left exactly where the user put it' (its own comment, :6889-6890) — the raise is careful and the focus grab is unconditional.
Hop 2: `ase::ui::run_started` (:7268-7274) calls `ase::ui::log_open`, which does a bare `toplevel $lw` (:7008) on every fresh run; the WM then grants the new toplevel the keyboard. Measured: focus goes from `.ase4.body.outs.tv` to `.ase4.logwin` — a `-state disabled` text widget with nothing to type into.
The house rule is explicit ('bring a window forward to show the user a message but never take the keyboard away from where they are working'), and issue 1389 already carries this as an open LOOK debt: 'raised but not focused is a claim about the user's own keyboard and only their eyes can settle it'. Note `ase::run_ciw_raise` (ase.tcl:6985-7008) gets it exactly right — it records who has the keyboard, raises only, and pays the focus only for a genuinely unmapped window. Two procs in the same feature, opposite policies.
- **Why it hurts.** Fifty runs a day means fifty times the keyboard silently relocates to a window the user did not ask for. Because there is also no focus ring (see no-focus-ring), the user cannot even see where it went — the next thing they type goes to the schematic canvas, where bare letters are destructive editing chords (`keybindings.csv`: p places a pin, l adds a wire label, d is deselect mode, n netlists).
- **Fix.** Lift rdw's mechanism verbatim — it was written for exactly this case and is 25 lines. `rdw::_focus_handback` (rdw.tcl:4522-4546) arms a `focus_pending` flag, catches the WM's asynchronous grant on `<FocusIn>` of the toplevel (bound at rdw.tcl:2850), and hands the keyboard back; `rdw::_focus_click` (bound at rdw.tcl:2858) spends the pending hand-back without moving anything when the user clicks INTO the window, which is the discriminator between 'the WM gave me focus' and 'the user came here on purpose'. Apply it to `$top.logwin`. Separately, give `ase::ui::raise_window_entry` a `focus_mode` parameter the way it already has `raise_mode`: `Session > Design Window` (the user's documented recovery — it SHOULD focus) keeps today's behaviour; `do_run`'s `ifhidden` call passes `nofocus` and drops the `catch {focus $tp}`.
- **Risk.** `raise_window_entry` has three callers (Session > Design Window, select_on_design/direct_plot, and the wave viewer's browser descend, per the comment at :6935-6940) — changing the default would change all three, so the new argument must default to today's behaviour and only `do_run` opt out. Issue 0616 is the standing landmine here: this proc's raise semantics were fixed once already because a run made the schematic window vanish under WSLg, and its comments say in terms 'do not simplify these two lines away' — the FOCUS line is not one of those two, but touching this proc at all wants test_ase_window.tcl W6/W6m (the private-bindtag `<Unmap>` counter on `.`) re-run on :0 as well as Xvfb. This pays down issue 1389's open look debt, so it needs the user's eyes, not a green suite.
- **ADE-L.** ADE-L's simulation output window appears without stealing the keyboard from the ADE-L form; you keep typing where you were. And ADE-L never moves your keyboard into the schematic when you press Run.

## [MAJOR] Nothing in ASE-L ever shows where the keyboard focus is — the highlight ring is switched off application-wide

- **id** `no-focus-ring`  ·  **effort** trivial
- **Evidence.** MEASURED: `.ase4.strip.netrun -highlightthickness = 0`, `.ase4.tb.temp -highlightthickness = 0` (probe4.out). Source located: src/xschem.tcl:19099 `option add *highlightThickness 0 startupFile` in the light-theme arm and :19117 in the dark arm — an application-wide option ASE-L inherits for every classic Tk widget it creates: all eight strip buttons, the temperature entry, and every OK/Cancel/Add/Edit/Remove/Close button in all 17 dialogs. VISUAL CONFIRMATION: scratchpad/l9/focus_button.png was captured with `[focus]` verified as `.ase4.strip.netrun`, and the `N&>` button is pixel-identical to the seven around it. The one exception is the three panes, where ttk's Treeview paints the selected row in the palette's `selectbg #4a6984` — visible in focus_tree.png with `START` highlighted — so the panes accidentally show focus and nothing else does.
- **Why it hurts.** There is a 15-stop Tab chain in this window (measured) and it is completely invisible. A user who presses Tab has no way to find out where they are except by pressing Space and seeing what happens — which, four Tab stops in, deletes rows (see tab-order-hands-you-the-loaded-gun). It also makes every other keyboard improvement in this report only half-useful: you can navigate, but you cannot see.
- **Fix.** Do NOT change src/xschem.tcl:19099 — that is the whole program's look and out of ASE-L's scope. Scope it inside `ase::ui::apply_theme` (:224-251) instead: in the `Button` / `Entry` / `Checkbutton` arms add `-highlightthickness 1` and `-highlightcolor <c>`, which overrides the option-database default for ASE-L's tree only. For the ttk side, the panes already read correctly; the ttk `default` theme is in use (measured) and `Ase.Treeview` could additionally get a `-focuscolor` map if the user wants the pane border to change too.
- **Risk.** THE RING COLOUR IS A PALETTE DECISION AND THE PALETTE IS USER-LOCKED (ase_window.tcl:167-174) — do not pick one. The two candidates already in the dict are `accent #8b0000` (the colour the Labelframe titles use) and `selectbg #4a6984` (the colour the pane selection uses); `#4a6984` is the more conventional focus colour and reuses a value already meaning 'this is where you are'. Either way it is a `rule` debt, not a choice to make here. No suite reads `-highlightthickness` (`grep -rn highlightthickness tests/headless/` — nothing). W1's 'themed widgets (locked palette + named fonts)' rows in test_ase_window.tcl assert `-background` and `-font` on the ASE widgets, not highlight options, so adding one option to the same `switch` arms moves nothing — verify against the live W1 row list before shipping.
- **ADE-L.** ADE-L, like every Motif/Qt form, draws a focus indicator. A window where Tab moves an invisible cursor is not something ADE-L asks of a user.

## [MAJOR] Tab×4 then Space silently deletes the selected rows, with no confirmation, no undo, and no visible focus to warn you — the destructive action strip sits at the FRONT of the tab chain, before any data

- **id** `tab-order-hands-you-the-loaded-gun`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** MEASURED tab chain from the ASE-L toplevel (probe.out): 1 `.tb.temp` (Entry) → 2-9 the eight strip buttons in order `ana var out del netrun run stop plot` → 10 `.body.vars.tv` → 11 `.body.vars.sb` → 12 `.body.ana.tv` → 13 `.body.ana.sb` → 14 `.body.outs.tv` → 15 `.body.outs.sb` → wraps. So the FOURTH stop is `.ase4.strip.del`, the `X` button, and the SIXTH is `.ase4.strip.netrun`, the `N&>` button (measured: 'Tab presses from toplevel to reach N&> : 6'). Classic Tk Button's class bindings include `<Key-space>` (measured: `<<Invoke>> <Button-1> <ButtonRelease-1> <Enter> <Key-space> <Leave>`), so a focused strip button fires on Space. `ase::ui::delete_selection` (:991-1014) deletes without any confirmation, and ASE-L ships no undo and no Revert — test_ase_window.tcl's own W1m row is titled 'Session menu entries (v2, no Revert)', and `ase::ui::revert_state` (:6825) has no menu door. So the loss is unrecoverable short of `Session > Load State`, which discards every unsaved edit in the session. Meanwhile the three SCROLLBARS are focusable (`tk::FocusOK $top.body.vars.sb` → 1, measured) and burn three of the fifteen stops on widgets a keyboard user never wants, and the panes — the actual content — are stops 10, 12 and 14.
- **Why it hurts.** The tab order is exactly backwards for the way the window is used: the data the user came to edit is last and the irreversible verbs are first. Combined with the invisible focus ring, a user who tabs by reflex to 'get into the table' walks straight through Delete and Netlist-and-Run with no visual warning at any point.
- **Fix.** Three independent moves, all one-line-ish.
(a) `-takefocus 0` on the three pane scrollbars in `ase::ui::build_pane` (:855) — a keyboard user scrolls with the treeview's own Prior/Next/Up/Down, never with the scrollbar.
(b) `-takefocus 0` on the eight strip buttons (:757-778). Once the Tier-1 chords and the palette exist, the strip is a mouse affordance and does not need to be in the keyboard chain at all; that makes the FIRST stop after the temperature entry the Design Variables pane, which is what a user actually wants. If the user prefers the strip stay reachable, the alternative is to reorder the `pack` so `$top.body` is created before `$top.strip` — but the packing order is load-bearing for the layout (the item-05 lesson recorded at :755) so `-takefocus 0` is the safer instrument.
(c) Independent of tab order: `ase::ui::delete_selection` should route through the existing `ase::ui::confirm` (:4039) when the selection is more than one row, or ASE-L should grow the `Session > Revert` door that `revert_state` is already sitting behind.
- **Risk.** (a) is invisible to every suite. (b) changes the focus chain, which nothing asserts today — but it also means a user who has ONLY the keyboard loses the strip entirely until the chords ship, so (b) should land WITH finding no-accelerators-no-mnemonics, not before it. (c) adds a user-facing confirmation sentence and/or a new menu entry: both are user rulings, and W1m explicitly locks the Session menu as having no Revert, so adding one moves test_ase_window.tcl:789.
- **ADE-L.** ADE-L's outputs and variables tables support Delete with an undo behind it; the destructive verb is not the first thing keyboard traversal lands on.

## [MAJOR] Return does nothing in Load State, Select Results or Simulators — including in the very listbox Load State deliberately puts your cursor in

- **id** `return-dead-in-the-three-browsers`  ·  **effort** trivial
- **Evidence.** MEASURED on the live dialogs (probe.out, dialog section): `DLG .ase4.loadst … esc=<destroy .ase4.loadst> ret=<>`, `DLG .ase4.rsel … ret=<>`, `DLG .ase4.simdlg … ret=<>`. For Load State the code is complete at ase_window.tcl:6141-6144: the only bindings on the three listboxes are `<<ListboxSelect>>` on lib and cell plus `bind_dialog_esc` on the toplevel — no `<Return>`, and no `<Double-1>` either, where the equivalent Library Manager browser binds `<Double-1>` to `libmgr::open_view` on both its cell and view lists (library_manager.tcl:139-140). And `ase::ui::loadst_default_to_session` ends with `catch {focus $w.pw.view.lb}` (:6188) under a comment that says the reason is 'so the pick needs no mouse trip' — VISIBLE in uxshots/loadstate.png, where `debug_st1` carries the `-activestyle dotbox` ring showing the keyboard really is in that list. The user is deliberately handed a keyboard-navigable list they cannot act from: arrow to the view you want, press Return, nothing happens, reach for the mouse. `rsel_dialog` is the same shape one level up — `<Return>` exists only on `$w.path.e` (:3869) while the two result treeviews get `<Double-1>` (:3902) and no key at all, so a keyboard user in the Loaded list cannot select a result.
- **Why it hurts.** These are the two windows a designer uses to move between saved states and between result files — the navigation spine of a working session. Getting 90% of the way with the keyboard and then being forced to the mouse for the commit is worse than never offering the keyboard route, because the user learns to distrust it everywhere.
- **Fix.** `bind $w.pw.view.lb <Return> [list ase::ui::load_state_ok $key]` and `<Double-1>` to the same, matching libmgr's browser; likewise on `$w.pw.cell.lb`. For rsel, `bind $f.tv <Return> [list ase::ui::rsel_dblclick $key $which]` in `rsel_build_list` (:3896-3906) — the double-click path already exists and already does the right thing, so this is a keyboard twin of shipped behaviour and mints nothing new. For `simulators_dialog`, `<Return>` on its treeview should invoke `Edit…`, matching the `<Delete>` precedent below.
- **Risk.** None of these move a suite (no ASE suite reads the bindings on these widgets). The Load State case has one subtlety already recorded in the source at :6178-6182: the View column is deliberately left UNSELECTED so a stray OK cannot discard the session's edits for a state the user never chose — so the Return binding must be a no-op when `curselection` is empty, exactly the way `load_state_ok` already refuses an incomplete lib/cell/view. Do not 'fix' that by preselecting index 0; the comment rules that out explicitly.
- **ADE-L.** Every ADE-L library/cell/view browser commits on Return and on double-click. A browser that commits on neither is a restriction ADE-L does not have.

## [MINOR] Not one dialog has a default button: Return on a focused OK does nothing, and nothing on screen says which button Return would press

- **id** `no-default-button-anywhere`  ·  **effort** trivial
- **Evidence.** MEASURED across all 12 dialogs probed live: every OK/Select/Close button reports `default=<disabled>` — `.ase4.addvar.btns.proceed`, `.ase4.edout.btns.proceed`, `.ase4.chana.btns.proceed`, `.ase4.design.btns.proceed`, `.ase4.saveall.btns.proceed`, `.ase4.saveas.btns.proceed`, `.ase4.simdlg.btns.close`, `.ase4.loadst.b.ok`, `.ase4.rsel.btns.select`, `.ase4.models.btns.close`, `.ase4.simopt.btns.close` (probe.out). Compounding it: classic Tk Button has NO `<Key-Return>` class binding (measured: `<<Invoke>> <Button-1> <ButtonRelease-1> <Enter> <Key-space> <Leave>`) and `event info <<Invoke>>` is EMPTY — no sequence is bound to it — so a focused OK button responds only to Space. The `<Return>` coverage that does exist is per-widget and patchy: `ase::ui::dialog_buttons` (:1681-1691) centrally wires Escape for every scaffold dialog but wires no Return at all, so Return lives only on individual entries (:1704-1705, :1755-1756, :1835-1836, :4142, :4249-4250, :4369, :5058-5059, :5363, :6347) and on four toplevels (askclose :410, bus :1431, confirm :4054, save_all :5722). The consequence is that Return works in a form dialog right up until you Tab off the last entry onto a checkbutton, combobox or the OK button itself, and then dies.
- **Why it hurts.** Fill in a value, press Return: that is the reflex in every dialog anyone has ever used, and here whether it works depends on which widget happens to hold focus. The inconsistency is worse than uniform absence — the user cannot form a rule.
- **Fix.** Do it in the one place the Escape rule already lives, so future dialogs inherit it by construction exactly as they inherit Escape: in `ase::ui::dialog_buttons` (:1681), add `-default active` to the proceed button, `-default normal` to cancel, and `bind $w <Key-Return> $okcmd` on the toplevel (a `<Key-Return>` bound on the toplevel tag reaches every child, the same mechanism the `bind_dialog_esc` header at :1655-1657 documents). Do the same by hand for the three non-scaffold dialogs that lack it (`loadst`, `rsel`, `simdlg`) — see the previous finding. `-default active` also draws Tk's default ring, which is the only 'which button does Return press' affordance available without touching the locked palette.
- **Risk.** `-default active` adds ~2px of border around the OK button in every dialog, so any suite asserting a dialog's exact `wm geometry` or `winfo reqwidth` would move — grep before shipping; the ASE dialog suites assert widget paths and behaviour rather than pixel sizes, so this is probably clean. A toplevel `<Key-Return>` must NOT be added to a dialog containing a multi-line Text widget (none of the ASE-L dialogs do). And note the deliberate asymmetry to preserve: the ASE MAIN window and the LOG window stay Escape-unbound by design and are LOCKED by test — test_ase_dialogs.tcl:1275-1276 (GE14, 'session toplevel has no Escape binding') and :1295 (GE15, 'log window has no Escape binding'). Do not extend any of this to those two windows without a ruling.
- **ADE-L.** ADE-L dialogs have a default button and Return commits them.

## [MINOR] The three panes answer to no key but the arrows — no Return to edit, no Delete to delete, no Shift+F10 for the context menu — while the Model Files list one file over already binds Delete

- **id** `pane-rows-have-no-keys`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** Live instance bindings on every pane treeview are exactly `<<TreeviewSelect>> <Button-1> <Button-3> <Double-Button-1>` (measured on `.ase4.body.ana.tv`); `ase::ui::build_pane` (:843-891) adds nothing else. Arrow navigation DOES work — measured, `<Key-Down>` moved the selection from item 0 to item 1 and moved `[$tv focus]` with it — so a keyboard user can get to the right row and then has nowhere to go. Meanwhile the SAME FILE binds `<Delete>` on the Model Files / Simulation Options list at ase_window.tcl:5309 (`bind $w.tv <Delete> [list ase::ui::listdlg_delete $key $which]`), so Delete removes a row in one table of ASE-L and does nothing in the other three. The three verbs are all present in the panes' own `<Button-3>` context menus (`Add… / Edit… / Delete`, built at :866-890) and the menu itself has no keyboard door: nothing binds `<Shift-F10>` or `<Key-Menu>`, the two standard ways to post a context menu from the keyboard.
- **Why it hurts.** Small, constant friction: every row edit and every row delete is a mouse round trip even though the user is already sitting on the row with the keyboard. And the internal inconsistency (Delete works in one ASE-L table, not in three others) is the kind of thing that teaches a user to stop trying.
- **Fix.** In `ase::ui::build_pane`, three bindings that are all keyboard twins of already-shipped mouse gestures, so none of them mints a new behaviour or a new sentence:
  `bind $pf.tv <Delete> [list ase::ui::delete_selection $key]`            (twin of the `X` strip button and the ctx `Delete`; matches :5309)
  `bind $pf.tv <Key-Return>` → the row's edit dialog                        (twin of `<Double-1>` at :863)
  `bind $pf.tv <Shift-F10>` and `<Key-Menu>` → `pane_ctx_post` at the focused row's screen coords (twin of `<Button-3>` at :888)
The `<Key-Return>` half must coexist with the checkbox toggle from finding outputs-checkboxes-mouse-only — one handler that dispatches on which column the row's focus sits in.
- **Risk.** `<Delete>` on a pane inherits the no-confirm, no-undo hazard from tab-order-hands-you-the-loaded-gun and should land after (or with) whatever confirmation the user rules on. `pane_ctx_post` currently takes `%X %Y` root coordinates from the button event; the keyboard path must synthesise them from `[$tv bbox [$tv focus]]` plus `winfo rootx/rooty`, which is a real (small) code addition rather than a rebind. No suite reads these bindings.
- **ADE-L.** ADE-L's setup tables take Delete on the selected row and Return to open the row editor.

## [POLISH] Ctrl+Shift+W already closes the ASE-L session, spent on a duplicate of Ctrl+W, and the same in the log window

- **id** `ctrl-shift-w-spent-on-a-duplicate`  ·  **effort** trivial
- **Evidence.** ase_window.tcl:322-323 binds BOTH `<Control-w>` and `<Control-W>` to `ase::ui::close_request`, and :7015-7016 does the same pair for the log window. On X11 `<Control-W>` is Control plus the keysym `W`, which requires Shift — so the second binding is not a caps-lock guard for the first, it is a live Ctrl+Shift+W accelerator. Confirmed on the live window: `bind top:` returns `<Control-Key-W> <Control-Key-w> <FocusIn>` as two distinct sequences. The sibling waveform viewer does not do this — it binds `<Control-Key-w>` only (wave_viewer.tcl:14911-14912) and gives Ctrl+Q its own separate verb.
- **Why it hurts.** Barely at all today, but it silently removes Ctrl+Shift+W from the pool before any keymap is designed, and it means a user reaching for a 'close everything' or 'close the viewer' variant lands on 'discard-prompt and close this session' instead. Worth knowing before the chord table in finding no-accelerators-no-mnemonics is drawn up.
- **Fix.** Either drop the `<Control-W>` half (Tk's caps-lock case is handled by the X server's keysym mapping, not by binding both cases — a Caps-Lock'd Ctrl+w still delivers keysym `w` unless Shift is physically held), or keep it and record that Ctrl+Shift+W is taken. Decide it as part of the keymap, not separately.
- **Risk.** test_ase_window.tcl W6c drives the log window's Ctrl-W close through a focus-gated `send_return`-style helper (:2831-2846) and would need checking if the `<Control-W>` half is removed — read which of the two sequences the suite generates before touching either line.


---

# cross-window  (10 findings)

**Headline.** Six of the seven windows in this application were written by the same author in the last four months, and every one of them invented its own font policy — the process now carries seven named fonts in two families, ASE-L is the only window that renders in Nimbus Sans while all five siblings render in DejaVu, and merely opening ASE-L changes the dropdown font of 39 comboboxes it does not own.

**The one slick move.** Delete the words `Arial` and `Courier` from `ase::theme` (src/ase_window.tcl:185-191) and derive the three Ase* fonts from `TkDefaultFont` / `TkFixedFont` the way RDW, the Library Manager, the CIW and the property form ALL already do. It is one edit at three lines, and it converges five windows at once — because every other new window in this tree already anchors on those two fonts, the family split is created by exactly one file. It also stops being a portability guess: `wave_viewer.tcl:9273` already records that "Arial 13 is 19.93 px here and something else on the next machine", and the machine that matters (the user's Windows X server, a different server from `:99`) has never been measured. And it hands the whole application one knob — `font configure TkDefaultFont -size N` would then rescale ASE-L, RDW, the CIW, the Library Manager and the property form together, which is what RDW's `aA` button already does for one window and what ADE-L does for the whole session. Measured cost in tests: NO suite in all 397 hardcodes Arial, Courier or Nimbus (the one `Courier` hit is `gui_gate_widget.tcl:228`, the gate panel, not a test), and the single rowheight assertion `test_wave_sigbrowser_sea.tcl:682` is already derived as `[font metrics AseEntryFont -linespace] + 4`, so it follows the change instead of fighting it. It needs a user ruling because the families and sizes are written into `doc/claude/specs/ase_l.md:876-880`, and because linespace 19 -> 17 shrinks ASE-L's rows and its natural 798x502.

## [CRITICAL] The ASE-L spec forbids Tk defaults; the Calculator spec mandates them. The incoherence is written down, not accidental.

- **id** `two-specs-opposite-orders`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** doc/claude/specs/ase_l.md:876-880: "Fonts: named-font pattern ... AseLabelFont Arial 10 bold ... AseEntryFont Arial 13 ... AseMonoFont Courier 13 ... No widget left on Tk defaults." Against doc/claude/specs/calculator.md:365-367 (R113, as amended by RULING-1): "Fonts stay stock: nothing in the tree themes fonts for a new dialog ... and ASE's named fonts are ASE's." Both windows obey their own spec exactly. Measured live in one process: ASE-L's 49 Menu widgets render AseLabelFont/Nimbus Sans 10 bold; the Calculator's 14 Menu widgets render TkMenuFont/DejaVu Sans 10 normal — and both paint the same #f2f2f2. Visible in uxshots/coherence.png: ASE-L's pane titles (Design Variables / Analyses / Outputs) and the Calculator's (Selectors / Buffer / Stack / Functions / Keypad) are the SAME dark red at DIFFERENT weights and families, six inches apart on one desktop.
- **Why it hurts.** The two windows are open side by side for the whole of a bandgap trim session: read a value in ASE-L's Outputs pane, push it into the Calculator, read it back. They share a palette, so the eye expects one application; the type says otherwise, and the effect is that the Calculator reads as somebody else's tool bolted on. No amount of code review fixes this because no code is wrong — both files are correct against their instructions.
- **Fix.** Not a code fix. Amend one of the two spec clauses so they say the same thing, then the code follows. Recommended direction: amend calculator.md R113's "fonts stay stock" to "fonts come from the shared reader", because R113's stated reason is ase::theme's SIDE EFFECTS (see combobox-font-leak below), not a preference for DejaVu — remove the side effect and the reason evaporates.
- **Risk.** NEEDS A USER RULING — this is a spec change, and R113 was already amended once by RULING-1 (doc/claude/calculator_batch/LEDGER.md), so the user owns this text. No suite moves on the doc edit alone.
- **ADE-L.** ADE-L, the Virtuoso Calculator and the waveform viewer render in one skin with one font; there is no per-window font policy to disagree about.

## [CRITICAL] ASE-L is the one window that names a font family; the other five derive from TkDefaultFont/TkFixedFont, so ASE-L alone renders in Nimbus Sans while everything else renders in DejaVu.

- **id** `arial-courier-the-only-named-family`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** Measured live on :99 in a single process, all windows open at once (script at scratchpad/m3.tcl). Seven named fonts exist: AseLabelFont -> Nimbus Sans 10 bold (linespace 15); AseEntryFont -> Nimbus Sans 13 (19); AseMonoFont -> Nimbus Mono PS 13 (19); RdwPaneFont -> DejaVu Sans Mono 10 (17); RdwHdrFont -> DejaVu Sans Mono 10 bold (17); CiwFont -> DejaVu Sans Mono 12 (20); LibMgrBold -> DejaVu Sans 10 bold (17). Sources: ase_window.tcl:185-191 spells `-family Arial` / `-family Courier` as literals; rdw.tcl:2600-2602 does `font create $name {*}[font configure TkFixedFont]`; ciw.tcl:396 uses `-family Monospace`; library_manager.tcl:94 does `font create LibMgrBold {*}[font actual TkDefaultFont]`; property_form.tcl:336-342 derives slickPropLabel/Value/Header/Hint from TkDefaultFont/TkFixedFont. Arial and Courier are not installed here; on the user's real display (a different X server — the HC-Consult Windows server, per CLAUDE.md's three-server table) they probably ARE, so the split is bigger there and has never been measured. Visible in uxshots/coherence.png: ASE-L's chrome is uniformly bold and slightly narrower than the Calculator's and the Library Manager's regular DejaVu right beside it.
- **Why it hurts.** This is the thing the user actually reported. A designer with ASE-L, the waveform viewer, the RDW and the schematic all mapped sees four windows in three type treatments; nothing tells the eye they are one program. Worse, it is one-directional damage: five windows already agree with each other, and ASE-L is the outlier — the tool the user is in most of the day is the one that looks foreign.
- **Fix.** In ase::theme (src/ase_window.tcl:185-191) replace `-family Arial` / `-family Courier` with the base font's own spec: `font create AseLabelFont {*}[font configure TkDefaultFont] -weight bold`, `font create AseEntryFont {*}[font configure TkDefaultFont]`, `font create AseMonoFont {*}[font configure TkFixedFont]`. Keep the three NAMES (49 sites and 6 suites reference them). The sizes 10/13/13 are a separate decision — see the risk note.
- **Risk.** NEEDS A USER RULING: the families and sizes are written into doc/claude/specs/ase_l.md:876-880, and the palette-adjacent look is USER-LOCKED. Test exposure is measured and small: no suite in all 397 hardcodes Arial/Courier/Nimbus; test_ase_window.tcl:548-556 only asserts the names exist and that .tb.temp uses AseEntryFont (survives); test_results_dialog.tcl:791 and test_wave_sigsearch.tcl:1450 name the fonts, not their families (survive); test_wave_sigbrowser_sea.tcl:677-682 asserts rowheight is DERIVED from `font metrics AseEntryFont -linespace` (follows automatically). The real risk is pixels: AseEntryFont linespace 19 -> 17 shrinks Ase.Treeview -rowheight (ase_window.tcl:203) and ASE-L's natural 798x502, so the shape in every uxshot moves and this is a look debt (owed.sh add look).

## [MAJOR] Opening ASE-L rewrites the dropdown font of every combobox in the application, one-way and process-global — coherence damage in the direction nobody wants.

- **id** `combobox-font-leak`  ·  **effort** medium
- **Evidence.** ase_window.tcl:194: `option add *TCombobox*Listbox.font AseEntryFont`, executed by ase::theme, which every ASE window build calls. MEASURED LIVE (scratchpad/m5.tcl, :99): before ASE-L opens, a ttk::combobox popdown reports font `TkTextFont` (DejaVu Sans 10, linespace 17); after `ase::open_state sky130_tests_ase tb_bandgap ngspice_state1`, a newly created combobox's popdown reports `AseEntryFont` (Nimbus Sans 13, linespace 19) — +11.8% row height, and it never reverts, because an `option add` cannot be undone. There are 45 `ttk::combobox` call sites in src/*.tcl; 6 are in ase_window.tcl, so 39 belong to windows ASE-L does not own (xschem.tcl 17, calculator.tcl 9, wave_viewer.tcl 6, library_manager.tcl 3, copy_form.tcl 2, property_form.tcl 1).
- **Why it hurts.** The Library Manager's library picker and xschem's own Preferences dropdowns silently change size and family depending on whether the designer happened to open a simulation window first — the same dialog looks different in two sessions with no user action in between. And this single line is the documented reason the Calculator refuses to share ANY of ASE's typography (calculator.tcl:119-130), so it is simultaneously the one thing ASE-L does impose on other windows and the reason it cannot share the things it should.
- **Fix.** Split ase::theme in two: a pure reader (`ase::fonts ?name?` — lazily `font create`s and returns the name, NO option add, no style writes) and an applier (`ase::theme_apply` — the ttk::style block plus the option add). Then move the `option add` from process scope to ASE's own widgets: set the popdown font per-combobox instead, which ttk supports via the widget's own `-font` plus `option add` scoped to the ASE toplevel path (`option add *ase*TCombobox*Listbox.font AseEntryFont`), or drop it entirely and let Ase.TCombobox carry it.
- **Risk.** test_calc_skeleton.tcl:145 and :405-408 MOVE — they take a baseline `ase_font_at_start` before any ASE code runs and then assert "S13 opening the Calculator created no ASE font". Those two rows are fencing the LEAK, not the font, so they should be re-expressed as "opening the Calculator changed no combobox popdown font" (which is what cbfont at :136 already measures). test_calc_widgets.tcl (10 palette refs) and test_wave_sigsearch.tcl:1450 read the applier's results and should survive if theme_apply keeps doing what theme did.

## [MAJOR] The waveform viewer is a single toplevel carrying both chrome languages at once — 25 stock menus beside 18 ASE menus, 30 stock buttons beside 4 ASE buttons.

- **id** `wave-viewer-two-chromes-one-window`  ·  **effort** large
- **Evidence.** Measured live, widget census of `.x1` alone (scratchpad/m3.tcl): Menu x25 TkMenuFont/DejaVu Sans 10/bg grey80 AND Menu x18 AseLabelFont/Nimbus Sans 10 bold/bg #f2f2f2; Button x30 TkDefaultFont/DejaVu/bg white AND Button x4 AseLabelFont/Nimbus/bg #f2f2f2; Entry x2 TkTextFont/bg PaleGreen AND Entry x2 AseEntryFont/bg #ffffff. Cause: the toplevel and its menubar/toolbar are built by xschem.tcl's own drawing-window code, while wave_viewer.tcl:9252 calls `ase::ui::apply_theme $f` on the signal-browser sidebar only (and the sidebar dialogs at :16233, :16683). Visible in uxshots/ase_root.png: the waveform window's menubar (File View Graph Cursors Options) is stock grey while the ASE-L window beside it is #f2f2f2 bold.
- **Why it hurts.** Plotting is the reason the designer opened ASE-L. The window they land in is the one that most obviously fails to be one thing — a themed sidebar hanging off unthemed chrome reads as a half-finished port, and it is the window a colleague sees first in a screen share.
- **Fix.** Do NOT chase this one to zero. The chrome belongs to xschem's window builder, which is upstream (src/xschem.tcl, added 2020-08-08) and shared with every schematic window; theming it means diverging the fork's most-merged file. The affordable half is to make the seam deliberate rather than accidental: either theme the wave viewer's OWN menubar cascades (the 18 ASE menus are already there, so the boundary is drawable), or accept the split and record it beside the existing precedent at ase_window.tcl:222-223 ("The shared textwindow viewer (Netlist > Display) deliberately stays stock: restyling it would restyle every non-ASE use").
- **Risk.** Any move here touches xschem.tcl's window build, which every schematic window shares — the blast radius is the whole application, not ASE-L. Suites: test_wave_tabs.tcl, test_wave_sigbrowser_0312/0318, test_wave_sigbrowser_sea.tcl, test_wave_sigsearch.tcl all read this window. Recommend a ruling that this seam stays, so it stops looking like an oversight.
- **ADE-L.** ViVA and ADE-L share one skin; there is no window in the Virtuoso session with two chrome languages inside it.

## [MAJOR] One dark red, three files, three spellings, hand-mirrored by comment — and it already cost a two-file bug fix this month.

- **id** `accent-8b0000-written-three-times`  ·  **effort** trivial
- **Evidence.** Measured live, all three resolve to #8b0000: `ase::palette accent` = #8b0000 (ase_window.tcl:168); `rdw::_notefg` = `dark red` (rdw.tcl, the proc is a one-liner `return {dark red}`); the CIW's tag at ciw.tcl:490 = `dark red`. ciw.tcl:488-489 says so out loud: "ONE DECISION, TWO WINDOWS: rdw::_notefg (src/rdw.tcl) exists to mirror this tag and says so in its own header, so it moved with it." Recent commit 19f8e351 is literally titled `fix(ciw,rdw): the note colour was a contrast ratio of 1.45 and the user could not read it` — one colour, two files, one commit. Separately, property_form.tcl:347 returns a FOURTH accent, `#d08000` amber (measured), for the modified-field dot in the dialog a designer opens on every instance edit.
- **Why it hurts.** Not a pixel problem — a drift problem that has already fired. The next accent change needs three coordinated edits and a comment to keep them together, and the failure mode is silent: two windows agree, the third quietly does not, and no check can see it because each file's value is locally correct. The amber is a real second visual identity in the flow the designer uses most.
- **Fix.** Add `notefg` to ase::palette as a named role (value unchanged, `#8b0000`), then `rdw::_notefg` returns `[ase::palette notefg]` and ciw.tcl:490 reads the same. Zero pixels move — measured identical. The amber #d08000 is a separate question for the user: is the property form part of this look or not?
- **Risk.** Zero pixel change (measured: `dark red` == #8b0000 == ase::palette accent). One suite interaction to check first: test_rdw_window_1245.tcl:4555-4584 temporarily REPLACES ::ase::palette with a fake to prove the window reads the palette — folding notefg in means that substitution would now reach notefg too, which is arguably what that row wants, but confirm the expected values at :4669 before the edit. The amber question NEEDS A USER RULING (it is a fourth colour identity, and the palette is USER-LOCKED).

## [MAJOR] The RDW paints its panels from the ASE palette and then leaves its own seven buttons stock grey — a mismatch inside the newest window in the tree.

- **id** `rdw-buttons-are-stock-grey-on-an-ase-panel`  ·  **effort** trivial
- **Evidence.** rdw.tcl:3093-3096: `frame .rdw.b -background [rdw::color panel]` (= #f2f2f2) then `::button .rdw.b.$id -text $label -width 8 -command ...` with NO -background and NO -font. Measured live: `.rdw` census shows Button x7 TkDefaultFont/DejaVu Sans 10/bg grey80, Label x1 /bg #f2f2f2 — grey80 resolves to #cccccc (measured), 38/255 darker than the #f2f2f2 frame they sit on. Source of the grey: xschem.tcl:19093 `option add *background {grey80} startupFile`. Visible in uxshots/coherence.png: the Up / Down / Delete / Add / Save / aA / Close column reads as a grey stripe against the lighter window.
- **Why it hurts.** The RDW is where OP annotation lands, so it is up beside ASE-L during every bias check. Its column of controls is the first thing the eye goes to and it is the one part of the window that did not get the theme — which makes the whole window read as unfinished even though its text pane, its accent and its selection colours are all correct.
- **Fix.** Add `-background [rdw::color panel] -activebackground [rdw::color panel]` to the button creation at rdw.tcl:3095 (and to the `aA` and `Close` buttons built just below it, which are deliberately outside rdw::_buttons per the comment at :1128). If the shared-font work lands, add the shared label font in the same line.
- **Risk.** Low. The RDW suites read button STATE and LABELS (test_rdw_window_1245.tcl rows CB1-CB3, LX12), and the FZ font rows read `.rdw.p.t`, not the buttons — grep test_rdw_*.tcl for `-background` before the edit to confirm no row golds grey80. Note the dark-colour-scheme case: xschem.tcl:19112-19122 sets a different option-database background, so hardcoding is what would be wrong; reading rdw::color panel is right.

## [MAJOR] RDW can be scaled 6..32 points with a dedicated button; ASE-L, the window the designer is in all day, has no size control at all and its sizes are literals.

- **id** `no-text-size-control-where-the-user-lives`  ·  **effort** medium
- **Evidence.** rdw.tcl:2276 `proc rdw::font_limits {} { return {6 32} }`, :2352 `rdw::set_font_size`, plus the `aA` button (visible in uxshots/coherence.png, bottom of the RDW column) and roughly 120 lines of geometry defence at :2660-2700 that keep the toplevel near 893x498 across the whole band. ciw.tcl:406 `ciw_set_font_size` (4..72), settable from an rc or the CIW. Grepped ase_window.tcl for `font_size|set_font|aA|Text Size`: ZERO hits. Same for calculator.tcl. ASE-L's sizes are the literals 10 / 13 / 13 at ase_window.tcl:185-191.
- **Why it hurts.** On a laptop panel or a 4K monitor, 10-point bold Nimbus in the Design Variables pane is the difference between reading a value and leaning in. The designer can fix the annotation-dump window and the command window and cannot fix the simulation window — which is precisely backwards, and it is the concrete daily version of "fonts could stand to improve".
- **Fix.** This is the second dividend of the family rebase (see slick_move): once AseLabelFont/AseEntryFont/AseMonoFont derive from TkDefaultFont/TkFixedFont, `font configure TkDefaultFont -size N` rescales ASE-L, the RDW, the CIW, the Library Manager and the property form together, and the per-window knobs become one application preference. RDW's band-and-clamp machinery (rdw::font_limits / _pane_chars) is the pattern to reuse, not to reinvent — but note RDW's is a private band over a private font; a shared setter is a different, simpler thing.
- **Risk.** NEEDS A USER RULING: an application-wide text size is a new user-visible control and, if it gets a menu entry, a new user-facing sentence. Also a geometry risk — rdw.tcl:2660-2690 records the measured failure (one step to size 20 took that window from 893x498 to 1757x914 before the clamp existed); ASE-L's three treeview panes would need the same defence or the same measurement.
- **ADE-L.** Virtuoso has one session-level font preference that moves every window at once; ADE-L does not have a per-window text-size button because it does not need one.

## [MINOR] OK and Cancel sit in three different places in three windows of the same workflow.

- **id** `three-ok-cancel-layouts`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** ase_window.tcl:1685-1688: `pack $w.btns.proceed -side left` / `pack $w.btns.cancel -side right` — OK at the far left edge, Cancel at the far right, the whole dialog width apart. Visible in uxshots/addvar.png. rdw.tcl:7131-7135: `pack $w.btns.cancel $w.btns.ok -side right` — the pair right-aligned and adjacent, Cancel outermost. library_manager.tcl:246-248: `pack $d.b.ok $d.b.cancel -side left` inside a centred gridded frame — the pair adjacent and centred. Three layouts, one application, all three written this year.
- **Why it hurts.** Muscle memory. A designer dismissing a run dialog, then a scope dialog, then a library dialog aims at three different targets; ASE-L's is the odd one because OK is nowhere near where the eye goes to commit. Not dangerous — OK is left of Cancel in all three, so the misclick is a cancel, not a destructive confirm — but it is the kind of small friction that makes a tool feel homemade.
- **Fix.** Pick one and put it in ase::ui::dialog_buttons (ase_window.tcl:1683-1694), then have the other two call it or copy it. The right-aligned adjacent pair (RDW's) is the one that matches the rest of the desktop and needs the least motion from a designer's hand at the bottom-right of a dialog.
- **Risk.** Suites that grid- or pack-check dialog buttons will move: grep tests/headless for `btns.proceed`, `btns.cancel`, `b.ok`, `b.cancel` before touching it — ASE-L's path names (`$w.btns.proceed`) are widely referenced, so change the PACKING and keep the paths. NEEDS A USER RULING on which convention wins.

## [MINOR] Seven windows, five title conventions — a taskbar of them cannot be grouped, and ASE-L never calls itself ASE-L.

- **id** `seven-windows-five-title-conventions`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** Read off the live windows: `Analog Sim Environment tb_bandgap` (ase_window.tcl:6728), `Results Display Window - the annotation list` (rdw.tcl:4227), `xschem Calculator` (calculator.tcl:448), `Library Manager` (library_manager.tcl:76), `xschem CIW - /tmp/Xschem.log.NN` (ciw.tcl:429), `Waveforms tb_bandgap (ngspice_state1)`, `xschem [3] - untitled.sch`. Three prefix the application, four do not; the separator is a hyphen in the RDW and the CIW, an em dash in ASE-L's own children (`Select Results — $cell` at :3832, `Simulation Log — <cell>` at :7009), a bracket-number in the schematic. And the window the user, the docs and the menus all call ASE-L is titled by an expansion nobody uses — against the house rule that acronyms are UPPERCASE.
- **Why it hurts.** Seven entries in a window list, no shared prefix, no way to tell at a glance which belong to the simulation session and which to the schematic. Cadence solves this by prefixing every window with the product and suffixing lib/cell/view, which is exactly what a designer with fifteen windows open needs.
- **Fix.** One convention, applied at the seven `wm title` sites: `<Window> — <lib> <cell> <view>` with a single separator, or an application prefix on all seven. For ASE-L specifically, `ASE-L tb_bandgap` reads correctly under the house style and is shorter than what is there now.
- **Risk.** NEEDS A USER RULING — every one of these is a user-facing string and a new sentence is the user's to ratify. Test exposure is real but shallow: suites match on titles (grep tests/headless for `wm title`, `Analog Sim Environment`, `Results Display Window`); the dirty-marker suite test_ase_dirty.tcl reads ASE-L's title composition at ase_window.tcl:6728-6731 and would move.

## [MINOR] The Library Manager is the one new window that reads no palette at all — and being pure ttk, it is also the cheapest window in the tree to bring in.

- **id** `library-manager-reads-no-palette-and-is-the-cheapest-to-fix`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** Measured live, `.libmgr` census: TFrame x4, TLabel x4, Treeview x3, TScrollbar x3, TButton x4, TCheckbutton x1 — every one on `(default <class>)` style, plus Menu x7 on TkMenuFont/grey80. grep of library_manager.tcl for `ase::palette|ase::theme|#[0-9a-f]{6}`: zero hits except one `-foreground red` error label at :1212. Its only typography is LibMgrBold (library_manager.tcl:94), derived from TkDefaultFont. Visible in uxshots/coherence.png: flat ttk buttons and plain column headers beside ASE-L's raised Tk buttons and red pane titles.
- **Why it hurts.** Low daily cost — it is a picker, opened and dismissed. It matters because it is where a shared look is nearly free: everything in it is ttk, so a handful of `ttk::style configure` lines on TFrame/TLabel/TButton/Treeview reach every widget, with no per-widget walk and no `apply_theme` recursion. It is the right first customer for a shared theme module, precisely because failure there costs nothing.
- **Fix.** Give it the ASE palette through named styles (Ase.TFrame / Ase.TLabel / Ase.TButton, plus the existing Ase.Treeview) rather than through ase::ui::apply_theme, which walks classic-Tk classes and would not reach ttk widgets anyway (ase_window.tcl:244 already records that ttk ignores `-configure`). Its 7 classic Tk menus need the panel background separately.
- **Risk.** Low, and this is the safe rehearsal for the whole migration. Suites: test_lib_manager_gui.tcl, test_lib_manager_bold.tcl (reads LibMgrBold on tracked rows), test_lib_manager_ctx.tcl, test_lib_browse.tcl — none of the ones grepped assert colours, but confirm before the edit. If the shared look lands here first and looks wrong, nothing important is disturbed.


---

# ade-l-gap  (14 findings)

**Headline.** ASE-L has ADE-L's silhouette — Design Variables / Analyses / Outputs, the right-hand eight-button strip, the four-segment status line — but under it the analysis forms are two free-text boxes plus a key/value bag whose keys are shown back to you and then dropped from the deck, the Outputs Value column only fills for a run that happened in this process, and the window never says which library it is simulating.

**The one slick move.** Put real icons on the eight action-strip buttons. It is the only always-visible graphic in the window, it is the exact place ASE-L's silhouette diverges from ADE-L's — ADE-L's identical eight-action right-hand toolbar is icons, in the same order — and `N&>` / `!` / `~` are the three most important actions in the program rendered as ASCII nobody can read cold. The spec already blesses it (doc/claude/specs/ase_l.md:906, "real icons welcome where Tk can do them"), it needs no new dependency (inline base64 via `image create photo -data` in Tk 8.6), and if `-text` is left in place beside `-image` the one test row that pins the labels (test_ase_window.tcl:1391) does not move — so it is the largest change in how the window FEELS available at the smallest change in what it IS. Chosen over the two findings I rank higher: the dropped analysis options and the dead Value column are correctness defects that change what the window can be trusted to say, not how it looks. Watch the landmine — `-width 5` silently means 5 PIXELS once a button carries an image.

## [CRITICAL] Analysis Options… accepts, displays and saves options the deck never emits

- **id** `analysis-options-shown-then-dropped`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** src/ase_window.tcl:4210-4212 comment on chana_options: "Extra keys round-trip through the state file and show in the Arguments summary (arg_summary's unknown-key arm); DECK emission of extra keys stays deferred (v1 limit, documented here)." The emitter, src/ase.tcl:10962-10966, reads only the quick fields: `dc [source] [start] [stop] [step]` / `ac dec [points] [start] [stop]` / `tran [step] [stop]`. arg_summary (src/ase_window.tcl:1080-1084) prints every non-type/enabled key into the Analyses pane's Arguments column. chana_opt_tran.png / chana_opt_dc.png / chana_opt_ac.png are three screenshots of the SAME generic Name/Value table with Add and Delete.
- **Why it hurts.** A designer types `uic` on the tran of a bandgap so it starts from zero, presses OK, sees `uic=` confirmed back in the Arguments column of the main window, runs, and gets a run that started from the DC solution — no warning, no diagnostic, and the pane agreeing with them. The whole point of an Options escape hatch is that it is the place you go when the quick fields are not enough; here it is the one control in the program that lies about what the simulator was asked.
- **Fix.** Two acceptable ends. Minimum honest: in render_deck's per-type arms, append the row's non-{type,enabled,quick-field} keys to the analysis line (bare key -> token, key=value -> `key=value`), the way the `.options` emitter at src/ase.tcl:10564-10566 already does. Better: replace chana_options' generic bag with a per-type field set (see ac-sweep-type-hardcoded) and keep the bag only for genuinely unknown keys. Either way, an option that is not emitted must be refused at OK, not stored.
- **Risk.** tests/headless/test_ase_dialogs.tcl exercises chana_options round-trip through state; a deck-shape change will move any golden netlist under tests/headless/gold that carries an analysis with extra keys — grep for `tran ` / `ac dec ` in the goldens first. Adding emission changes user-visible simulator behaviour for any state file already carrying extra keys, so which keys become live is a user ruling.
- **ADE-L.** ADE-L's Choosing Analyses > Options opens a per-analysis simulator-options form (spectre/aps tran options, ac options, dc options) and every field on it lands in the netlist. There is no state in ADE-L where the form shows a setting the deck does not carry.

## [CRITICAL] The Outputs Value column is blank whenever the numbers came from anywhere but a run in this process

- **id** `outputs-value-dead-unless-run-in-process`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** ase_main.png: five Outputs rows (VBG, ENN, START, Icc, TEMPERAT), Value column empty for all five. logwin.png, same bench, same state: `VBG = 1.177085e+00`, `EN_N = 0.000000e+00`, `START = 1.803088e+00`, `i(VCC) = 2.895431e-05`, `TEMPERAT = 2.700000e+01`, `=== exit 0 after 8.12 s ===`. rselect.png shows tb_bandgap_ase.raw (tran) bound and loaded. The `results` session attr has exactly ONE writer in the tree: src/ase_window.tcl:7245, inside run_finished's `ec == 0` arm, with the comment at :7242 "display-only, never serialized to the state file". rsel_commit (src/ase_window.tcl:3549-3614) calls results::select, regenerates the viewer and writes a status sentence, and never touches `results` or calls refresh_output_values.
- **Why it hurts.** Reopen yesterday's bench and the one number you came for — VBG = 1.177 — is on disk, is in the log two clicks away, and the pane that exists to show it is empty. Bind a raw with Results > Select and it is still empty. So the Outputs pane, which is a third of the window, is dead furniture for every session that did not just finish a run, and the designer goes to the log window to read numbers out of ngspice's own output.
- **Fix.** Fill `results` from the bound result as well as from a live run: in rsel_commit, after a non-refused results::select, re-derive the scalar map and call ase::ui::refresh_output_values. Two sources are available without new machinery — the run log beside the raw (result_probe already takes a log text, src/ase.tcl:11232) or the raw's op plot read through the existing results:: layer. Same call on state load when a raw and its log are present.
- **Risk.** tests/headless/test_ase_window.tcl W1p asserts the Value cell is BLANK pre-run (`W1p id Value blank before run`) — that row must be re-read as "blank when no result is bound", not deleted. Which analysis's scalars the column reports is already a settled user ruling (issue 1243: the operating point); a raw-side reader must honour it or it re-opens that ruling.
- **ADE-L.** Results > Select is the ADE-L gesture for exactly this: point it at a results directory and the Outputs pane's Value column repopulates from those results without re-running. Reopening a state and selecting last night's results to read the numbers is a daily ADE-L move.

## [MAJOR] The action strip is eight text placeholders where ADE-L's identical strip is icons

- **id** `strip-is-eight-ascii-glyphs`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** src/ase_window.tcl:756 — "right vertical action strip (spec \"Action strip\"): text placeholders"; :762-779 build eight classic Tk buttons `-width 5` with -text `OP,TR`, `=`, `-->`, `X`, `N&>`, `>`, `!`, `~`. ase_main.png right edge and ase_large.png right edge show them. doc/claude/specs/ase_l.md:905-906 already sanctions the fix: "Action strip (right vertical panel; text placeholders for now — real icons welcome where Tk can do them, e.g. unicode ▶ ■)".
- **Why it hurts.** This is the only always-visible graphic in the window and it is the one place ASE-L's silhouette diverges from ADE-L's. `N&>` and `!` are not readable as Netlist-and-Run and Stop by anyone who has not been told; issue 1391 conceded exactly that and answered it with hover tooltips, which is the answer for an icon, not a substitute for one. A designer coming from ADE-L reaches for the green arrow and finds `>`.
- **Fix.** Add `-image` to each of the eight buttons with an inline base64 GIF/PNG built by `image create photo -data` (Tk 8.6, no new dependency, no asset files). Keep `-text` exactly as it is: with `-compound none` (the default) Tk displays the image and `cget -text` still returns the glyph, so the one test row that pins the labels does not move. ⚠ LANDMINE: `-width 5` is CHARACTERS for a text button and SCREEN UNITS for an image button — leaving it makes every button 5 px wide. Set a pixel width (~28) in the same change.
- **Risk.** tests/headless/test_ase_window.tcl:1391 `W1s strip buttons in order` reads `[$b cget -text]` and expects `{OP,TR = --> X N&> > ! ~}` — unchanged by the -image approach. W1s2/W1s2b (strip_tips covers every packed button, both ways) unchanged. Drawing eight glyphs is a pixel deliverable: record a `look` debt rather than calling it done on a green suite.
- **ADE-L.** ADE-L's right-hand toolbar is the same eight actions in the same order as icons — analyses, variables, outputs, delete, netlist-and-run, run, stop, plot — and they are the tool's visual signature.

## [MAJOR] The window never says which library or view it is simulating

- **id** `no-design-identity-in-the-window`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** refresh_title, src/ase_window.tcl:6728: `set t "Analog Sim Environment [ase::ui::design_cell_name $key]"` — cell only. refresh_status, :6749-6754, lassigns lib/cell/view out of `meta` and then renders only `State: $view`; `$lib` is read and discarded. ase_root.png title bar: "Analog Sim Environment tb_bandgap". ase_large.png: at 1500x900 the top-left corner above Design Variables is empty white. The library exists only inside Setup > Design… (designdlg.png) and Session > Save State (savestate.png).
- **Why it hurts.** Three ASE-L windows on one screen, three benches called tb_bandgap in three libraries — sky130_tests_ase, a corner copy, a colleague's — and nothing on any of them tells you which. In a PDK-swap or a library-fork workflow that is the mistake that costs a night. ADE-L answers it without a click.
- **Fix.** Two parts, both label-only. (1) refresh_title: `Analog Sim Environment (N) — <lib> <cell> <view>`, matching ADE-L's own title shape and the window number already in the status bar. (2) A `Design` box of three Label rows — Library / Cell / View — in the dead space at the top of the left column, fed from the `meta` dict refresh_status already lassigns.
- **Risk.** ⚠ THE SPEC SAYS THREE PANES AND A TEST ENFORCES IT. doc/claude/specs/ase_l.md:882 "Panes (ONLY these three; log pane REMOVED)", and tests/headless/test_ase_window.tcl:1354-1357 `W1p exactly the three v2 panes` names `$top.body.mods`/`opts`/`setup` as forbidden. A `$top.body.design` frame passes that literal check but contradicts the spec sentence — this needs a user ruling before it is built. ⚠ AND :1362 `W1p no inline add/del buttons under the panes` fails on ANY Button widget under $top.body, so the box must be labels only (no browse `…` button there). The title change moves any suite grepping wm title — grep tests/headless for `Analog Sim Environment`.
- **ADE-L.** ADE-L's title bar is `Virtuoso Analog Design Environment (N) — <lib> <cell> <view>`, and a permanent Design box at the top-left of the body shows Library / Cell / View as three static rows above Design Variables. Both are always visible; neither costs a click.

## [MAJOR] No linear or octave AC sweep, no tran start time, maxstep or uic — the analysis line is a 3-to-5 token template

- **id** `analysis-line-is-a-fixed-template`  ·  **effort** medium
- **Evidence.** src/ase.tcl:10964-10966, verbatim: `ac dec [dict get $a points] [start] [stop]` — `dec` is a literal, never read from state; `tran [step] [stop]` — two tokens, where ngspice's syntax is `tran tstep tstop <tstart <tmax>> <uic>`. chana_fields, src/ase_window.tcl:4072-4079, is the whole field set: dc {source start stop step}, ac {points start stop}, tran {step stop}. doc/claude/specs/ase_l.md:55 still carries `dec 1` in the schema example — a key nothing reads. Escape via Options… is closed by the finding above.
- **Why it hurts.** A linear AC sweep is how you look at a narrow band around a pole; an octave sweep is how you read an audio-band filter. Neither is reachable. `uic` is how a startup-circuit or oscillator bench is run at all. tstart is how you skip the first 10 us of a 1 ms settling run so the raw is not 700 MB. Every one of those is a routine analog move and none of them can be expressed in this window at any price short of hand-editing the .state file.
- **Fix.** Give chana_fields a per-type field set that matches what the backend can emit, and render it as a real form: AC gets a sweep-type control (dec/oct/lin) beside Points, TRAN gets Start and Max Step entries plus a `Skip initial DC (UIC)` checkbutton, DC keeps its four. Then teach the three render_deck arms to emit the optional tokens in ngspice's positional order, omitting trailing ones that are empty.
- **Risk.** New user-facing labels and a new checkbutton caption — house rule says a new sentence is the user's to ratify, so the field names need a ruling before they ship. chana_show's deterministic widget paths `$w.<field>` are read by tests/headless/test_ase_dialogs.tcl (G-series); new fields add paths rather than move them, but the row-numbering (grid rows 2..) and the fixed Options/OK rows at 8/9 will need re-checking. Deck-shape change: re-check any tests/headless gold carrying `ac dec` or `tran `.
- **ADE-L.** ADE-L's Choosing Analyses is a different form per analysis type: tran gets Stop Time / accuracy defaults / transient noise; ac and dc get Sweep Variable, Sweep Range (Start-Stop or Center-Span) and Sweep Type (Automatic / Linear / Logarithmic with Points Per Decade or Number of Steps). Not one of those is a free-text box, and none of them is missing.

## [MAJOR] Clicking another analysis radio silently throws away what you just typed, and there is no Apply

- **id** `chana-discards-on-type-switch`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** src/ase_window.tcl:4098-4100 comment: "switching repopulates the bottom form from state (D4: in-form edits of the previous type are DISCARDED — deterministic, no hidden multi-type writes)". chana_show (:4124-4145) destroys `$w.<field>` for all five field names and re-inserts from `ase::state_get $row $f`; chana_ok (:4152) is the only writer. analyses.png shows the dialog's whole button set: Options…, OK, Cancel. No Apply.
- **Why it hurts.** Type `stop 500u` into tran, click `ac` to remind yourself what the frequency range was, click back to `tran` — the 500u is gone and nothing said so. And because there is no Apply, setting up an op and a tran in one sitting is two full open-edit-OK-reopen cycles through a 249x126 dialog. In ADE-L you keep Choosing Analyses open, fill in each type, and press Apply between them.
- **Fix.** Cache the form's current field values per type in `dlg($key,anform,<type>)` on every radio -command before rebuilding, and prefer that cache over state when re-showing a type within one dialog lifetime; drop the cache in chana_cancel/chana_ok as the other per-key dlg records are dropped. Add an Apply button beside OK that runs chana_ok's commit without the chana_cancel teardown.
- **Risk.** D4 is a recorded decision ("deterministic, no hidden multi-type writes") — reversing it is a user ruling, not a repair, and the multi-type-write hazard it was written against must be answered (the cache is per-dialog-lifetime and only ever feeds the form, never state, which is the answer). Adding a third button moves tests/headless/test_ase_dialogs.tcl's GE4 perturb-and-cancel rows and any row reading dialog_buttons' geometry.
- **ADE-L.** ADE-L's Choosing Analyses holds an independent form per analysis type; switching the type button changes which form is shown and preserves the others, and Apply commits without closing so you can set up several analyses in one visit.

## [MAJOR] The Analyses pane always shows all four types, empty or not, and cannot hold two of the same type

- **id** `analyses-pane-is-a-fixed-four-row-menu`  ·  **effort** medium
- **Evidence.** src/ase.tcl:512 seeds every new session with `analyses {{type op enabled 1} {type dc enabled 0} {type ac enabled 0} {type tran enabled 0}}`. The live bench file sky130A/xschem_libs/sky130_tests_ase/tb_bandgap/ngspice_state1/tb_bandgap.state carries `{type dc enabled 0} {type ac enabled 0}` with no fields at all. ase_main.png and ase_large.png show them as rows 2 and 3 with a blank Arguments cell forever. chana_ok, src/ase_window.tcl:4180-4190, searches for the FIRST row of the type and edits it, appending only when no row of that type exists — so the dialog can never create a second dc.
- **Why it hurts.** Two of the four rows in the pane are permanent noise on every bench that is not doing a dc sweep and an ac sweep, which is most of them, and at 1500x900 (ase_large.png) they are the only thing in the top half of the window besides the two real analyses. And a bench that legitimately wants two dc sweeps — VIN and temperature — cannot say so through any control in the program.
- **Fix.** Seed `analyses {}` and let Choose Analyses append. Give the pane an explicit row identity so chana_ok edits the row that was double-clicked rather than the first of its type (pane_dblclick already knows the index and throws it away at src/ase_window.tcl:1006-1012 — it passes only the type). X already deletes rows, so removal needs nothing new.
- **Risk.** tests/headless/test_ase_window.tcl:1369-1371 reads the `num` column of every ana row and W1p's seeded-row checks assume the four; test_ase_dialogs.tcl's chana rows assume first-row-of-type addressing. Changing the default seed changes what a brand-new session looks like — a user-visible default, so it needs a ruling. The 104 committed .state files keep their four rows and must still round-trip.
- **ADE-L.** ADE-L's Analyses pane starts empty and contains exactly the analyses you chose, in the order you chose them; two dc sweeps or two trans are ordinary rows. The four types live in the Choosing Analyses dialog, not in the pane.

## [MAJOR] The run directory is a state key with no control anywhere in the GUI

- **id** `no-run-directory-control`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** `rundir` is read at src/ase_window.tcl:3374 and src/ase.tcl:6452 and defaulted by ase::rundir, and `grep -rn 'rundir' src/*.tcl | grep 'dict set'` returns nothing — no writer exists in the tree. The live bench carries `rundir {}`. Setup's whole menu is Design… / Model Files… / Simulators… (src/ase_window.tcl:519-531); none of them mentions a directory. logwin.png shows the consequence: the raw lands in `/home/analog/.xschem/simulations/tb_bandgap_ase.raw`, the shared default.
- **Why it hurts.** Every bench in the process writes its raw into the same shared simulations directory, so a long tran on one bench and a quick op on another collide on filename, and there is no way to park a run's artifacts beside the design or on fast local scratch. Changing it means closing xschem and hand-editing a .state file — which is precisely the clutter-in-the-schematic problem ASE-L exists to remove, relocated.
- **Fix.** Add a Run Directory row to Setup > Design… (which already carries the session's identity and already has the OK/Cancel commit path), or a Setup > Simulator… dialog holding rundir beside the in-force simulator. Writer: `dict set st rundir <path>` + ase::session_update, which dirties the session like every other key.
- **Risk.** New dialog row and a new user-facing label — needs ratification. Setup > Design…'s widget paths are asserted in tests/headless/test_ase_dialogs.tcl; adding a row moves its grid rows. A rundir that does not exist must be created or refused at commit, not at run time.
- **ADE-L.** Setup > Simulator/Directory/Host is the first dialog an ADE-L user opens: it sets the simulator, the project directory that all results go under, and the host mode. The project directory is a first-class, always-editable setting.

## [MAJOR] Model Files and Simulation Options look read-only: a table and a Close button, with Add/Edit/Delete hidden on right-click and no double-click

- **id** `listdlg-shows-no-verbs`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** modelfiles_main.png and simoptions_main.png: a two-column table and one button, `Close`. listdlg_open, src/ase_window.tcl:5295-5312, packs only `$w.btns.close` and puts Add…/Edit…/Delete in `$w.ctx`, bound to `<Button-3>` and `<Delete>` only — there is no `<Double-1>` binding, unlike the main panes, which do bind it (build_pane, :871). Contrast simulators.png, the dialog next to it in the same Setup menu, which shows Add… / Edit… / Remove / Close as real buttons.
- **Why it hurts.** Switching corner tt -> ss is the single most common setup gesture in a PDK flow, and the dialog that owns it presents as a viewer. A designer opens Setup > Model Files, sees a table with a Close button, closes it and goes looking for the corner somewhere else. The inconsistency is inside one menu: Simulators… shows its verbs, Model Files… hides them.
- **Fix.** Give listdlg_open the same button row Simulators… has — Add… / Edit… / Delete on the left, Close on the right, driving the procs the context menu already drives — and add `bind $w.tv <Double-1>` to listdlg_edit_first, matching the main panes. Keep the context menu.
- **Risk.** tests/headless/test_ase_dialogs.tcl drives these dialogs by widget path — new `$w.btns.*` paths are additive, but any row asserting the exact child list of `$w.btns` moves. The W1p "no inline add/del buttons" rule is scoped to `$top.body` and does not reach these toplevels.
- **ADE-L.** ADE-L's Setup > Model Libraries is an editable table with a visible field row and explicit Add / Delete / Change buttons (and Up/Down for precedence); nothing about editing it is hidden behind a right-click.

## [MAJOR] The Calculator opens and every operator, function, stack key, Eval, Plot and Table is a stub — and no output editor reaches it

- **id** `calculator-is-a-shell`  ·  **effort** large
- **Evidence.** src/calculator.tcl:607 `proc calc::inert {what phase} { return [calc::status "$what: not implemented (phase $phase)"] }`, with 13 wiring sites: operators (:2126), functions (:2027), user buttons (:2112), stack (:1571), buffer toolbar (:1533), selectors (:1385), pick scope (:1424), Clip (:1435), Plot (:1448), Eval (:1176), plot destination (:1487), Table (:1479). ase::ui::output_editor, src/ase_window.tcl:1801-1840, has exactly one helper button, `From Design…` (:1830) — no Calculator seam; outputedit.png confirms.
- **Why it hurts.** In ADE-L the Calculator is where an output expression is built — bandwidth, gain, a cross-measure, a ratio of two nets — and then sent into the Outputs pane. In ASE-L an output expression must be typed correctly by hand into a bare entry with no function list, no signal picker and no evaluation, or clicked off the schematic as a plain v(net). So the Outputs pane can only hold raw signals and whatever ngspice expression syntax the designer happens to remember.
- **Fix.** Out of scope for one change, but the seam is not: add a `Calculator…` button beside `From Design…` in output_editor that opens calc::open and, on the Calculator's send gesture, writes the buffer text into the Expression entry. That is worth building before the operator keys are, because it is the direction ADE-L users actually use (build there, land here).
- **Risk.** The Calculator's phases are a planned build (doc/claude/specs/calculator.md); this is a schedule observation, not a defect to patch around it. The seam alone is small and touches output_editor's grid — tests/headless/test_ase_dialogs.tcl asserts that dialog's widget paths, and R101 (one Calculator per process, no session key) must not be broken by passing $key to calc::open.
- **ADE-L.** ADE-L's Setting Outputs form has `Calculator: Open / Get Expression` beside the expression field, and the Calculator itself is a working RPN/algebraic evaluator with the full analog function set (bandwidth, gain_margin, cross, deriv, integ, ymax, settlingTime) and Plot/Print/Eval destinations.

## [MAJOR] The three panes cannot be re-proportioned; at 1500x900 the window is mostly white

- **id** `no-pane-sashes`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** src/ase_window.tcl:812-823: `frame $top.body` with `grid` and fixed weights (column 0 weight 1, column 1 weight 2, rows 1 and 1) — no sash of any kind. The only ttk::panedwindow in the file is at :6116, inside Load State's three-column browser (loadstate.png). ase_large.png at 1500x900: five variables, four analyses and five outputs floating in three near-empty tables. ase_small.png at 560x360: `VCCGAUSS` clipped to `VCCGAUS`, `Type`/`Enable` headers clipped to `Typ`/`Ena`, `Plot`/`Save`/`Save Options` to `Plc`/`Sa`/`Save Op`.
- **Why it hurts.** A bandgap bench has three variables and twenty outputs; a corner-sweep bench has twenty variables and two outputs. Neither can be given its space. The window is unusable below ~700 px wide and pointless above ~1000, so it occupies a fixed slot on the desktop instead of adapting to the work — and this is a window that sits open beside the schematic and the waveform viewer all day.
- **Fix.** Replace `frame $top.body` + grid with a horizontal ttk::panedwindow (left = Design Variables, right = a vertical ttk::panedwindow of Analyses over Outputs). Classic `panedwindow` if the sash needs to be styled to the locked palette — calculator.tcl:58 records why it chose the classic widget. Persist sash positions per session the way calculator.tcl:151 already does.
- **Risk.** Widget paths `$top.body.vars` / `.ana` / `.outs` must be preserved exactly — tests/headless/test_ase_window.tcl:1354-1357 (W1p exactly-three-panes), :551 (pane title accent colour on `$top.body.vars`) and ~15 rows across test_ase_interact.tcl / test_ase_plot.tcl address them by path. A panedwindow keeps them as children, so paths survive; managing them with `add` instead of `grid` will move any row reading `grid info`.
- **ADE-L.** ADE-L's main window is splitter-separated: the Design/Variables column, the Analyses pane and the Outputs pane all have draggable boundaries, and the layout is remembered.

## [MINOR] Session has no Delete State and no Reset, and Save State carries no description

- **id** `session-menu-thin`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** src/ase_window.tcl:503-515: Session is exactly Design Window | Load State | Save State | separator | Close. savestate.png is a bare Library dropdown + Cell + View entries + OK/Cancel — no description field, no per-category tick list. `grep -rn -i 'delete state' src/ase*.tcl` returns nothing.
- **Why it hurts.** State views accumulate — ngspice_state1, debug_st1, and whatever the last three experiments were called (loadstate.png shows two already on this one cell) — and there is no way to remove one from inside the tool or to record what any of them was for. Six months later the View column is a list of names nobody can decode, and the only remedy is a file manager.
- **Fix.** Add Session > Delete State (the Load State browser in a delete flavour, with a confirm), and a Description entry in Save State written to a `description` state key that Load State shows under its three columns. The state loader already preserves unknown keys round-trip (src/ase.tcl:525-527), so the key costs no schema change.
- **Risk.** New menu labels and a new dialog sentence — the Session labels are v2-spec-fixed and asserted by tests/headless/test_ase_window.tcl (W1m), so a fourth entry moves that row and needs a ruling on the label. A delete path that removes a directory needs the confirm to be unmissable.
- **ADE-L.** ADE-L's Session menu carries Save State / Load State / Delete State, its Save State form has a Description field and a per-category tick list (analyses, variables, outputs, model files, simulator options…) plus a Cellview-or-Directory destination, and Load State shows the description beside each state name.

## [POLISH] Save All ships a disabled 'Levels:' field

- **id** `save-all-levels-is-dead`  ·  **effort** trivial
- **Evidence.** src/ase_window.tcl:5719 builds it, :5734 kills it: `$le configure -state disabled ;# after theming: inert v1 field`. saveall.png shows the greyed entry under the three live checkbuttons.
- **Why it hurts.** A control on screen that cannot be used is a small promise broken every time the dialog opens, and this one sits directly under three controls that do work, so it reads as "broken" rather than "not yet".
- **Fix.** Either implement it (ngspice has no hierarchy-depth save, so the honest mapping is a subcircuit-depth filter applied to the generated .save set) or remove the row until it does something. Removing is the smaller honest change.
