- **Risk.** tests/headless/test_ase_window.tcl W1z-series and test_ase_dialogs.tcl address `$w.levels` by path and assert its disabled state; removing it moves those rows and changes dialog_buttons' grid row. Deleting a visible field is user-visible — flag it rather than doing it silently.
- **ADE-L.** ADE-L's Save Options form has a real Save Levels / subcircuit-probe-level control that limits how deep the saved set goes — the single most useful knob on that form for a large hierarchical bench.

## [POLISH] The Analyses pane numbers its rows and the Outputs pane does not

- **id** `outputs-pane-has-no-row-numbers`  ·  **effort** trivial
- **Evidence.** src/ase_window.tcl:828-836: build_pane for `ana` passes columns `{num type enable args}` with heading `#`; build_pane for `outs` passes `{name value plot save saveopts}` with no number column. Visible side by side in ase_main.png and ase_large.png.
- **Why it hurts.** Trivially, but it is the kind of asymmetry the eye reads as unfinished when two tables sit one above the other in the same window, and it costs you the ability to say "delete output 4" to a colleague.
- **Fix.** Add a `num` column to the outs pane, width 30, filled with `[expr {$i + 1}]` in populate's outputs loop exactly as the analyses loop does at :1568.
- **Risk.** tests/headless/test_ase_window.tcl:1366 `W1p outputs columns` pins the column list byte for byte, and every `$top.body.outs.tv set <it> <col>` read in the suites is by column NAME so those survive; the column-list row moves.
- **ADE-L.** ADE-L numbers both the Analyses rows and the Outputs rows.


---

# critique  (10 findings)

**Headline.** Three of the surfaces in the screenshot folder were never opened by any of the eleven — the shipped dark colour scheme (which renders the temperature field at 1.00:1, literally invisible), the first-run empty session, and the two-windows case — and underneath all of them sits the defect none of the lenses is shaped to see: ASE-L's "state", the thing its status bar, its two Save/Load menu entries and its whole ADE-L premise are built on, does not exist on disk, so two states of one cell share one deck, one raw and one log, and Save State will silently delete a sibling bench.

**The one slick move.** Make the state a real thing, by deriving the run directory from lib/cell/view instead of from the cell alone — one change inside `ase::rundir` (src/ase.tcl:4812), whose empty-`rundir` arm today returns the shared `[set_netlist_dir 0]` for every session in the program.

Every one of the eleven answered "slick" with appearance or controls: fonts, hover states, icons, accelerators, a status surface, `wm transient`. All worth doing. But none of them asked whether the window is telling the truth, and measured, it is not: I opened `ngspice_state1` and `debug_st1` side by side and their deck, raw and log paths are byte-identical (`DECK identical = 1  RAW identical = 1  LOG identical = 1`). That single fact is the root of a surprising amount of what the others found downstream. The Outputs Value column cannot be filled from disk *correctly* until it exists — the task-flow lens's slick move would happily paint the corner run's numbers into the nominal window. `Results > Select` shows the same file twice in two boxes (rselect.png) because a cell can only ever have one raw. `ase::has_results` — the one named staleness predicate — answers per cell, so it says "current" in both windows at once. And I measured `>` on a brand-new, never-netlisted, zero-output session actually launching ngspice on a deck a different session had left behind.

Fixing it costs no user-facing sentence, no palette decision, no widget path, and no test that asserts a widget shape. It is a path function. What it buys is the thing that makes a bench feel like a bench rather than a form: you can keep nominal open next to a corner, run both, and each window keeps its own numbers, its own log, and its own answer to "are these results current?" That is what an analog designer does all day, and it is the one thing in this window that no amount of typography will fix.

It needs a user ruling, and I would not ship it without one: existing benches' raws live in `~/.xschem/simulations`, 26 files under `tests/headless` name those paths, and the waveform viewer's Recent history and the annotation path both point there. So the ruling is a migration question, not a design question — the design question is already answered by the status bar, which has been promising a per-state world since it was written.

## [CRITICAL] The shipped dark_gui_colorscheme renders every control, caption and menu at 1.12:1 and the temperature field at 1.00:1 — and the screenshot proving it was in the folder, uncited

- **id** `dark-scheme-unreadable-today`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** uxshots/ase_dark.png — CITED BY NONE OF THE ELEVEN. Visible: all eight strip glyphs are ghosts, the whole status line is a ghost, and the temperature entry appears EMPTY. It is not empty. Measured live on :99 by reproducing the shipped option block from src/xschem.tcl:19107-19120 (`option add *foreground white startupFile`) and building a real session window, then computing WCAG contrast from `winfo rgb`:
  strip.netrun / .run / .stop  Button  fg=white bg=#f2f2f2  = 1.12:1
  status.stat / .state / .sim  Label   fg=white bg=#f2f2f2  = 1.12:1
  .mb (whole menubar)          Menu    fg=white bg=#f2f2f2  = 1.12:1
  .tb.temp                     Entry   fg=white bg=#ffffff  = 1.00:1   <-- text '27' is present and mathematically invisible
Only two things survive: the labelframe titles (8.94:1, because apply_theme sets the accent explicitly at src/ase_window.tcl:231) and the treeview rows (#000000 on #ffffff, set through the Ase.Treeview ttk style, not the option database). Cause: apply_theme's classic arm (src/ase_window.tcl:227-233) sets -background and -font and never -foreground, so ASE-L forces the ground to near-white while the option database keeps the ink white. `dark_gui_colorscheme` is a documented user setting (src/xschemrc:294-296).
- **Why it hurts.** This is not a hypothetical. It is one uncommented line in the user's own xschemrc, it is documented as supported, and it turns the window an analog designer runs simulations from into three legible tables floating in a sea of invisible controls — including a live, editable temperature field whose value they can change and cannot see. The colour-contrast lens reported this as `[minor] A dark theme is BLOCKED by two missing lines`, tested with a hypothetical stub palette. That framing is wrong in the direction that matters: it is not a feature waiting to be built, it is a defect that ships today, and the severity is critical rather than minor.
- **Fix.** Add `-foreground [ase::theme fieldfg]` to apply_theme's classic arm (src/ase_window.tcl:227-233) and to the Entry (:234) and Text (:237) arms, so the locked palette — not the ambient option database — decides the ink. On the light scheme the rendered bytes are unchanged (fieldfg is #000000, which is what `option add *foreground black` already supplies), so this is a no-op on the default path and a repair on the dark one. Also set `-insertBackground [ase::theme fieldfg]` on the Entry arm, or the caret stays white on white. This is the same two lines the colour lens proposed; the correction is that they are not optional groundwork for a future theme.
- **Risk.** Very low. No widget path moves, no label moves, no palette value changes — it applies a colour the user already locked. Grep of tests/headless shows no ASE suite cgets a -foreground on a classic ASE widget, so no check moves. The one thing to verify is that the light-scheme render is byte-identical, which is directly assertable.

## [CRITICAL] Save State onto a different existing state overwrites it with no confirm, no existence check and no undo — while the near-identical dialog one menu away gives you pickers for the same three fields

- **id** `save-state-silently-destroys-a-sibling-bench`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** MEASURED live on the real bench (session ngspice_state1 open, sibling state debug_st1 present and writable):
  save_as_needs_confirm onto EXISTING OTHER state debug_st1 = 0
  save_as_needs_confirm onto OWN state ngspice_state1       = 0
  save_as_needs_confirm onto BRAND NEW state zz_new         = 0
  debug_st1 file writable = 1
The predicate is src/ase_window.tcl:6373-6386 and its own comment states the rule: `D13: overwriting a DIFFERENT existing view needs NO confirm in v1`. Its only trigger is read-only AND same-target. The dialog carries no warning widget — measured children are exactly `llib lib lcell cell lview view btns` (uxshots/savestate.png). ASE-L has no undo (confirmed by both the task-flow and keyboard lenses; `grep -in undo` returns comments only). The look-alike, measured side by side in one run:
  Setup > Design…  geom 334x120  lib=TCombobox  cell=TCombobox  view=TCombobox   View='schematic'
  Session > Save State geom 341x120  lib=TCombobox  cell=Entry  view=Entry        View='ngspice_state1'
Seven pixels apart in width, identical height, identical three-row Library/Cell/View shape, adjacent menus — and the SAFE one (picking an existing design) has three pickers while the DESTRUCTIVE one (writing a state file) has two free-text fields.
- **Why it hurts.** A designer who keeps a nominal state and a corner state — the entire reason ADE-L has states — is one mistyped or half-remembered word away from destroying a bench, with no prompt, no undo and no trace. The Load State browser sitting in the same menu is showing the exact list of names that are already taken; Save State does not consult it. And the affordance is backwards: the dialog that can only ever select gives pickers, the dialog that can destroy gives a blank entry. No lens looked at Save State's failure mode; the layout and widget-idiom lenses cited savestate.png only for its button placement.
- **Fix.** Two parts. (1) Extend `ase::ui::save_as_needs_confirm` (src/ase_window.tcl:6373) with a third arm: the target resolves to an existing file that is NOT the session's own path. The confirm machinery already exists and is already wired here — `ase::ui::confirm` at :6400 is called for the read-only case with a two-line message, so the plumbing costs nothing. (2) Make the View field a picker, not a blank: it is already a ttk::combobox in the sibling dialog, and `load_state_dialog` already knows how to enumerate the states of a lib/cell (src/ase_window.tcl:6218-6241). Populate `-values` from that enumeration and keep it editable so a new name is still typeable.
- **Risk.** The confirm's wording is a new user-facing sentence and must be ratified — record a rule debt with the proposed text rather than minting it. The predicate change may move a headless check: grep tests/headless for save_as_needs_confirm before editing, since D13 reads like a golded decision. Changing the View widget class from Entry to TCombobox would move any suite that cgets it or drives `$w.view insert`; test_ase_window.tcl drives the Save State path, so check it first.
- **ADE-L.** Cadence prompts before overwriting an existing cellview on a Save As, and ADE-L's Save State dialog names the target as a State Name chosen from the existing list rather than as a bare typed field.

## [CRITICAL] Two states of one cell share one deck, one raw and one log byte-identically — so the state axis the whole window is built on does not exist on disk, and a fresh session's Run button launched a deck a different session wrote

- **id** `one-cell-one-artifact-namespace`  ·  **effort** medium
- **Evidence.** MEASURED live with both states of the real bench open at once (.ase4 and .ase6):
  ngspice_state1  deck=/home/analog/.xschem/simulations/tb_bandgap_ase.spice  raw=…_ase.raw  log=…_ase.log
  debug_st1       deck=/home/analog/.xschem/simulations/tb_bandgap_ase.spice  raw=…_ase.raw  log=…_ase.log
  DECK identical = 1   RAW identical = 1   LOG identical = 1
By construction: src/ase.tcl:4835 `file join $rd ${cell}_ase.spice`, :11139 `${cell}_ase.log`, :11150 `${cell}_ase.raw` — every artifact is keyed on the CELL. `ase::rundir` (src/ase.tcl:4812) returns the shared `[set_netlist_dir 0]` whenever the state's `rundir` key is empty, and it is empty on the shipped bench (`rundir=''`) because the key has no GUI writer anywhere (the one `dict set … rundir` at src/ase_window.tcl:3376 builds a local input dict for results::select, not state).
CONSEQUENCE MEASURED, not argued: on a brand-new session (`ase::new_session` → 0 variables, 0 outputs, never netlisted, title 'Analog Sim Environment tb_bandgap (unsaved)'), pressing `>` moved the status bar to 'Status: Running' and echoed 'This run is starting the simulator you named ngspice-v50'. `ase::run_existing` (src/ase.tcl:7048-7065) tests only `file isfile <rundir>/<cell>.spice` — existence, no owner, no mtime, no session identity. Also visible: uxshots/rselect.png shows the identical `tb_bandgap_ase.raw (tran)` row, same red bullet, in BOTH the 'Loaded' and 'Recent' boxes — a two-list history architecture that a cell-keyed raw can never populate.
- **Why it hurts.** The status bar segment says `State: ngspice_state1`, Session offers Load State and Save State, and the ADE-L premise is that a state is a named, keepable configuration. On disk none of that is true. Run nominal, then run the corner, and the nominal window's Value column, its Results > Select binding, its annotation and its `ase::has_results` verdict all now describe the corner — with nothing on screen saying so, and with `has_results` (mtime raw vs mtime deck) actively asserting 'current' in both windows because there is only one of each file. This is the failure mode issue 0838 was written about, reintroduced by the naming rather than by the timing. Not one of the eleven opened two windows.
- **Fix.** Change the empty-`rundir` arm of `ase::rundir` (src/ase.tcl:4812-4818) to derive a per-session directory from the state's identity rather than returning the shared netlist dir — `file join [set_netlist_dir 0] <lib> <cell> <view>`, created on demand exactly as the non-empty arm already does with `file mkdir`. Every artifact path already routes through this one proc precisely so it cannot drift (the comment at :4822 says so), so deck, raw, log and the run lock all follow from the single edit. Then give `rundir` its first GUI writer — a Run Directory row in Setup > Design…, which already owns the session's identity and already has an OK/Cancel commit path — so the derived default can be overridden per bench.
- **Risk.** Needs a user ruling: it moves where result files land, and existing benches' raws are in the flat `~/.xschem/simulations`. 26 files under tests/headless name `_ase.raw` / `_ase.spice` / `_ase.log` or `simulations` and would need their expected paths updated; test_ase_core.tcl's byte-exact golden deck is a path-sensitive one to check first. The waveform viewer's Recent history (wviewer::rawhist_get) and the annotation path both point at the old location, so a migration or a fallback read is part of the change, not an afterthought.
- **ADE-L.** ADE-L exposes the project/run directory as a first-class control in Setup > Simulator/Directory/Host, and writes results under <proj>/<cell>/<simulator>/<view>/, so two configurations of one cell do not collide. It also has Results > Save to name and keep a result set. ASE-L has neither the control nor the separation.

## [MAJOR] A new user's first ASE-L window is two empty white tables with no guidance, whose only door is a right-click with no affordance and an unlabelled ASCII glyph — and the menubar has no Add Output entry at all

- **id** `first-run-is-two-blank-boxes`  ·  **effort** medium
- **Evidence.** uxshots/ase_empty.png — CITED BY NONE OF THE ELEVEN. Design Variables and Outputs are large blank white rectangles with column headers and nothing else. Measured on a true fresh session (`ase::new_session` + `ase::ui::open`):
  title = 'Analog Sim Environment tb_bandgap (unsaved)'
  status = '4' 'Status: Ready' 'T=27 C' 'Simulator: ngspice-v50' 'State: (unsaved)'
  pane vars rows=0  children = .tv .sb .ctx      <-- no empty-state widget of any kind
  pane outs rows=0  children = .tv .sb .ctx
  pane ana  rows=4  (the seeded op/dc/ac/tran)
The context menu IS the only door and it is invisible: instance bindings are `<Button-3> <Double-Button-1> <Button-1> <<TreeviewSelect>>` and nothing draws a hint. Worse, on an EMPTY pane its three entries are all `state=normal` — measured `[0] 'Add…' normal  [1] 'Edit…' normal  [2] 'Delete' normal` — so two of the three cannot possibly do anything, and pressing them echoes 'ase: nothing selected' to a CIW that is not on the desktop. Full live menubar walk confirms no Add Output door: `Outputs -> To Be Saved | To Be Plotted | Save All…`. Separately: `ase::open_state` on a state name that does not exist returns 0, opens no window and shows nothing on screen at all (measured: `returned '0'  wins=`).
- **Why it hurts.** This is the first ninety seconds of the product. The two panes a designer must fill before anything can happen are blank, silent, and reachable only through a gesture with no visual affordance, next to eight glyphs the source itself calls placeholders. The status bar meanwhile reads `Status: Ready` and `State: (unsaved)` — a parenthesised non-name presented as a state. The menus-ia lens found the missing Outputs door and the action-strip lens found the mute glyphs, but both reasoned from a loaded bench; neither asked what the window looks like with nothing in it, which is the only view a new user gets.
- **Fix.** Three small pieces, in increasing cost. (a) Grey what cannot work: give each pane's context menu a `-postcommand` that disables Edit… and Delete when `[$tv selection]` is empty — the mechanism is already in the file at src/ase_window.tcl:648-690 for the Annotate submenu. (b) Add the missing menubar doors so the verbs are discoverable without a right-click: `Outputs > Setup…` wired to `ase::ui::output_editor $key -1` and `Variables > Add…` wired to `ase::ui::add_variable_dialog` — both procs already exist and are already driven by the strip. (c) An empty-pane hint: Tk 8.6's ttk::treeview cannot host a placeholder row cleanly, but a `label` gridded into the labelframe behind the treeview and raised/lowered on row count is a dozen lines. The hint TEXT is a new user-facing sentence and must be ratified — record a rule debt with the proposed wording rather than minting it.
- **Risk.** (a) and (b) move nothing: no widget path changes, and the menubar additions are new entries, which will move any suite that asserts the Outputs/Variables menu shape — test_ase_window.tcl:571 and its neighbours assert menu entry lists, so those rows move by construction and should be updated in the same commit. (c) needs the ruling above before it can ship.
- **ADE-L.** ADE-L's Outputs pane is also empty on a fresh session, but every verb has a menubar door (Outputs > Setup…, Variables > Edit…) and a labelled toolbar, so the emptiness is not also a dead end.

## [MAJOR] 'View' is the schematic view in Setup > Design… and the state name in Save State — two dialogs seven pixels apart in width, adjacent menus, same three labels, different meanings, and the app's own sentence uses both words at once

- **id** `view-means-two-different-things`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** Measured live, both dialogs opened in one session:
  Setup > Design…     geom 334x120  Library:/Cell:/View:  View value = 'schematic'
  Session > Save State geom 341x120  Library:/Cell:/View:  View value = 'ngspice_state1'
Visible in uxshots/designdlg.png and uxshots/savestate.png — near-identical three-row forms. Meanwhile the same concept is spelled three more ways elsewhere in the same window:
  status bar (src/ase_window.tcl:6754)  `State: $view`
  Load State browser column (:6132)      `View`
  Load State's own status sentence (:6222) `"$lib/$cell — choose a state View"`
  and (:6203) `pick a Library / Cell / simulation-state View`
uxshots/loadstate.png shows that last line rendered: `sky130_tests_ase/tb_bandgap — choose a state View`, which puts lowercase 'state' and capitalised 'View' in one phrase for one thing. Affordance is inverted between the two look-alikes as well: Design's three fields are all TCombobox; Save State's are TCombobox + Entry + Entry (measured classes).
- **Why it hurts.** A designer reads `View: schematic` in one dialog and `View: ngspice_state1` in another that looks the same and sits one menu away, and has to learn by experiment which 'View' each means. The status bar then calls the second one 'State', the browser column calls it 'View', and the browser's explanatory line calls it 'a state View'. This is the vocabulary axis, and none of the eleven lenses is shaped to see it: typography measured the fonts of these strings, layout measured their geometry, menus-ia measured the tree they hang from, but nobody read what the words mean across surfaces.
- **Fix.** Pick one word for the simulation state and use it in all five places. Given the menu entries are already 'Load State' and 'Save State' and the status segment is already 'State:', the cheap coherent answer is: Save State's third field becomes `State:`, the Load State browser's third column heading becomes `State`, and its two status sentences drop the word 'View'. Setup > Design… keeps `View:` because there it correctly means the cellview. All five strings are user-facing, so this is a ruling, not an edit — record a rule debt with the proposed set. Separately and independently of the ruling, make Save State's View/State field a ttk::combobox populated from the existing-state enumeration load_state_dialog already performs (see save-state-silently-destroys-a-sibling-bench), which fixes the inverted affordance.
- **Risk.** Every one of the five strings is a user-facing sentence or label and must be ratified before it moves. Label changes will move headless rows: grep tests/headless for the literal `View` heading and for the two Load State status sentences — test_ase_window.tcl and the load-state suite assert dialog text. No widget path moves.
- **ADE-L.** Cadence keeps the two ideas separate by name: a cellview is lib/cell/view, and a saved ADE state is a State Name saved via Session > Save State with its own category list. ADE-L never calls a state a View.

## [MAJOR] ASE-L never sets, saves or restores a window geometry — the string does not occur once in either file — so it opens at the packer's natural 798x502 every time, which is a size at which a design variable's value is already clipped

- **id** `window-never-remembers-its-size`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** `grep -c geometry src/ase_window.tcl src/ase.tcl` returns 0 and 0 — not one occurrence of the word in 19,236 lines, confirmed. So there is no default `wm geometry`, no save, no restore, and no sash to persist either (the body is a plain `frame` with fixed grid weights, src/ase_window.tcl:333-337). Measured natural size on open: 798x502. At that size, per the prompt's own measured facts and visible in uxshots/ase_main.png, the Design Variables value `agauss(1.8, 'ABSVAR', 1)` renders as `agauss(1.8, 'A` — 204px of content in a 120px column. In the same tree, the siblings do persist: src/calculator.tcl:2496 `catch {set geom [wm geometry .calc]}` and :2511 `if {$geom ne {}} {catch {wm geometry .calc $geom}}`; src/library_manager.tcl:77 `wm geometry $w 760x460` and :806 `wm geometry $w 660x480`.
- **Why it hurts.** The window opens clipped, the user resizes it to read their own variable, and the next session opens clipped again — every time, forever. In one application the Calculator remembers how you sized it and the window you run simulations from does not. The layout lens measured the natural size, the missing minsize and the absent sash, and mentioned 'set a default wm geometry too' as a clause inside its minsize fix; it never made persistence the finding and never noticed that a sibling window three files over already does it. This is the difference between a tool you configure once and a tool you re-configure daily.
- **Fix.** Copy the Calculator's shape verbatim, since it is already shipped and already tested. On close (`ase::ui::close`, src/ase_window.tcl:339) capture `catch {set geom [wm geometry $top]}` into a persisted variable; on `ase::ui::open` (:276) apply it with `catch {wm geometry $top $geom}` after the build. Store it beside the other per-window preferences rather than in the state file — window size is not part of the bench, and putting it in the state would dirty the session on a resize. Set a sane default geometry for the very first open, derived from the summed column widths plus the strip, rather than leaving it to whatever the packer happens to total.
- **Risk.** Low, but two real hazards. Restoring a saved geometry interacts with the absent `wm minsize` (measured 1 1 by the layout lens) — restore a 120x100 window once and the panes come back unmapped, so the minsize floor should land in the same change. And any headless suite that asserts the natural 798x502 will move; grep tests/headless for that literal before editing.
- **ADE-L.** ADE-L restores its window size and pane proportions per session, so a designer sets the split once.

## [MAJOR] One press of Netlist and Run emits three notices totalling 757 characters, one of them a 450-character, 87-word second-person paragraph — which makes the feedback lens's top-ranked fix actively dangerous

- **id** `three-notices-757-chars-per-run`  ·  **effort** medium
- **Evidence.** MEASURED by wrapping `ase::echo` and pressing the button once on the real bench:
  MSG 1  tag=<>     chars=166  words=31  'ASE: 468 device OP save card(s) prepared from this schematic, covering 78 device(s)…'
  MSG 2  tag=<note> chars=450  words=87  'Your simulator can print out every device's operating-point numbers in one go, so this run asked for the whole set at once instead of making a separate request for each number. That is the shortest way there is: the deck names no device at all, and the numbers come back in a small file of their own beside the results. It covers more of your devices than asking one at a time does, not fewer. If that file does not appear, this run will tell you so.'
  MSG 3  tag=<note> chars=141  words=17  'This run is starting the simulator you named ngspice-v50, and the program it is running is …'
  === ONE press of 'Netlist and Run' emitted 3 messages ===
Opening a session emits one more. The longest single return in `ase::sim_why` measures 669 characters (src/ase.tcl:1235; also 553 at :1232, 540 at :1198, 471 at :1229, 451 at :1178). The house rule is terse copy with acronyms in uppercase; MSG 2 is conversational, second-person, explains an internal implementation choice nobody asked about, and fires on every run.
- **Why it hurts.** Two costs. First, on every single run a designer is handed three paragraphs about how the deck asked for OP data — noise that trains them to stop reading the channel, which is exactly where the real refusals also go. Second and sharper: the feedback lens's slick_move is to route all 107 echo sentences into a new ASE-L status surface modelled on RDW's. Route MSG 2 into a status line and you have put an 87-word paragraph under the panes on every run; RDW's surface auto-fits 1 to 4 display lines and never truncates, so it would consume the window. That lens counted the sentences and measured their lengths but treated length as a delivery problem; it is a copy problem, and it has to be fixed BEFORE the delivery fix, not after.
- **Fix.** Two separable passes. (a) Volume: the run path should emit one line per run by default, not three. MSG 1 and MSG 2 describe the same decision — one states the count, the other explains the mechanism — and belong in the log file's header (`ase::run_log_write`, src/ase.tcl:7473, already writes framing there) rather than in the notice channel. (b) Length: bring the `ase::sim_why` returns to the house style. Every one of these is a user-facing sentence, so this is a ratification exercise, not an edit — record a rule debt carrying the current text and a proposed terse replacement for the worst offenders (src/ase.tcl:1178, :1198, :1229, :1232, :1235 and the 450-char OP paragraph), and let the user rule. Do not silently rewrite them.
- **Risk.** Every string is user-facing and unratified changes are forbidden — this cannot ship without the user's ruling. Moving MSG 1/MSG 2 out of the echo channel will move any headless suite that asserts them; grep tests/headless for 'save card' and for the sim_why line numbers, since the OP-cards behaviour has its own suite history.
- **ADE-L.** ADE-L logs run detail to its output log and keeps the CIW to short one-line notices, so the channel a designer watches stays scannable.

## [MAJOR] The product spells its own name four ways — 152 sentences say 'ase:', 10 say 'ASE:', the title bar says 'Analog Sim Environment', and the menus and docs say ASE-L — against a house rule that acronyms are uppercase

- **id** `four-names-for-itself`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** Counted across the two files that own every user-facing sentence:
  lowercase '"ase: '  : 152
  uppercase '"ASE: '  : 10
  no prefix           : 0
Samples, all reaching the same sink: `"ASE: Save All was closed without OK …"`, `"ASE: device operating-point parameters (gm, gds,…"` against `"ase: state file not found: $path"`, `"ase: malformed state file (not a Tcl list): $path"`. Measured live in one run, adjacent in the same message stream: `ASE: 468 device OP save card(s) prepared…` followed two lines later by `ase: simulation finished (exit 1), log: …`. Fourth spelling: `wm title` is `Analog Sim Environment tb_bandgap` (src/ase_window.tcl:6728, visible in uxshots/ase_root.png and three_windows.png). Fifth surface for contrast: the spec, the issues and the menus all call it ASE-L.
- **Why it hurts.** The user's own house style says acronyms are spelled in uppercase, and 152 of 162 prefixed sentences break it. Worse than the style violation is that a single scroll of the message channel shows the tool referring to itself by two different names, and the window title uses a fourth expansion that appears nowhere else in the product, the docs or the menus — so a designer cannot search their log for the tool's name and find their tool's output. The cross-window lens caught the title, ranked minor, inside a finding about title conventions across seven windows; nobody counted the prefixes or noticed the two spellings collide inside one run's output.
- **Fix.** Pick one spelling and apply it mechanically. `ASE-L:` matches the menus, the spec, the issue tracker and the house acronym rule, and is what the user calls it. That is 162 prefix rewrites in src/ase.tcl and src/ase_window.tcl — a sed-able pass, but every one is a user-facing string, so the SPELLING is a ruling: record a rule debt naming the three candidates (`ASE-L:`, `ASE:`, keep `ase:`) rather than choosing. Separately retitle the window `ASE-L <lib> <cell> <state>`, which is shorter than what ships now and reads correctly under the house style.
- **Risk.** Mechanical but wide. Any headless suite that greps a notice by its literal prefix will move — grep tests/headless for 'ase: ' before touching it; the ASE suites assert echo text in several places. The title change will move test_ase_window.tcl's title rows. Needs the user's ruling on which spelling wins before any of it.
- **ADE-L.** ADE-L uses one name for itself in its title bar, its menus and its CIW output.

## [MINOR] Three user-facing sentences explain a refusal with the bare noun 'fold', a word the product never defines and the user has no reason to know

- **id** `fold-jargon-in-user-copy`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** src/ase.tcl:1128, :1131, :1142 — verbatim: `"$path has not been tried yet, so fold is all that can be offered; press Detect to try it."`, `"$path was tried, but it did not say which spellings of a net name it can hand back, so fold is all that can be offered."`, `"xschem has no way to try $path, so fold is all that can be offered."`. The first is rendered in uxshots/simdlg_editor.png and is the largest block of text in that dialog. `fold` appears 199 times in src/ase.tcl as an internal term. The same dialog's combobox shows `preserve (not tried yet)` — a value with a parenthesised status baked into the option text — and a field labelled `-n:` whose control is a checkbox reading `--no-spiceinit`, i.e. a bare command-line flag used as a form label.
- **Why it hurts.** This dialog is where a designer registers the simulator they will use all day, and the sentence explaining why they cannot have the option they want is built on an undefined noun. 'Fold' is ngspice's case-folding behaviour; nothing on screen says so. A designer reads 'so fold is all that can be offered' and learns nothing about what to do next except press Detect. Two lenses cited simdlg_editor.png — for the bold weight of this prose and for the grey combobox behind it. Neither read what it says.
- **Fix.** Name the thing the sentence is about. 'fold' is the case-folding of net names, and the sentence already has a clause that explains the concept correctly in the second variant ('which spellings of a net name it can hand back') — so the vocabulary already exists in the file and just is not used consistently. Rewrite the three returns to use that phrasing, and give the combobox values a plain word plus a separate status rather than a parenthetical inside the option. All four strings are user-facing: record a rule debt carrying the current text and a proposed replacement, and let the user rule.
- **Risk.** User-facing sentences — cannot ship unratified. The three sim_why returns are likely asserted: grep tests/headless for 'fold is all that can be offered' before editing. The combobox value strings are read back by the simulators-dialog suite.

## [MINOR] The one always-visible editable field in the window has no label, no range check, and on a bad value silently snaps back while the reason is printed in a different window

- **id** `temperature-field-reverts-in-silence`  ·  **effort** small
- **Evidence.** src/ase_window.tcl:1622-1637 `ase::ui::temp_commit`: on `![string is double -strict $v]` it does `delete 0 end`, re-inserts the stored value, and `::ase::echo "ase: temperature must be numeric" error` — which goes to the CIW, measured by the feedback lens as absent from the live desktop (uxshots/ase_root.png holds three windows and no CIW). No range check at all: any double is accepted, so -500 or 1e9 goes straight into `.temp <T>` in the deck. The source itself flags the labelling at src/ase_window.tcl:745: 'This entry is the only widget in the window carrying no word of its own -- the `°C` label beside it gives the unit, not the subject.' Visible in uxshots/ase_main.png and ase_large.png as a bare `27 °C` adrift at the top-left of an otherwise empty 27px band.
- **Why it hurts.** Temperature is the single most-changed parameter on an analog bench and this is its only control. A designer types a value, tabs away, and the field silently shows the old number again — the classic 'did that take?' failure, with the explanation delivered to a window that is not on screen. And nothing stops a typo like 2700 from reaching the deck, where it will run and produce garbage rather than refuse. The layout lens measured this band's emptiness and the source's own comment about the missing label; nobody exercised the field.
- **Fix.** Three small changes, none of which moves a widget path. (1) Refuse in place rather than reverting in silence: leave the typed text, and surface the reason where the user is looking — this is the same in-dialog status surface the feedback lens proposes, and the pattern already exists in the file as `ase::ui::rsel_status` (src/ase_window.tcl:3502). (2) Add a range guard alongside the numeric guard so a value the simulator cannot use is refused at commit; the bound is a user-visible decision, so record a rule debt with a proposed range rather than picking one. (3) Give the field its subject: a `Temperature:` label before the entry, with the existing `°C` after — the words already exist as the ratified constant behind the balloon at src/ase_window.tcl:728 (`ase::ui::lbl_sim_temperature`), so no new sentence is minted.
- **Risk.** Adding a label into `$top.tb` changes that frame's children, which test_ase_window.tcl's W1 rows may enumerate — grep tests/headless for `tb.temp` and `tb.degc` first. The commit-behaviour change must not disturb the load-bearing bind order documented at src/ase_window.tcl:711-724 (the balloon must be armed before the two binds, and the FocusOut bind must stay `+`); W1s3 reads that composed script back and will red if it moves.
- **ADE-L.** ADE-L's temperature field is labelled, and its setup forms report a rejected value in the form itself rather than in the CIW.
